## Introduction

It is a classical result of Grothendieck [@sga1 §5, Théorème 4.1] that
finite étale schemes over a connected base scheme $S$ are nothing but
finite discrete sets with a continuous action of the étale fundamental
group $\pi_1^{\textnormal{ét}}(S)$, in symbols $$\begin{equation}
\label{eq:fetspione}
\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\simeq \pi_1^{\textnormal{ét}}(S)\textsf{\textup{-set}}.
\end{equation}$$ When $S$ is the spectrum of a field $\mathbb{F}$ we can
follow Voevodsky's approach to motives and construct an additive
category of *$\mathbb{F}$-correspondences*
$\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(\mathbb{F};\mathbb{Z})$ from the category of
smooth $\mathbb{F}$-schemes $\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_\mathbb{F}$. For a
commutative ring $R$, additive $R$-module valued presheaves on
$\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(\mathbb{F};\mathbb{Z})$ which are sheaves for
the Nisnevich topology are called *sheaves with transfers*. From the
category they comprise one constructs effective Voevodsky motives
$\mathcal{DM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}^{\textnormal{eff}}(\mathbb{F};R)$
through passage to the derived category followed by
$\mathbb{A}^1$-localisation, and there is an 'associated motive' functor
$$\mathcal{M}_\textnormal{Nis}: \; \textsf{\textup{Sm}}_\mathbb{F}\to \mathcal{DM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}^{\textnormal{eff}}(\mathbb{F};R).$$
Under this linearisation procedure *Artin motives*
$\mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\mathbb{F};R)$, defined as the
localising subcategory inside
$\mathcal{DM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}^{\textnormal{eff}}(\mathbb{F};R)$
generated by the motives associated to $0$-dimensional smooth
$\mathbb{F}$-schemes, are closely related to permutation representations
of $\textnormal{Gal}(\mathbb{F}^{\textnormal{sep}}/\mathbb{F})$, the
absolute Galois group of the field $\mathbb{F}$. This was originally
observed by Voevodsky [@voe00 §3.4] and has recently been revisited by
Balmer--Gallauer [@bg23 §7]. Namely, there is a symmetric monoidal
equivalence $$\begin{equation}
\label{eq:damaspermfield}
\mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\mathbb{F};R) \simeq \mathcal{D}\textsf{\textup{Perm}}(\textnormal{Gal}(\mathbb{F}^{\textnormal{sep}}/\mathbb{F});R),
\end{equation}$$ where the latter is the category of derived permutation
modules defined in [@bg22a]. Since the absolute Galois group
$\textnormal{Gal}(\mathbb{F}^{\textnormal{sep}}/\mathbb{F})$ is
precisely the étale fundamental group of the scheme
$\textnormal{Spec}(\mathbb{F})$, the equivalence
[\[eq:fetspione\]](#eq:fetspione){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:fetspione"} suggests a natural question:

::: center
*Is [\[eq:damaspermfield\]](#eq:damaspermfield){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:damaspermfield"} still true when we replace
$\textnormal{Spec}(\mathbb{F})$ by a general base scheme $S$\
and $\textnormal{Gal}(\mathbb{F}^{\textnormal{sep}}/\mathbb{F})$ by its
étale fundamental group?*
:::

We answer this question in the affirmative, under mild assumptions on
the base scheme. To this end, we define the $\infty$-category of
effective Voevodsky motives
$$\mathcal{DM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}^{\textnormal{eff}}(S;R) \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=L_{\mathbb{A}^1}\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
over a base scheme $S$ with coefficients in $R$ as the $\infty$-category
of $\mathbb{A}^1$-invariant Nisnevich sheaves with transfers with values
in spectra.[^1] For this, we employ the theory of finite correspondences
with coefficients [@cd19 §9]. Voevodsky motives are usually defined by
first deriving the $1$-category of Nisnevich sheaves with transfers. In
Appendix [6](#sec:sheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref"
reference="sec:sheaveswithtransfers"} we show that both of these
approaches are equivalent.\
We are then in a position to consider specific subcategories of motives,
and we define the $\infty$-category of *smooth Artin motives*
$$\mathcal{DAM}_\textnormal{Nis}(S;R) \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=\textnormal{Loc}(\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(X) \mid X \textnormal{ finite étale over } S)$$
as the localising subcategory of
$\mathcal{DM}_\textnormal{Nis}^{\textnormal{eff}}(S;R)$ generated by the
motives associated to finite étale $S$-schemes. When $S$ is the spectrum
of a field, this recovers the notion of Artin motives that was
considered by Voevodsky. Crucially relying on the equivalence
[\[eq:fetspione\]](#eq:fetspione){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:fetspione"}, we then show:

::: thm*
**Theorem 1** ([1](#thm:damnisasmodunderlr){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:damnisasmodunderlr"}). *Let $S$ be a connected,
noetherian scheme of finite Krull dimension. Then there is a symmetric
monoidal equivalence
$$\mathcal{DAM}_\textnormal{Nis}(S;R) \simeq \mathcal{D}\textsf{\textup{Perm}}(\pi_1^{\textnormal{ét}}(S,s);R),$$
where $s: \textnormal{Spec}(k) \to S$ is a geometric point of $S$.*
:::

The $\infty$-categories above are presentably symmetric monoidal stable
$\infty$-categories and hence their homotopy categories are 'large'
tt-categories. By recent work of Balmer--Gallauer [@bg25a; @bg25b], the
Balmer spectrum of the compact part of
$\mathcal{D}\textsf{\textup{Perm}}(G;R)$ (and hence of *geometric*
smooth Artin motives) is known for any profinite group $G$ when $R$ is a
field, and by work of Dubey--Gómez [@dg25] its set is known when $G$ is
finite and $R$ is Noetherian.\
In fact, in order to prove the above theorem, we work with an
equivariant description of the $\infty$-category
$\mathcal{D}\textsf{\textup{Perm}}(\pi_1^{\textnormal{ét}}(S,s);R)$,
which by Corollary 4.8 or Theorem 5.12 of [@fuh25] is given by
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$, modules
over the $R$-linear Bredon cohomology spectrum in equivariant spectra
for the profinite group $G=\pi_1^{\textnormal{ét}}(S,s)$. In the proof
we rely on its spectral Mackey functor description, see [@bcn25 Example
2.15] or [@fuh25 Corollary 3.18].\
So far we have used the Nisnevich topology for the motives we
considered, but replacing it by the étale topology comes with no
difficulty, yielding étale versions
$$\mathcal{DM}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{(\wedge),\textnormal{eff}}(S;R) \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=L_{\mathbb{A}^1}\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
inside which we can similarly define smooth Artin motives. As indicated
by the superscript $(\wedge)$, in the étale case it is crucial to
distinguish between sheaves and hypersheaves, since the étale topos of a
scheme is generally not hypercomplete. For this we again use sheaves
with transfers - versions of Artin motives for the étale topology but
without transfers have among others been considered by Ayoub--Zucker
[@az12] and Ruimy [@rui25a; @rui25b]. It is now an easy observation that
étale (hyper)sheafification on sheaves with transfers descends to
motives, and there is a second natural question:

::: center
*Do étale smooth Artin motives also admit descriptions in terms of\
representation theory and equivariant homotopy theory?*
:::

To answer this question, we will take a digression into equivariant
homotopy theory, which will take up the first two sections. Let us
outline our considerations.\
When $G$ is a finite group, a $G$-spectrum $X$ is *Borel complete* if
maps $Y \to X$ are null whenever $Y$ is nonequivariantly contractible,
i.e. $\textnormal{res}^G_1(Y) \simeq 0$, or equivalently
$Y \otimes \mathbb{D}(G) \simeq 0$, where $\mathbb{D}(G)$ is the
equivariant function dual of the orbit $\Sigma^{\infty}_+G/1$. This
already suggests that in this case the 'genuine' $G$-action on $X$
should come from a 'naive' one, which is made precise by the
identification of the Borel complete subcategory of
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$ as a functor category
$$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G_{\textnormal{Borel}} \simeq \textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
established by Mathew--Naumann--Noel in [@mnn17 §6.3]. In terms of
spectral Mackey functors [@bar17], we can think of Borel completion as
an evaluation at the orbit $G/1$. If one tries to generalise these
statements to profinite groups, one faces the difficulty that the
equivariant homotopy theory of such groups is already controlled by the
finite (discrete) $G$-sets, and the orbit $G/1$ is not among such.\
Instead, it is an easy observation that Borel complete equivariant
spectra are preserved by categorical fixed points for arbitrary normal
subgroups. Since $G$-spectra for profinite $G=\textnormal{lim}_i \, G_i$
are themselves a limit along categorical fixed points, we can define a
full subcategory
$$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G_{\textnormal{lwBorel}} \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=\textnormal{lim}_i \; \textnormal{Spec}^{G_i}_{\textnormal{Borel}} \subseteq \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G,$$
the objects of which we call *levelwise Borel complete*. We can view
objects of $\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$ as families of $G_i$-spectra
$(X_i)_{i \in I}$ which are compatible with respect to categorical fixed
points, i.e. $X_i^{N_{ij}} \simeq X_j$, where $N_{ij}$ is the kernel of
the canonical map $G_i \twoheadrightarrow G_j$. Such an object is
levelwise Borel complete if each $X_i$ is a Borel complete
$G_i$-spectrum.\
A second possible approach is motivated by the observation that in a
profinite group the trivial subgroup is the intersection of all open
normal subgroups. Instead of working with the dual $\mathbb{D}(G)$ of
the orbit $\Sigma^{\infty}_+G/1$ as one does in the finite group case,
one should thus consider the duals $\mathbb{D}(G/N_i)$ of all open
normal subgroups at the same time, where $N_i$ is the kernel of the
natural map $G \twoheadrightarrow G_i$. They assemble into a filtered
diagram in $\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$, and we denote
its colimit by
$A \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=\textnormal{colim}_i \, \mathbb{D}(G/N_i)$. It naturally identifies
with $\textnormal{coind}^G_1(\mathbb{S})$, the coinduction of the
nonequivariant sphere spectrum to $G$. The $\infty$-category of Borel
complete $G$-spectra is then defined as
$$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G_{\textnormal{Borel}} \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G_{A\textnormal{-cpl}},$$ that is, as complete
objects (in the sense of [@mnn17 §2.2]) with respect to the algebra
object $A$. This means that a $G$-spectrum is Borel complete if all maps
it receives from nonequivariantly contractible $G$-spectra are null, as
for finite groups.\
In
[1](#prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete"} we observe that
there is a right complete t-structure on
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$, and for any t-structure
we can make sense of the hypercomplete objects as the ones in the right
orthogonal of the acyclics. By an acyclic object we mean one for which
all t-structure homotopy objects vanish. In the main theorem of
[3](#sec:borelprofinite){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="sec:borelprofinite"} we then show that
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G_{\textnormal{Borel}}$ identifies with the full
subcategory on the hypercomplete objects with respect to this
t-structure.

::: thm*
**Theorem 2**
([1](#thm:borelandhyperlewelwise){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:borelandhyperlewelwise"}). *There is a symmetric monoidal
equivalence
$$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G_{\textnormal{Borel}} \simeq (\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G_{\textnormal{lwBorel}})^h.$$*
:::

In fact, we work with coefficients in any connective commutative
nonequivariant ring spectrum, and nonequivariant coefficients are not
less general than equivariant ones when dealing with Borel complete
categories, see [1](#cor:justrestriction){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:justrestriction"}.\
We then connect the above two versions of Borel complete $G$-spectra for
profinite groups $G=\lim_i \, G_i$ to $\infty$-categories of
representations and étale sheaves. The latter will further down the line
be the bridge to étale motives.

::: prop*
**Proposition 1**
([1](#prop:sheavesborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:sheavesborelcomplete"} and
[1](#prop:sheavesgrmodules){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:sheavesgrmodules"}). *There is a commutative diagram
with horizontal equivalences and fully faithful vertical functors
$$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}} & {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{\wedge}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)} & {\mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))} \\
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}} & {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)} & {\textnormal{colim}_i \; \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R)).}
    \arrow["\sim", from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[hook, from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow["\sim", from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[hook, from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow[hook, from=1-3, to=2-3]
    \arrow["\sim", from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["\sim", from=2-2, to=2-3]
\end{tikzcd}$$*
:::

Here $R_G$ is the inflation of a discrete commutative ring $R$ to
$G$-spectra, the sheaf categories consist of (hyper)sheaves of
$R$-modules in spectra defined on the site of finite discrete $G$-sets
with jointly surjective covering maps,
$\mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))$ denotes the derived
$\infty$-category of discrete $(G;R)$-modules, and
$\textnormal{colim}_i \; \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R))$ is
the filtered colimit of the derived categories
$\mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R))$ with transition maps induced
by restriction along the quotient maps $G_i \twoheadrightarrow G_j$,
taken in $\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}$. In fact, for the
left-hand square of this diagram the proposition holds for any
connective commutative ring spectrum $R$.\
By work of Clausen--Mathew [@cm21 §4.1], both rows agree if the virtual
cohomological dimension of $G$ is finite, in the case that $R$ is
discrete. Analogous statements can be made for general connective
$R \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$, see
[1](#prop:sheavesborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:sheavesborelcomplete"}.\
Furthermore, note that for the sheaf categories in the above diagram the
two notions *hypersheaf* and *hypercomplete sheaf* (the latter defined
with respect to the canonical t-structure on sheaves) agree. We will
show this in
[1](#prop:hypersheafhypercomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:hypersheafhypercomplete"} by reducing the case with
coefficients in a ring spectrum to the sheaves of spectra case.\
We finally come full circle and note that by Grothendieck's equivalence
[\[eq:fetspione\]](#eq:fetspione){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:fetspione"}, étale sheaves on finite étale $S$-schemes are
the same as sheaves on finite discrete $G$-sets when $G$ is the étale
fundamental group $\pi_1^{\textnormal{ét}}(S)$. It is then a crucial
(and well known, see e.g. [@cd16 Proposition 3.1.4]) observation that
étale sheaves on (finite) étale $S$-schemes already have transfers
([1](#thm:etaletransfers){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:etaletransfers"}):
$$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \simeq \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S;R).$$
Here $\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$ is the full subcategory of the
category of smooth schemes with finite correspondences
$\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R)$ spanned by the finite étale $S$-schemes.
We give an independent proof of this result that does not rely on the
identifications of hom-sets made in [@cd16]. It is equivariant in
spirit - morally speaking we can think of étale sheaves as Borel
complete $G$-spectra, since in the finite group case they are right Kan
extended from the free orbit. They hence have an overlying $G$-spectrum
which canonically admits transfers. For $R$ a discrete commutative ring
and $G=\pi_1^{\textnormal{ét}}(S)$ the full picture then looks as
follows:

::: thm*
**Theorem 3** ([1](#thm:bigdiagram){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:bigdiagram"}). *There is a commutative diagram of left
adjoints in which all horizontal functors are equivalences.
$$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\mathcal{D}\textsf{\textup{Perm}}(G;R)} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)} & {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})}  \\
    {\textnormal{colim}_i \, \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R))} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}} & {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})}  \\
    {\mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}} & {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} 
    \arrow["\sim", from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow["\sim", from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow["\textsf{\textup{a}}_{\textnormal{ét}}", from=1-3, to=2-3]
    \arrow["\sim", from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["L^h", from=2-1, to=3-1]
    \arrow["\sim", from=2-2, to=2-3]
    \arrow["L^h", from=2-2, to=3-2]
    \arrow["(-)^{\wedge}_\textnormal{ét}", from=2-3, to=3-3]
    \arrow["\sim", from=3-1, to=3-2]
    \arrow["\sim", from=3-2, to=3-3]
\end{tikzcd}$$*
:::

Each of the categories of sheaves with transfers on the right then maps
to the respective version of smooth Artin motives constructed as a
localising subcategory of effective motives. As stated above, this
functor is always an equivalence in the Nisnevich case
([1](#thm:damnisasmodunderlr){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:damnisasmodunderlr"}). In the hypercomplete étale case we
need to impose assumptions on base scheme and coefficients to obtain an
equivalence: If the ring $R$ has positive characteristic $n$ and the
residue characteristics of $S$ are prime to $n$, the categories of the
third row coincide with $\mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\wedge}(S;R)$,
as a special form of the rigidity theorem
([1](#cor:dametassheaves){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:dametassheaves"}). In the non-hypercomplete étale case an
analogous statement would require the computation of certain mapping
spaces in the corresponding category of effective motives, and we will
refrain from doing those.\
By work of Cisinski--Déglise [@cd16 §2] which generalises a theorem of
Voevodsky [@vsf00 §5, Theorem 4.1.12], all rows as well as the
respective categories of Artin motives and effective motives collapse to
a single row when we use coefficients in a $\mathbb{Q}$-algebra. We
summarise this in [1](#thm:bigdiagramright){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:bigdiagramright"}.\
**[Organisation of the paper.]{.upright}** The first two sections are
entirely devoted to equivariant homotopy theory. In
[2](#sec:borelfinite){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="sec:borelfinite"} we review the identification of the Borel
complete subcategory for finite groups as a functor category, as
established by Mathew--Naumann--Noel. We do this with coefficients and
then prove basic change of group results.
[3](#sec:borelprofinite){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="sec:borelprofinite"} then generalises this theory to
profinite groups, where we define the two differing notions alluded to
above. The main result of this section is that one is the
hypercompletion of the other. The latter is then used in
[4](#sec:sheavesandreps){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="sec:sheavesandreps"} to connect the Borel complete categories
of equivariant spectra for profinite groups to categories of étale
sheaves and representations. In
[5](#sec:motives){reference-type="ref+Label" reference="sec:motives"} we
dive into the theory of correspondences and motives, establish an
identification of smooth Artin motives for the Nisnevich topology, and
study the effect of étale sheafification in the equivariant and
representation theoretic setting. We end with Appendix
[6](#sec:sheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref"
reference="sec:sheaveswithtransfers"} by comparing our definitions of
motives with the more classical ones in the literature.\
**[Notations and conventions.]{.upright}** We freely use the language of
$\infty$-categories as developed by Lurie [@lur09; @lur17; @lur18]. We
sometimes speak of 'categories' instead of '$\infty$-categories' when it
is clear that the context is $\infty$-categorical. We write
$\textsf{\textup{Spc}}$ for the $\infty$-category of spaces/homotopy
types and $\textsf{\textup{Sp}}$ for the $\infty$-category of spectra.
The $G$-equivariant analogues are denoted $\textsf{\textup{Spc}}^G$ and
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$, the latter is what is sometimes referred to as
'genuine' $G$-spectra. We tacitly identify discrete rings and Mackey
functors with their Eilenberg-MacLane spectra when viewing them as
(equivariant) spectra. Mapping spaces in an $\infty$-category
$\mathcal{C}$ are denoted $\textnormal{Map}_{\mathcal{C}}$, and by
$\textnormal{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}$ we mean
$\pi_0 \textnormal{Map}_{\mathcal{C}}$, the hom-set in the homotopy
category $\textnormal{h}\mathcal{C}$.\

## Borel completeness for finite groups

This section mostly consists of recollections and immediate
generalisations of the results on Borel completeness in [@mnn17]. We
first recall some facts about module $\infty$-categories and equivariant
spectra for profinite groups that we will use throughout the paper and
then consider Borel completeness for modules over equivariant ring
spectra in the finite group case. At the end of the section we establish
change of group results for Borel complete subcategories.

::: {#rec:modules .rec}
**Recollection 1**. *If $\mathcal{C}$ is a presentably symmetric
monoidal[^2] stable $\infty$-category and
$A \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})$, then the $\infty$-category
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_A(\mathcal{C})$ of $A$-modules in $\mathcal{C}$
is presentably symmetric monoidal and stable [@lur17 Theorem 3.4.4.2].
It comes with a free-forgetful adjunction
$$(F_A,U_A): \mathcal{C}\rightleftarrows \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_A(\mathcal{C}),$$
the free functor $F_A$ is symmetric monoidal, and the composite $U_AF_A$
is the functor $(-) \otimes A: \mathcal{C}\to \mathcal{C}$ [@lur17
Corollary 4.2.3.7, 4.2.4.8, Theorem 4.5.2.1, §4.5.3]. If $\mathcal{C}$
is compactly generated by a set of objects $\mathcal{G}$, then
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_A(\mathcal{C})$ is compactly generated by the set
$F_A(\mathcal{G})$. If the objects of $\mathcal{G}$ are also dualisable,
then the objects of $F_A(\mathcal{G})$ are. If the unit of $\mathcal{C}$
is compact then $A$ is compact in
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_A(\mathcal{C})$, so in this case an object of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_A(\mathcal{C})$ is compact if and only if it is
dualisable, and $\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_A(\mathcal{C})$ is
rigidly-compactly generated by the set $F_A(\mathcal{G})$.\
A symmetric monoidal functor $\alpha: \mathcal{C}\to \mathcal{D}$
restricts to commutative algebra objects
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\mathcal{C}) \to \textnormal{CAlg}(\mathcal{D})$, and
for any $A \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})$ there is an induced
symmetric monoidal functor
$$\bar{\alpha}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_A(\mathcal{C}) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\alpha(A)}(\mathcal{D})$$
on module categories. If $\alpha$ preserves colimits then so does the
induced $\bar{\alpha}$ [@lur17 Corollary 4.2.3.5], and as explained in
[@erg22 Remark 1.1.11] both squares in the following diagram commute.
$$\begin{tikzcd}
    \mathcal{C}& \mathcal{D}\\
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_A(\mathcal{C})} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\alpha(A)}(\mathcal{D})}
    \arrow["\alpha", from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[""{name=0, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{F_A}"', shift right=2, from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow[""{name=1, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{F_{\alpha(A)}}"', shift right=2, from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow[""{name=2, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{U_A}"', shift right=2, from=2-1, to=1-1]
    \arrow["\bar{\alpha}", from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow[""{name=3, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{U_{\alpha(A)}}"', shift right=2, from=2-2, to=1-2]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center}, draw=none, from=0, to=2]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center}, draw=none, from=1, to=3]
\end{tikzcd}$$ If $\alpha$ preserves limits, then so does $\bar{\alpha}$
[@lur17 Corollary 4.2.3.3], and a right adjoint
$\beta: \mathcal{D}\to \mathcal{C}$ to $\alpha$ induces a right adjoint
$\bar{\beta}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\alpha(A)}(\mathcal{D}) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_A(\mathcal{C})$
to the functor
$\bar{\alpha}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_A(\mathcal{C}) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\alpha(A)}(\mathcal{D})$.
As a right adjoint to a symmetric monoidal functor the functor
$\beta: \mathcal{D}\to \mathcal{C}$ is lax symmetric monoidal, and when
$B \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\mathcal{D}^{\otimes})$, then we also have an
induced lax symmetric monoidal functor
$$\tilde{\beta}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_B(\mathcal{D}) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\beta(B)}(\mathcal{C}).$$
It factors as
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_B(\mathcal{D}) \stackrel{\varepsilon_B^*}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\alpha \beta(B)}(\mathcal{D}) \stackrel{\bar{\beta}}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\beta(B)}(\mathcal{C})$,
where $\varepsilon_B^*$ is the restriction of scalars along the counit
$\varepsilon_B: \alpha \beta(B) \to B$, and hence $\tilde{\beta}$ has a
symmetric monoidal left adjoint
$\tilde{\alpha}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\beta(B)}(\mathcal{C}) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_B(\mathcal{D})$
given by the composition
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\beta(B)}(\mathcal{C}) \stackrel{\bar{\alpha}}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\alpha \beta(B)}(\mathcal{D}) \stackrel{\varepsilon_{B,!}}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_B(\mathcal{D})$,
where $\varepsilon_{B,!}$ is the extension of scalars along
$\varepsilon_B: \alpha \beta(B) \to B$.*
:::

There are multiple models for the $\infty$-category of $G$-spectra
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$ for a profinite group $G$ which are all known
to be equivalent [@bbb24 §A]. Our preferred model in this paper will be
the one of spectral Mackey functors [@bar17; @bgs20], since in this
model the relation to the Borel complete subcategory is particularly
clear. We could hence take the functor category
$$\textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
as a definition for $\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$. Here
$\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)$ denotes the effective Burnside
$\infty$-category of finite continuous $G$-sets [@bar17 §3, Example B]
and the superscript $\times$ indicates that we are taking the full
subcategory of finite product preserving functors.[^3]

::: rec
**Recollection 1**. *The *$\infty$-category of $G$-spectra*
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$ for a profinite group $G$ is stable and
presentably symmetric monoidal. We denote the symmetric monoidal product
by $\otimes$ and the internal mapping $G$-spectrum by
$\underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(-,-)$. It
receives a symmetric monoidal left adjoint suspension functor
$\Sigma^{\infty}: \textsf{\textup{Spc}}^G_{\ast}\to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$
from pointed $G$-spaces. We write
$\Sigma^{\infty}_+: \textsf{\textup{Spc}}^G\to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$
for the composition $\Sigma^{\infty} \circ (-)_+$, where the functor
$(-)_+: \textsf{\textup{Spc}}^G\to \textsf{\textup{Spc}}^G_{\ast}$ adds
a disjoint base point with trivial action.\
The sphere spectrum $\Sigma^{\infty}_+ G/G$ is denoted
$\mathbb{S}_G = \mathbb{S}^0_G$. It is the unit of the symmetric
monoidal structure. The $\infty$-category $\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$ is
rigidly compactly generated by the suspension spectra of orbits
$\Sigma^{\infty}_+G/H$, for open subgroups $H \leq G$. It has a natural
t-structure for which the t-structure homotopy objects agree with the
homotopy Mackey functors
$\pi_n^{H}(-) = \textnormal{Hom}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(\Sigma^{\infty + n}_+ G/H,-)$.
This t-structure is both left and right complete, compatible with
filtered colimits, and it is compatible with the symmetric monoidal
structure on $\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$, in the sense that
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G_{\geq 0}$ is a symmetric monoidal subcategory.*
:::

::: rec
**Recollection 1**. *For any homomorphism of profinite groups (i.e.,
continuous group homomorphism) $\varphi: G \to G'$ there is a symmetric
monoidal left adjoint functor
$\varphi^*: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G'} \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$ with
lax symmetric monoidal right adjoint
$\varphi_*: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G} \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G'}$.\
If $\varphi$ is the inclusion of a closed subgroup
$H \hookrightarrow G$, the left adjoint
$\varphi^*: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G} \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{H}$ is
the *restriction functor* $\textnormal{res}^G_H$, and its right
adjoint[^4] is the *coinduction functor* $\textnormal{coind}^G_H$. If
$\varphi: G \twoheadrightarrow G/N$ is a quotient homomorphism for
$N \trianglelefteq G$ a closed normal subgroup then
$\varphi^*: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N} \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G}$ is
the *inflation functor* $\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}$, and its right
adjoint $\varphi_*$ is the *categorical fixed point functor*
$(-)^N: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G} \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N}$.*
:::

We explain what the above functors mean in terms of spectral Mackey
functors.

::: {#rem:changeofgroupspectralmackey .rem}
**Remark 1**. *For a closed subgroup $H\leq G$ the functor
$\overline{\textnormal{res}}^G_H: \textsf{\textup{Span}}(G) \to \textsf{\textup{Span}}(H)$
that restricts the $G$-action to an $H$-action induces the adjunction
$(\textnormal{res}^G_H,\textnormal{coind}^G_H)$ through restriction and
left Kan extension
$$((\overline{\textnormal{res}}^G_H)_!,(\overline{\textnormal{res}}^G_H)^*): \textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \rightleftarrows \textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(H)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).$$
When $H$ additionally is of finite index (i.e., open) the functor
$\overline{\textnormal{res}}^G_H$ has a left adjoint
$\overline{\textnormal{ind}}^G_H: \textsf{\textup{Span}}(H) \to \textsf{\textup{Span}}(G), \; X \mapsto (G \times X)/H,$
where $H$ acts by $h(g,x)=(gh^{-1},hx)$. For the induced adjunction
$$((\overline{\textnormal{ind}}^G_H)_!,(\overline{\textnormal{ind}}^G_H)^*): \textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(H)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \rightleftarrows \textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
we thus have
$(\overline{\textnormal{ind}}^G_H)^* \simeq (\overline{\textnormal{res}}^G_H)_! \simeq \textnormal{res}^G_H$
and its left adjoint $(\overline{\textnormal{ind}}^G_H)_!$ is usually
referred to as the *induction functor* $\textnormal{ind}^G_H$. It is
equivalent to $\textnormal{coind}^G_H$ via the Wirthmüller isomorphism,
see [@nar17 §A.3], [@cmnn24 §A].\
Analogously, when $N \trianglelefteq G$ is a closed normal subgroup
there is a functor on span categories
$\overline{\textnormal{infl}}^G_{G/N}: \textsf{\textup{Span}}(G/N) \to \textsf{\textup{Span}}(G), \; (G/N)/(H/N) \mapsto G/H$.
It induces
$$((\overline{\textnormal{infl}}^G_{G/N})_!,(\overline{\textnormal{infl}}^G_{G/N})^*): \textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G/N)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \rightleftarrows \textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Sp}}),$$
which recovers the adjunction $(\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N},(-)^N)$, see
[@bar17 Example B].*
:::

Let us now fix a finite group $G$ and a commutative equivariant ring
spectrum $R \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$. We will
return to the profinite case in the next section.

::: {#rec:coinddual .rec}
**Recollection 1**. *Since any finite $G$-set $T$ is a commutative
coalgebra in $\textsf{\textup{Spc}}^G$, the internal mapping spectrum
$\mathbb{D}(T) \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=\underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+T, \mathbb{S}_G)$
has the structure of a commutative algebra object in
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$. By [@bchnl25 Lemma 3.8] there is an
equivalence
$$\mathbb{D}(G/H) \otimes R \stackrel{\sim}{\longrightarrow} \textnormal{coind}^G_H\textnormal{res}^G_H(R)$$
in $\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ which is adjoint to the
map
$\textnormal{res}^G_H(\mathbb{D}(G/H) \otimes R )\to \textnormal{res}^G_H(R)$
that is obtained from the projection
$\textnormal{res}^G_H(\mathbb{D}(G/H)) \to \mathbb{S}_H$ by base change.
As $\mathbb{D}(G/H) \otimes R$ is the image of $\mathbb{D}(G/H)$ under
the free functor
$F_R: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G\to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$,
we can view $\mathbb{D}(G/H) \otimes R$ as a commutative algebra object
of $\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$.*
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *Since $\mathbb{D}(G/H) \otimes R$ is a dualisable
$R$-module with dual $\Sigma^{\infty}_+G/H \otimes R$ there is an
equivalence of $R$-modules
$\mathbb{D}(G/H) \otimes R \simeq \underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+G/H \otimes R, R)$,
and the underlying $G$-spectrum of this is
$\underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+G/H, R)$.*
:::

The definition for completeness with respect to an algebra object $A$ we
will use in this paper is the one of [@mnn17 §2.2]. When $A$ is
dualisable, [@mnn17 Proposition 3.11] shows that it agrees with the
perhaps more common one that defines complete objects as the twofold
right orthogonal of torsion objects (which are the objects in the
localising tensor ideal generated by $A$). The dualisability assumption
holds in the finite group case.

::: {#defi:borelcomplete .defi}
**Definition 1**. *An $R$-module
$X \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ is *Borel
complete* if it is complete with respect to the algebra object
$\mathbb{D}(G) \otimes R$. That is, for all
$Y \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ with
$Y \otimes_R (\mathbb{D}(G) \otimes R) \simeq 0$ the mapping space
$\textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(Y,X)$
is contractible. We write
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}} \subseteq \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$
for the full subcategory on the Borel complete $R$-modules.*
:::

::: {#rem:borelcompleteforget .rem}
**Remark 1**. *The case $R=\mathbb{S}_G$ is the now classical notion of
Borel completeness for $G$-spectra [@mnn17 §6.3]. By [@mnn17 §2.2] the
subcategory
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
is presentable and the inclusion
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}} \hookrightarrow \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$
has a left adjoint $L_{G;R}$ called *Borel completion*. There is a
unique symmetric monoidal structure on
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
that makes $L_{G;R}$ into a symmetric monoidal functor.*
:::

::: {#rem:tensorcondition .rem}
**Remark 1**. *Since the forgetful functor $U_R$ is conservative and the
free-forgetful adjunction satisfies a projection formula the condition
$Y \otimes_R (\mathbb{D}(G) \otimes R) \simeq 0$ is equivalent to
$U_R(Y) \otimes \mathbb{D}(G) \simeq 0$.*
:::

::: {#rem:completeunderlyingeasydirection .rem}
**Remark 1**. *If a module
$X \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ is Borel
complete, then the underlying $G$-spectrum
$U_R(X) \in \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$ is Borel complete. Indeed, assume
that $Y \in \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$ satisfies
$Y \otimes \mathbb{D}(G) \simeq 0$, then
$F_R(Y) \otimes_R (\mathbb{D}(G) \otimes R) \simeq F_R(Y \otimes \mathbb{D}(G)) \simeq 0$
and hence we have that
$\textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(Y,U_R(X)) \simeq \textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(F_R(Y),X) \simeq 0$.\
Similarly, when $X \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$
is Borel complete, $U_R(X) \in \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$ is complete with
respect to the algebra object
$\mathbb{D}(G) \otimes R \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$.
We will show the converse to the first statement in
[1](#cor:completionunderlying){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:completionunderlying"}.*
:::

As in the case without coefficients, we can give an explicit description
of the Borel completion as a functor
$L_{G;R}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}} \hookrightarrow \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$.

::: {#lem:completionexplicit .lem}
**Lemma 1**. *Let
$X \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$. The map
$X \to \underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+EG \otimes R, X)$
exhibits the target as the Borel completion of $X$.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* By [@mnn17 Proposition 2.21] the completion $L_{G;R}(X)$ is
computed as
$\textnormal{Tot}(X \otimes_R \textnormal{CB}^{\bullet}(\mathbb{D}(G) \otimes R))$,
the totalisation of the cobar construction of $\mathbb{D}(G) \otimes R$
tensored with $X$. We now note that there are equivalences
$$\begin{align*}
X \otimes_R (\mathbb{D}(G) \otimes R)^{\otimes_R \bullet} &\simeq X \otimes_R (\underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+G^{\bullet}, \mathbb{S}_G) \otimes R) \\
&\simeq X \otimes_R \underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+G^{\bullet} \otimes R, R) \\
&\simeq \underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+G^{\bullet} \otimes R, X).
\end{align*}$$ Totalising gives
$\underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+EG \otimes R, X)$,
using the standard simplicial decomposition of the free contractible
$G$-space $EG$ as in [@mnn17 Proposition 6.6]. ◻
:::

::: {#cor:completionunderlying .cor}
**Corollary 1**. *A module
$X \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ is Borel
complete if and only if the underlying $G$-spectrum $U_R(X)$ is.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* By [1](#lem:completionexplicit){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:completionexplicit"} $X$ is Borel complete if and only if
the completion map
$$X \to \underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+EG \otimes R, X)$$
is an equivalence. Since $U_R$ is conservative, this happens if and only
if the map
$$U_R(X) \to U_R(\underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+EG \otimes R, X)) \simeq \underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+EG, U_R(X))$$
is an equivalence. But this is precisely the completion map for
$U_R(X)$. ◻
:::

Recall from [@mnn17 Proposition 6.17] that the $\infty$-category of
Borel complete $G$-spectra has a particularly simple description as
spectra with a $G$-action. We can generalise this to the situation with
coefficients.

::: {#lem:resasbasechange .lem}
**Lemma 1**. *There is a symmetric monoidal equivalence
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\mathbb{D}(G)\otimes R}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \stackrel{\sim}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}),$$
and the equivalence functor fits into the following commutative diagram:
$$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)} & \\
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\mathbb{D}(G)\otimes R}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}
    \arrow["{\textnormal{res}^G_1}"', from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow["{F_{\mathbb{D}(G) \otimes R}}", from=1-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["\sim"', from=2-2, to=2-1]
\end{tikzcd}$$*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* The case for $R = \mathbb{S}$ is [@mnn17 Theorem 5.32] (see
also [@bds15 Theorem 1.1] for the original version), and a more general
statement than the one above is shown in [@bchnl25 Proposition 3.9]. ◻
:::

::: {#rem:nonequivariantlycontractible .rem}
**Remark 1**. *When $R = \mathbb{S}_G$,
[1](#lem:resasbasechange){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:resasbasechange"} shows that for a $G$-spectrum $Y$ the
condition $Y \otimes \mathbb{D}(G) \simeq 0$ is equivalent to $Y$ being
*nonequivariantly contractible* (that is,
$\textnormal{res}^G_1(Y) \simeq 0$) [@mnn17 Proposition 6.15]. Since
$\mathbb{D}(G)$ is dualisable these conditions also agree with the one
that $Y$ is *$\mathbb{D}(G)^{-1}$-local* [@mnn17 §3.2].\
More generally, if $Y$ is an $R$-module we have
$Y \otimes_R (\mathbb{D}(G) \otimes R) \simeq 0$ if and only if
$\textnormal{res}^G_1(Y) \simeq 0$ in
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$,
which holds if and only if
$\textnormal{res}^G_1(U_R(Y)) \simeq U_R(\textnormal{res}^G_1(Y)) \simeq 0$
in $\textsf{\textup{Sp}}$.*
:::

::: {#prop:borelcompleteasfun .prop}
**Proposition 1**. *There is an equivalence of symmetric monoidal
$\infty$-categories
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}} \simeq \textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})),$$
where the right-hand side carries the pointwise monoidal structure.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* This is the same proof as in [@mnn17 Proposition 6.17], where
the statement is shown for $R = \mathbb{S}_G$. We note that
$\mathbb{D}(G) \otimes R$ is a dualisable object of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ since it is the image
of the dualisable object $\mathbb{D}(G)$ under the free functor. Hence
by [@mnn17 Theorem 2.30] the $\infty$-category of
$(\mathbb{D}(G) \otimes R)$-complete objects is computed as the
totalisation of the cosimplicial extension of scalars diagram
$$\begin{equation}
\label{diag:totalisation}
\begin{tikzcd}
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\mathbb{D}(G)\otimes R}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\mathbb{D}(G \times G) \otimes R}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)} & {\cdots}
    \arrow[shift right=2, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[shift left=2, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[from=1-2, to=1-1]
    \arrow[from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[shift left=4, from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[shift right=4, from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[shift right=2, from=1-3, to=1-2]
    \arrow[shift left=2, from=1-3, to=1-2]
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}$$ which is obtained by applying the module functor to the
cobar construction for the algebra object
$\mathbb{D}(G) \otimes R \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G))$.
By [1](#lem:resasbasechange){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:resasbasechange"} we can identify
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\mathbb{D}(G)\otimes R}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$
with
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$,
and similarly
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\mathbb{D}(G^n)\otimes R}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$
identifies with
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+G^{n-1}, \,\mathbb{S}) \otimes \textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \simeq \prod_{G^{n-1}} \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).$$
The cosimplicial diagram
[\[diag:totalisation\]](#diag:totalisation){reference-type="ref"
reference="diag:totalisation"} then becomes $$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\prod_{G} \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & \cdots
    \arrow[shift right=2, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[shift left=2, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[from=1-2, to=1-1]
    \arrow[from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[shift left=4, from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[shift right=4, from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[shift right=2, from=1-3, to=1-2]
    \arrow[shift left=2, from=1-3, to=1-2]
\end{tikzcd}$$ and its totalisation is precisely the functor category
$\textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))$,
using the standard bar construction simplicial diagram that realises to
$BG$. ◻
:::

::: {#rem:forgetfulonborelcompletes .rem}
**Remark 1**. *The observation made in
[1](#rem:borelcompleteforget){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:borelcompleteforget"} says that the forgetful functor
$U_R: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$
induces a functor
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}} \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G_{\textnormal{Borel}}$,
which we can equivalently describe as the postcomposition
$$\textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})) \to \textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
with the forgetful functor
$U_{\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}$.*
:::

In particular, we see that the Borel complete subcategory of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ only depends on the
underlying nonequivariant spectrum $\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)$ of $R$. We
can make this precise as follows.

::: {#cor:justrestriction .cor}
**Corollary 1**. *There is an equivalence of symmetric monoidal
$\infty$-categories
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}} \simeq \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/G}\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}.$$*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* Since
$\textnormal{res}^G_1\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/G} \simeq \textnormal{id}$
as symmetric monoidal functors
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}\to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}$ there is an equivalence
of algebra objects
$\textnormal{res}^G_1(R) \simeq \textnormal{res}^G_1\textnormal{infl}^G_1\textnormal{res}^G_1(R)$.
The statement then follows from
[1](#prop:borelcompleteasfun){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:borelcompleteasfun"}. ◻
:::

::: {#ex:manylambdas .ex}
**Example 1**. *For $\Lambda$ a discrete commutative ring, we can
consider the Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum associated to its constant
Mackey functor $\underline{\Lambda}$, the Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum
associated to the $\Lambda$-linear Burnside Mackey functor
$\mathbb{A}_{\Lambda}$, and the inflation $\Lambda_G$ of its
nonequivariant Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum to $\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$.
Then all of the three Borel complete categories associated to these
equivariant ring spectra are equivalent to
$\textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\Lambda}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))$.*
:::

We will hence restrict our study of the Borel complete category with
coefficients to coefficients of the from
$R_G \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/G}(R)$, where
$R \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$ is a nonequivariant ring
spectrum. Note that this of course includes the case
$R_G = \mathbb{S}_G$.

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *Consider the adjunction
$(L_{G;\mathbb{S}_G},\iota): \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G\rightleftarrows \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G_{\textnormal{Borel}}$.
In view of [1](#prop:borelcompleteasfun){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:borelcompleteasfun"} we can express it in terms of
spectral Mackey functors as
$$(\theta^*,\theta_*): \textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \rightleftarrows \textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Sp}}),$$
where $\theta^*$ restricts along the map
$\theta: BG \to \textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}}$ that picks
out the orbit $G/1$, hence $\theta^*(X)$ is given by the mapping
spectrum
$\textnormal{map}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(\Sigma^\infty_+G/1,X)$, and
the functor $\theta_*$ is given by right Kan extension [@bgs20 §8]. One
may also view the functor $\theta^*$ as the one that the restriction
$\textnormal{res}^G_1: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G\to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}$
factors through by remembering the $G$-action on the section of the
orbit $G/1$.\
The functor $\theta_!$ given by left Kan extension is fully faithful as
well and has essential image the $G$-spectra with geometric fixed points
concentrated at the trivial group [@hau24 §2]. By [@psw22 §4] for
$R=\mathbb{Z}$ and [@bhs23 §14] for the general case there is a
symmetric monoidal equivalence
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \simeq \textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})),$$
and we can express the adjunction
$(L_{G;R},\iota): \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \rightleftarrows \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
as one of functor categories
$$(\theta^*,\theta_*): \textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})) \rightleftarrows \textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})),$$
where the functor
$\theta: BG \to \textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}}$ is as
before. Now $\theta^*$ can be expressed as the mapping $R$-module
spectrum
$\textnormal{map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\Sigma^\infty_+G,-)$.
In view of
[1](#rem:forgetfulonborelcompletes){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:forgetfulonborelcompletes"} there is a diagram
$$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))} & {\textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} \\
    {\textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))} & {\textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Sp}})}
    \arrow["{U_R}", from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[""{name=0, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\theta^*}"', shift right=2, from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow[""{name=1, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\theta^*}"', shift right=2, from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow[""{name=2, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\theta_*}"', shift right=2, hook, from=2-1, to=1-1]
    \arrow["{U_R}", from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow[""{name=3, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\theta_*}"', shift right=2, hook, from=2-2, to=1-2]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center}, draw=none, from=0, to=2]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center}, draw=none, from=1, to=3]
\end{tikzcd}$$ in which both squares commute. The horizontal functors
postcompose with the forgetful functor
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}$.
[1](#cor:completionunderlying){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:completionunderlying"} says that $X$ in the top left is
in the essential image of $\theta_*$ if and only if $U_R(X)$ is in the
essential image of $\theta_*$.*
:::

::: {#cor:tstructureonborelcomplete .cor}
**Corollary 1**. *When the coefficients $R$ are connective, there is a
t-structure on
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
which makes the localisation functor $L_{G;R}$ t-exact. The heart is
given by the category $\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;\pi_0(R))$ of
$\pi_0(R)$-modules with a linear $G$-action.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* First note that $\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
has a t-structure which is detected on the level of underlying spectra
[@lur17 Proposition 7.1.1.13]. Then the functor category
$\textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))$
inherits a t-structure which is detected pointwise. Similarly, the
t-structure on
$\textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))$
is detected pointwise after forgetting the $R$-module structure [@bgs20
§6]. It follows that the restriction functor $\theta^*$ is t-exact. For
the statement about the heart we use [@lur17 Proposition 7.1.1.13] and
the equivalence
$\textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))^{\heartsuit} \simeq \textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})^{\heartsuit})$. ◻
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *One can also check by hand that the essential image of
the two aisles
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G})_{\geq 0}$ and
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G})_{\leq 0}$ under
the Borel completion functor $L_{G;R}$ define a t-structure on
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G})_{\textnormal{Borel}}$,
the key ingredient being that the composition $\iota\circ L_{G;R}$ is
left t-exact: whenever
$X \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G})$ is
coconnective, the completion
$$\underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+EG \otimes R_G, X)$$
is coconnective as well, since the equivariant infinite loop space of
its underlying $G$-spectrum is the mapping space
$\textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+EG, U_R(X))$,
which is discrete since $\Sigma^{\infty}_+EG$ is connective. In more
categorical terms,
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
takes part in a recollement [@lur17 Definition A.8.1, Remark A.8.19]
$$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)[(\mathbb{D}(G)\otimes R_G)^{-1}]} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G})} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}.}
    \arrow[""{name=0, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, shift right=2, hook, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[""{name=1, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, shift right=2, from=1-2, to=1-1]
    \arrow[""{name=2, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{L_{G;R_G}}", shift left=2, from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[""{name=3, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\iota}", shift left=2, hook', from=1-3, to=1-2]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center, rotate=-90}, draw=none, from=2, to=3]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center, rotate=-90}, draw=none, from=1, to=0]
\end{tikzcd}$$ On the left is the full subcategory of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G})$ on
$(\mathbb{D}(G)\otimes R_G)^{-1}$-local objects which identifies with
the kernel of $L_{G;R_G}$, see [@mnn17 Proposition 3.11] and [@npr24
§1]. Then the t-structure on
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
from above is restricted from the one on
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G})$, in the sense of
[@bbd81 Proposition 1.4.12].*
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *By [@npr24 Proposition 4.14] the recollement of the
previous remark is base changed from the one for $G$-spectra along
$F_{R_G}: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G\to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$.
In particular, the completion functor
$$L_{G;R_G}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G}) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$$
identifies with the functor
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G}) \otimes_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G} L_{G;\mathbb{S}_G}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G}) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G}) \otimes_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G} \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G_{\textnormal{Borel}}.$$*
:::

Before turning our attention to the case of profinite groups, we
establish some change of group results we will use later.

::: {#lem:categoricalfixedpointsborelcomplete .lem}
**Lemma 1**. *Let $N \unlhd G$ be a normal subgroup. Denote by
$$(-)^N:\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N})$$
the categorical fixed point functor that is right adjoint to the functor
induced by inflation
$\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N} \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$
on module categories. Then $(-)^N$ restricts to a functor
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}} \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N})_{\textnormal{Borel}}.$$*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* Let $X \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$
be Borel complete and let
$Y \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ such
that $\textnormal{res}^{G/N}_1(Y) \simeq 0$. Then
$\textnormal{res}^G_1\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}(Y) \simeq \textnormal{res}^{G/N}_1(Y) \simeq 0$,
and hence
$$\textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N})}(Y,X^N) \simeq \textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}(Y),X) \simeq 0,$$
so $X^N \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ is
Borel complete. ◻
:::

We denote the functor induced by categorical fixed points by
$$(-)^{hN}:\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}} \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N})_{\textnormal{Borel}}.$$
We will explain the notation that alludes to homotopy fixed points
below. It follows formally that $(-)^{hN}$ has a left adjoint given by
$$h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N} \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=L_{G;R}\circ \textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}\circ \iota: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N})_{\textnormal{Borel}} \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}.$$
We hence obtain a diagram in which the respective squares of left and
right adjoints commute: $$\begin{equation}
\label{diag:fixedpointsinflationborel}
\begin{tikzcd}
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}} \\
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N})} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N})_{\textnormal{Borel}}.}
    \arrow[""{name=0, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{L_{G;R}}", shift left=2, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[""{name=1, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{(-)^N}", shift left=2, from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow[""{name=2, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, shift left=2, hook', from=1-2, to=1-1]
    \arrow[""{name=3, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{(-)^{hN}}", shift left=2, from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow[""{name=4, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}}", shift left=2, from=2-1, to=1-1]
    \arrow[""{name=5, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{L_{G/N;R}}", shift left=2, from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow[""{name=6, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}}", shift left=2, from=2-2, to=1-2]
    \arrow[""{name=7, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, shift left=2, hook', from=2-2, to=2-1]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center, rotate=-90}, draw=none, from=0, to=2]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center, rotate=-90}, draw=none, from=5, to=7]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center}, draw=none, from=4, to=1]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center}, draw=none, from=6, to=3]
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}$$

It is a well known fact that on Borel complete $G$-spectra categorical
fixed points and homotopy fixed points of the underlying spectrum with a
$G$-action agree, and in fact this characterises Borel complete
$G$-spectra [@mnn17 Proposition 6.19]. Taking residual actions into
account, we make this precise in our setting as follows.

::: {#prop:categoricalfixedpointsasrke .prop}
**Proposition 1**. *Under the equivalence of
[1](#prop:borelcompleteasfun){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:borelcompleteasfun"}, the adjunction
$$(h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}, (-)^{hN}):\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N})_{\textnormal{Borel}} \rightleftarrows \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$$
of
[1](#lem:categoricalfixedpointsborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:categoricalfixedpointsborelcomplete"} identifies with the
adjunction
$$(\beta^*,\beta_*): \textnormal{Fun}(B(G/N),\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})) \rightleftarrows \textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})),$$
where $\beta^*$ is given by restriction along the canonical map
$\beta: BG \to B(G/N)$ induced by the quotient homomorphism
$G \twoheadrightarrow G/N$ and $\beta_*$ is given by right Kan
extension.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* We show the commutativity of the following diagram.
$$\begin{equation}
\label{diag:inflationcompletion}
\begin{tikzcd}[scale cd=0.96]
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}} \simeq \textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))} \\
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N})} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N})_{\textnormal{Borel}} \simeq \textnormal{Fun}(B(G/N),\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))}
    \arrow["{L_{G;R}}", from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["{\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}}"', from=2-1, to=1-1]
    \arrow["{L_{G/N;R}}", from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["{\beta^*}"', shift right=5, from=2-2, to=1-2]
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}$$ Recall that in terms of spectral Mackey functors the
top and bottom horizontal compositions are given by restriction along
the maps $\theta_G: BG \to \textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^\textnormal{op}$
and
$\theta_{G/N}: B(G/N) \to \textsf{\textup{Span}}(G/N)^\textnormal{op}$
that pick out the orbits $G/1$ and $(G/N)/(N/N)$ respectively. Then the
restriction functor
$\textnormal{res}^G_1: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
factors as $\textnormal{ev}_G\,\theta_G^*$, where
$\textnormal{ev}_G: \textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
evaluates at the unique object of $BG$, and we similarly have
$\textnormal{res}^{G/N}_1 \simeq \textnormal{ev}_{G/N}\circ \theta_{G/N}^*$.\
Consider the unit map
$\textnormal{id} \to (-)^N\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}$ of endofunctors of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N})$. Composing
with $\theta_{G/N}^*$ gives a map $$\begin{align}
\label{eq:thetastransformation}
\theta_{G/N}^* \to \theta_{G/N}^* (-)^N\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N} \simeq  \tilde{\theta}_{G/N}^*\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N},
\end{align}$$ where
$\tilde{\theta}_{G/N}: B(G/N) \to \textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^\textnormal{op}$
picks out $G/N$. Here we use the description of categorical fixed points
as a restriction along a functor of span categories, see
[1](#rem:changeofgroupspectralmackey){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:changeofgroupspectralmackey"}. Now, evaluating spectral
$G$-Mackey functors on the map $G/1 \twoheadrightarrow G/N$ provides a
natural transformation $\beta^*\tilde{\theta}_{G/N}^* \to \theta_{G}^*$.
Through composition with
[\[eq:thetastransformation\]](#eq:thetastransformation){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:thetastransformation"} we obtain a natural transformation
$$\begin{align}
\label{eq:finalformation}
\beta^*\theta_{G/N}^* \to \theta_{G}^*\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}.
\end{align}$$ By the above considerations and using
$\textnormal{ev}_G \, \beta^* \simeq \textnormal{ev}_{G/N}$, composing
this natural transformation with
$\textnormal{ev}_G: \textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
gives a natural transformation
$\textnormal{res}^G_1\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N} \to \textnormal{res}^{G/N}_1$
which is an equivalence. Since $\textnormal{ev}_G$ is conservative,
[\[eq:finalformation\]](#eq:finalformation){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:finalformation"} is an equivalence as well. ◻
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *Denote by $\alpha_!$ the left Kan extension along the
inflation on span categories
$\alpha: \textsf{\textup{Span}}(G/N) \to \textsf{\textup{Span}}(G), \; (G/N)/(H/N) \mapsto G/H$.
We can illustrate the commutativity of the square of left adjoints
$$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))} & {\textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))} \\
    {\textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G/N)^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))} & {\textnormal{Fun}(B(G/N),\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})),}
    \arrow["{\theta^*_G}", from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["{\alpha_!}"', from=2-1, to=1-1]
    \arrow["{\theta_{G/N}^*}", from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["{\beta^*}"', from=2-2, to=1-2]
\end{tikzcd}$$ on objects as follows: For a spectral $G/N$-Mackey
functor $\mathcal{F}$ we obtain the restriction
$\beta^*\theta_{G/N}^*(\mathcal{F}) \simeq \mathcal{F}(G/N)$ which has a
$G$-action through $G \twoheadrightarrow G/N$. On the other hand,
$\alpha_!$ (using [@chll24 Proposition 3.3.2]) sends $\mathcal{F}$ to
the functor that assigns
$$G/H \mapsto \textnormal{colim}\left(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G/N)^\textnormal{op}\times_{\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^\textnormal{op}} (\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^\textnormal{op})_{\textnormal{\big /}(G/H)} \stackrel{\mathcal{F}}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{Sp}}\right),$$
and $\theta^*_G$ evaluates this at the orbit $G/1$, for which we get the
indexing category
$$\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G/N)^\textnormal{op}\times_{\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^\textnormal{op}} (\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^\textnormal{op})_{\textnormal{\big /}(G/1)}.$$
By the dual of [@bh21 p.106] it has a reflective subcategory
$$G/N\textsf{\textup{-set}}^\textnormal{op}\times_{G\textsf{\textup{-set}}^\textnormal{op}} (G\textsf{\textup{-set}}^\textnormal{op})_{\textnormal{\big /}(G/1)},$$
and the latter has a terminal object $G/1 \to G/N$, the canonical
projection. As right adjoints are cofinal [@kerodon Corollary 7.2.3.7]
we have $\theta^*_G\alpha_!(\mathcal{F}) \simeq \mathcal{F}(G/N)$.*
:::

This now justifies our notation of the induced functors on Borel
complete subcategories as *homotopy* fixed points $(-)^{hN}$ and
*homotopy* inflation $h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}$, since in terms of
spectra with an action of $G$ (resp. $G/N$) these functors take the
limit over $BN$ (resp. inflate the $G/N$-action to a $G$ action via
$G \twoheadrightarrow G/N$), which is what is known as homotopy fixed
points and inflation.\
Concretely, if $X$ is a spectrum with a $G$-action its homotopy
$N$-fixed points are modelled by the totalisation of the cosimplicial
object $$\begin{tikzcd}
    X & {\prod_N X} & {\prod_{N^2} X \;\; \cdots}
    \arrow[shift left, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[shift right, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[shift left=2, from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[shift right=2, from=1-2, to=1-3]
\end{tikzcd}$$ which has a residual $G/N$-action. The resulting spectrum
$X^{hN}$ can again be viewed as a cosimplicial object over the opposite
of the diagram that computes $B(G/N)$.

::: cor
**Corollary 1**. *The functor
$h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G/N}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G/N})_{\textnormal{Borel}} \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
is symmetric monoidal and t-exact with respect to the t-structure of
[1](#cor:tstructureonborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:tstructureonborelcomplete"}.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* Since
$\beta^*: \textnormal{Fun}(B(G/N),\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})) \to \textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))$
has these properties this follows immediately. ◻
:::

::: cor
**Corollary 1**. *For
$M \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$, the Borel
complete $R_G$-module
$$\underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+EG \otimes R, \textnormal{infl}^G_{G/N}(M))$$
obtained by completing the inflation of $M$ to $G$-spectra[^5] is
canonically equivalent to the right Kan extension $\theta_*(M)$, where
now $M$ is viewed as an object of
$\textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))$
with trivial $G$-action.*
:::

## \...and for profinite groups

The aim of this section is to develop a theory of Borel completeness for
profinite groups. We first consider a 'naive' notion which we call
*levelwise* Borel completeness - categorically this is the limit of the
Borel complete subcategories along fixed points, running over all open
normal subgroups of the group. We then introduce a second notion that is
given by completeness with respect to the colimit of the (duals of the)
orbits of all open normal subgroups. We finally identify the latter
notion with the hypercompletion of the former, in a t-structure sense.\
Throughout this section $G$ is a profinite group. We write
$(N_i)_{i \in I}$ for the cofiltered system of open normal subgroups of
$G$ under inclusion and let
$G_i \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=G/N_i$, so that $G = \lim_i G_i$. If $H \leq G$ is a closed subgroup,
then we have $H \cong \lim_i H_i$ and $H$ is itself profinite, since
$H_i \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=HN_i/N_i \cong H/(H \cap N_i)$ is a subgroup of the finite group $G_i$.
As before, $R \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$ is a
commutative nonequivariant ring spectrum which we now assume to be
connective. Again we write
$R_{K} \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=\textnormal{infl}^K_{K/K}(R)$ for its inflation to a group $K$,
whenever this makes sense.

::: {#rec:specgascolimit .rec}
**Recollection 1**. *There is a functor
$$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_{\bullet}}: I^\textnormal{op}\to \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}_{\textnormal{st}}), \; i \mapsto \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i}, \; (N_i \subseteq N_j) \mapsto (\textnormal{infl}_{G_j}^{G_i}: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_j} \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i}),$$
and as explained in [@bbb24 §6], [@bt26 Corollary 5.7] its colimit is
equivalent to $\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$. Equivalently, the latter is the
limit of the adjoint diagram of categorical fixed point functors in
$\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{R}}$ (using that the forgetful
functor
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}_{\textnormal{st}}) \to \textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}$
preserves filtered colimits [@lur17 Remark 4.8.1.24, Corollary
3.2.3.2]). As before, the canonical left adjoints into the colimit are
denoted
$\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i} \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$,
and their right adjoints
$(-)^{N_i}: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G\to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i}$. In
the presence of coefficients there is an analogous functor
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_{\bullet}}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_{\bullet}}): I^\textnormal{op}\to \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}_{\textnormal{st}})$$
which sends $(N_i \subseteq N_j)$ to the induced inflation on module
categories. Its colimit is equivalent to
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$. This follows from
[@agv22 Lemma 3.5.6] or by combining [@bt26 Theorem 5.6] with [@bhs23
§14]. We employ the same notation as above for the canonical left
adjoints into this colimit and their right adjoints.\
In both cases the colimit can also be computed in
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}_{\omega,\textnormal{st}})$,
the $\infty$-category
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G})$ is rigidly
compactly generated by the free modules associated to the orbits of open
normal subgroups. Because of
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}_{\omega,\textnormal{st}}) \simeq \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Cat}}_{\infty}^{\textnormal{perf}})$
and by [@lur17 Lemma 7.3.5.1] the filtered colimit of the compact parts
is computed in $\textsf{\textup{Cat}}_{\infty}$. In particular, a
compact $R_{G}$-module is inflated from $G_i$ for some $i \in I$.*
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *Whenever we write down diagrams of $\infty$-categories of
equivariant spectra as above we use the fact that there is a functor
$$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{(-)}: \textsf{\textup{Glo}}^\textnormal{op}\to \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}_{\textnormal{st}}), \; L \mapsto \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^L, \; (\alpha: L \to L') \mapsto (\alpha^*: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{L'} \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^L),$$
where $\textsf{\textup{Glo}}$ the global category of finite groups,
group homomorphisms and conjugations,[^6] see e.g. [@lnp25 §10]. It is a
(2,1)-category which we view as an $\infty$-category via the Duskin
nerve. We can then restrict this functor to the 1-category
$I^\textnormal{op}$ to obtain the desired filtered diagram.*
:::

::: {#defi:levelwiseborelcomplete .defi}
**Definition 1**. *We define the $\infty$-category of *levelwise Borel
complete $R_G$-modules* as the
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}_{\textnormal{st}})$-colimit
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}} \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=\textnormal{colim}_i \; \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})_{\textnormal{Borel}}$$
along the homotopy inflations
$h\textnormal{infl}^{G_i}_{G_j}:\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_j}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_j})_{\textnormal{Borel}} \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
for each inclusion $N_i \subseteq N_j$.*
:::

We denote the canonical functors
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})_{\textnormal{Borel}} \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$
by $h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}$ and write $(-)^{hN_i}$ for their right
adjoints.

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *After forgetting the monoidal structure,
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$
is given by the
$\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{R}}_{\textnormal{st}}$-limit
$$\textnormal{lim}_i \; \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})_{\textnormal{Borel}}$$
along homotopy fixed points
$(-)^{hN_{ij}}:\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})_{\textnormal{Borel}} \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_j}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_j})_{\textnormal{Borel}}$,
where
$N_{ij} = N_j/N_i = \textnormal{ker}(G/N_i \twoheadrightarrow (G/N_i)/(N_j/N_i) = G/N_j)$.
As a limit of the fully faithful functors
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})_{\textnormal{Borel}} \hookrightarrow \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})$
the induced functor
$$\iota_{\textnormal{lw}}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}} \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$$
is fully faithful as well. We can hence view an object of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$
as a compatible collection of $R_{G_i}$-modules $(X_i)_{i \in I}$ (i.e.,
$(X_i)^{N_{ij}} \simeq X_j$) such that each $X_i$ is Borel complete. We
denote the symmetric monoidal left adjoint of $\iota_{\textnormal{lw}}$
by $L_{\textnormal{lw}}$ and call it *levelwise Borel completion*. It
makes the following square commute: $$\begin{equation}
\label{diag:levelwisecompletion}
\begin{tikzcd}
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})_{\textnormal{Borel}}} \\
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G})} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}.}
    \arrow["{L_{G_i;R_{G_i}}}", from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["{\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}}", from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow["{h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}}", from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow["{L_{\textnormal{lw}}}", from=2-1, to=2-2]
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}$$*
:::

::: warn
**Warning 1**. *It follows from [@mnn17 Proposition 2.27] that for each
$i\in I$ the stable $\infty$-category
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
has a compact generator $\Sigma^{\infty}_+G_i \otimes R_{G_i}$. In terms
of the description
$\textnormal{Fun}(BG_i,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))$
of [1](#prop:borelcompleteasfun){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:borelcompleteasfun"} this generator is given by the
group algebra
$R[G_i] = \Sigma^{\infty}_+ G_i \otimes R \in \textnormal{Alg}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$,
see also [@mnn17 Remark 6.18]. The homotopy inflation functors in
general do not preserve these generators, and the colimit of
[1](#defi:levelwiseborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="defi:levelwiseborelcomplete"} is generally not compactly
generated.*
:::

We will now show that there is a canonical t-structure on
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$,
and we will record certain properties of this t-structure we will use
later on.

::: {#prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete .prop}
**Proposition 1**. *There is a t-structure on
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$
which is induced by the t-structures of
[1](#cor:tstructureonborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:tstructureonborelcomplete"}. It is right complete,
compatible with filtered colimits and compatible with the symmetric
monoidal structure.[^7]\
The canonical functors
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})_{\textnormal{Borel}} \to\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$
are t-exact, and the heart is given by the category
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;\pi_0(R))$ of discrete $\pi_0(R)$-modules on
which $G$ acts continuously.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* In our situation [@rs20 Lemma 3.2.18] shows the existence of
the t-structure and the t-exactness of the structural maps into the
colimit. The proof we give ensures the desired properties of this
t-structure.\
For each $i \in I$, the t-structure on
$\textnormal{Fun}(BG_i,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))$
is right complete and compatible with filtered colimits since $R$ is
assumed to be connective, which implies that the t-structure on
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$ is [@lur17 Proposition
7.1.1.13]. Hence by [@lur18 §C.1.2] there is a canonical equivalence to
the stabilisation
$$\textnormal{Fun}(BG_i,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})) \simeq \textsf{\textup{Sp}}(\textnormal{Fun}(BG_i,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})_{\geq 0}).$$
Since each of the functors $h\textnormal{infl}^{G_i}_{G_j}$ is t-exact,
[@lur18 Lemma C.2.4.4] implies that they restrict to left exact left
adjoints on connective parts
$$(h\textnormal{infl}^{G_i}_{G_j})_{\geq 0}: \textnormal{Fun}(BG_j,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})_{\geq 0}) \to \textnormal{Fun}(BG_i,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})_{\geq 0}),$$
and by [@lur18 Lemma C.3.1.1] the functor induced by each
$(h\textnormal{infl}^{G_i}_{G_j})_{\geq 0}$ on stabilisations is again
given by $h\textnormal{infl}^{G_i}_{G_j}$. Now, the
$\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}$-colimit
$$\mathcal{C}\mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=\textnormal{colim}_i \; \textnormal{Fun}(BG_i,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})_{\geq 0})$$
along the functors $(h\textnormal{infl}^{G_i}_{G_j})_{\geq 0}$ is one of
Grothendieck prestable $\infty$-categories and left exact left adjoints,
