so by [@lur18 Proposition C.3.3.5] the colimit is again Grothendieck
prestable and the canonical maps into the colimit
$$(h\textnormal{infl}^{G}_{G_i})_{\geq 0}: \textnormal{Fun}(BG_i,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})_{\geq 0}) \to \mathcal{C}$$
are left exact left adjoints. Since the functor
$\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}\to \textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}_{\textnormal{st}}$
preserves colimits [@lur17 Proposition 4.8.2.18], the stabilisation
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}(\mathcal{C})$ is equivalent to
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$.
So
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$
is the stabilisation of a Grothendieck prestable $\infty$-category and
thus carries a canonical right complete t-structure that is compatible
with filtered colimits, see e.g. [@lur18 Remark C.3.1.5]. It follows
from [@lur18 Lemma C.3.1.1, Proposition C.3.2.1] that the stabilisations
of the structure maps into the colimit
$$h\textnormal{infl}^{G}_{G_i} \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=\textsf{\textup{Sp}}((h\textnormal{infl}^{G}_{G_i})_{\geq 0}): \textnormal{Fun}(BG_i,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$$
are t-exact. For each $i$, the connective part
$\textnormal{Fun}(BG_i,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})_{\geq 0})$
is a symmetric monoidal subcategory of its stabilisation, and the
transition functors $(h\textnormal{infl}^{G_i}_{G_j})_{\geq 0}$ are
symmetric monoidal. We can hence compute $\mathcal{C}$ as a filtered
colimit in $\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}})$,
and the unit map
$$\mathcal{C}\simeq \mathcal{C}\otimes_{\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}} \textsf{\textup{Spc}}\to \mathcal{C}\otimes_{\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}} \textsf{\textup{Sp}}\simeq \textsf{\textup{Sp}}(\mathcal{C})$$
induced by
$\Sigma^\infty_+: \textsf{\textup{Spc}}\to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}$ is
symmetric monoidal, which means that the t-structure on
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}(\mathcal{C})$ is compatible with the symmetric
monoidal structure. This monoidal structure agrees with the one of
[1](#defi:levelwiseborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="defi:levelwiseborelcomplete"}.\
By [@lur18 §C.1.2] the heart of the resulting t-structure is given by
the discrete objects of its prestable part, and by [@lur18 Proposition
C.3.2.1] all transition functors in the diagram of prestable parts
preserve discrete objects. Since the $0$-truncation functor commutes
with filtered colimits and using
[1](#cor:tstructureonborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:tstructureonborelcomplete"} the heart is the filtered
colimit of the $\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;\pi_0(R))$ along inflation,
which is $\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;\pi_0(R))$. This concludes the
proof. ◻
:::

::: {#cons:colimitoforbits .cons}
**Construction 1**. *For $H$ a closed subgroup of $G$ we consider the
adjunction
$(\textnormal{res}^G_H, \textnormal{coind}^G_H): \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \rightleftarrows \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_H}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^H)$.
When $N_i \leq G$ is open and normal, we can view the coinduction
$\textnormal{coind}^G_{N_i}(R_{N_i})$ as a commutative algebra object of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$. Whenever
$N_i \subseteq N_j$, there is an equivalence
$\textnormal{coind}^G_{N_i} \simeq \textnormal{coind}^G_{N_j}\textnormal{coind}^{N_j}_{N_i}$,
and the unit map
$R_{N_j} \to \textnormal{coind}^{N_j}_{N_i}\textnormal{res}^{N_j}_{N_i}(R_{N_j}) \simeq \textnormal{coind}^{N_j}_{N_i}(R_{N_i})$
induces an algebra map
$$\textnormal{coind}^G_{N_j}(R_{N_j}) \to \textnormal{coind}^G_{N_j}\textnormal{coind}^{N_j}_{N_i}(R_{N_i}) \simeq \textnormal{coind}^G_{N_i}(R_{N_i})$$
by applying $\textnormal{coind}^G_{N_j}$. This determines a filtered
diagram
$I^\textnormal{op}\to \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G))$,
and we let
$A_R \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G))$
be its colimit. As in the finite group case
([1](#rec:coinddual){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rec:coinddual"}), there is an equivalence $$\begin{equation}
\label{eq:dualcoind}
\mathbb{D}(G/N_i) \otimes R_G \stackrel{\sim}{\longrightarrow} \textnormal{coind}^G_{N_i}\textnormal{res}^G_{N_i}(R_G) \simeq \textnormal{coind}^G_{N_i}(R_{N_i})
\end{equation}$$ in $\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$. Like in
the proof of [@bchnl25 Lemma 3.8], the equivalence
[\[eq:dualcoind\]](#eq:dualcoind){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:dualcoind"} boils down to the fact that the projection
formula for the adjunction
$(\textnormal{res}^G_{N_i}, \textnormal{coind}^G_{N_i})$ holds, see e.g.
[@bds16 Theorem 1.3]. In terms of the left-hand side of
[\[eq:dualcoind\]](#eq:dualcoind){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:dualcoind"} the map
$\textnormal{coind}^G_{N_j}(R_{N_j}) \to \textnormal{coind}^G_{N_i}(R_{N_i})$
is induced by the map of coalgebras in finite $G$-sets
$G/N_i \twoheadrightarrow G/N_j$ on function duals, tensored with $R_G$.
Since the tensor product on $\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$ commutes with
colimits in both variables it is clear that
$A_R \simeq A_{\mathbb{S}} \otimes R_G$.*
:::

Recall that in the finite group case
[1](#lem:resasbasechange){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:resasbasechange"} expressed the restriction functor to an
arbitrary subgroup as a base change. This is still true in the profinite
case when we work with closed subgroups.

::: {#lem:resasbasechangeprofinite .lem}
**Lemma 1**. *Let $H \leq G$ be a closed subgroup. Then there is a
symmetric monoidal equivalence
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{coind}^{G}_H(R_{H})}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \stackrel{\sim}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{H}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{H}),$$
and the equivalence functor fits into the following commutative diagram:
$$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)} & \\
    {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{H}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{H})} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\textnormal{coind}^{G}_H(R_{H})}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G).}
    \arrow["{\textnormal{res}^G_H}"', from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow["{F_{\textnormal{coind}^{G}_H(R_{H})}}", from=1-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["\sim"', from=2-2, to=2-1]
\end{tikzcd}$$ When $H$ additionally is of finite index (i.e., it is an
open subgroup), then the algebra object
$\textnormal{coind}^{G}_H(R_{H})$ identifies with
$\mathbb{D}(G/H)\otimes R_G$.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* This is shown as [@bchnl25 Proposition 3.9] by combining [@bs20
Lemma 3.9] and [@mnn17 Proposition 5.29].\
Note that the coinduction functor
$\textnormal{coind}^G_H:\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{H} \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$
still preserves colimits since its left adjoint $\textnormal{res}^G_H$
preserves compact objects (see also the description given in
[1](#rem:changeofgroupspectralmackey){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:changeofgroupspectralmackey"}). It is conservative since
all
$\textnormal{coind}^{G_i}_{HN_i/N_i}:\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{HN_i/N_i} \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i}$
are, and the family of categorical fixed point functors
$(-)^{H\cap N_i}: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{H} \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{HN_i/N_i}$
indexed by $i \in I$ is jointly conservative. The description of the
algebra object in the finite index case is the same as in
[1](#cons:colimitoforbits){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cons:colimitoforbits"}. ◻
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *The previous lemma is a striking difference to the case
where $G$ is a compact Lie group and $H$ is an arbitrary closed
subgroup, here $\textnormal{coind}^G_H$ in general fails to be
conservative [@mnn17 Warning p.1034]. In the compact Lie case the
restriction $\textnormal{res}^G_H$ always has a left adjoint
$\textnormal{ind}^G_H$ that is related to $\textnormal{coind}^G_H$ via a
Wirthmüller isomorphism. On the contrary, in the profinite case
$\textnormal{res}^G_H$ for $H$ again an arbitrary closed subgroup might
not have a left adjoint, although its right adjoint
$\textnormal{coind}^G_H$ always preserves colimits, cf.
[1](#rem:changeofgroupspectralmackey){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:changeofgroupspectralmackey"}. This goes in line with the
fact that tensoring with $\textnormal{coind}^{G}_H(R_{H})$, or
equivalently applying $\textnormal{coind}^G_H\textnormal{res}^G_H$,
might not preserve limits - we will see below that there is generally no
reason to expect that $\textnormal{coind}^{G}_H(R_{H})$ is compact (and
hence neither dualisable).*
:::

When we work with an arbitrary closed subgroup $H \leq G$, the only
caveat of the previous lemma is that $\textnormal{coind}^G_H(R_H)$ is
not a very explicit description of the algebra object we are working
with. For $H=1$ we will give a description of the functor
$\textnormal{coind}^G_1\textnormal{res}^G_1$ as tensoring with the
algebra object $A_R$ from
[1](#cons:colimitoforbits){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cons:colimitoforbits"}. We first prove a general statement
about filtered colimits of monadic adjunctions.\
Recall from [@mnn17 Construction 5.23] (or from
[1](#rec:modules){reference-type="ref+Label" reference="rec:modules"})
that if we have an adjunction
$(L,R): \mathcal{M}\rightleftarrows \mathcal{N}$ of presentably
symmetric monoidal $\infty$-categories where $L$ is symmetric monoidal,
then the right adjoint applied to the unit
$R(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{N}})$ has a commutative algebra structure and
there is an induced adjunction
$$(\bar{L},\bar{R}): \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{N}})}(\mathcal{M}) \rightleftarrows \mathcal{N}$$
for which the left adjoint $\bar{L}$ factors as
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{N}})}(\mathcal{M}) \stackrel{L}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{LR(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{N}})}(\mathcal{N}) \stackrel{\varepsilon_!}{\longrightarrow} \mathcal{N}$,
where
$\varepsilon: LR(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{N}}) \to \mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{N}}$
is the counit map. We will say that the adjunction $(L,R)$ is *monadic*
if the induced adjunction $(\bar{L},\bar{R})$ is an equivalence.

::: {#lem:filteredcolimitofmonadicadjunctions .lem}
**Lemma 1**. *Let $\mathcal{C}$ be a filtered $\infty$-category that has
an initial object $X_0$ and let
$\mathcal{E}: \mathcal{C}\to \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}})$
be a functor. Write $f_{X} = \mathcal{E}(X_0 \to X)$ and $g_X$ for its
right adjoint. Assume that for every $X \in \mathcal{C}$ the adjunction
$$(f_{X},g_{X}): \mathcal{E}(X_0) \rightleftarrows \mathcal{E}(X)$$ is
monadic. Set
$\mathcal{E}_{\infty} = \textnormal{colim}_{\mathcal{C}} \; \mathcal{E}$,
where the colimit is taken in
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}})$. Then the
induced adjunction
$$(f_{{\infty}},g_{\infty}): \mathcal{E}(X_0) \rightleftarrows \mathcal{E}_{\infty}$$
is monadic. In particular, there is an equivalence
$\mathcal{E}_{\infty} \simeq \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{g_{\infty}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{\infty}})}(\mathcal{E}(X_0))$,
and the commutative algebra object
$g_{\infty}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{\infty}})$ is equivalent to the
filtered colimit of the $g_{X}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}(X)})$ in
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\mathcal{E}(X_0))$.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* This is an application of [@agv22 Lemma 3.5.6] which computes
filtered colimits of module categories with extensions of scalars
between them.\
We write $A_X$ for
$g_{X}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}(X)}) \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\mathcal{E}(X_0))$.
The assignment $X \mapsto A_X$ determines a filtered diagram
$\epsilon: \mathcal{C}\to \textnormal{CAlg}(\mathcal{E}(X_0))$, and we
write $A_{\infty}$ for its colimit. Then by [@lur17 Theorem 4.8.5.11]
the functor $\mathcal{E}$ identifies with the composition
$$\mathcal{C}\stackrel{\epsilon}{\longrightarrow} \textnormal{CAlg}(\mathcal{E}(X_0)) \stackrel{\widehat{\Theta}_*}{\longrightarrow} \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}})_{\mathcal{E}(X_0)/} \stackrel{\textnormal{fgt}}{\longrightarrow} \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}), \; X \mapsto \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{A_X}(\mathcal{E}(X_0)),$$
where $\widehat{\Theta}_*$ is as in [@lur17 Notation 4.8.5.10]. Applying
[@agv22 Lemma 3.5.6] to the constant functor
$X \mapsto \mathcal{E}(X_0)$ and the diagram of algebra objects
$\epsilon$ gives an equivalence
$$\mathcal{E}_{\infty} \simeq \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{A_{\infty}}(\mathcal{E}(X_0))$$
under which the left adjoint
$f_{\infty}: \mathcal{E}(X_0) \to \mathcal{E}_{\infty}$ identifies with
the free module functor $A_{\infty} \otimes (-)$. In particular, by
applying its right adjoint $g_{\infty}$ to the unit of
$\mathcal{E}_{\infty}$ we see that
$g_{\infty}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{\infty}}) \simeq A_{\infty}$ in
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\mathcal{E}_{\infty})$. This concludes the proof. ◻
:::

::: {#rem:monads .rem}
**Remark 1**. *Since in the previous lemma the monads $g_Xf_X$ and
$g_{\infty}f_{\infty}$ in $\textnormal{End}(\mathcal{E}(X_0))$ are
determined by the algebra objects $g_{X}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}(X)})$
and $g_{\infty}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{\infty}})$ they tensor with,
the lemma implies that the monad $g_{\infty}f_{\infty}$ is the colimit
of the monads $g_Xf_X$ taken in endofunctors of $\mathcal{E}(X_0)$.*
:::

::: {#lem:spectraascolimit .lem}
**Lemma 1**. *There is a symmetric monoidal equivalence
$$\textnormal{colim}_i \; \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_i}) \simeq \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}),$$
where the colimit is taken in
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}_{\textnormal{st}})$
along the restrictions $\textnormal{res}^{N_j}_{N_i}$ for
$N_i \subseteq N_j$.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* Consider the filtered diagram $$\begin{align*}
&\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_{\bullet}}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_\bullet}): I^\textnormal{op}\to \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}_{\textnormal{st}}), \; i \mapsto \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_i}), \\
&(N_i \subseteq N_j) \mapsto (\textnormal{res}_{N_i}^{N_j}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_j}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_j}) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_i})).
\end{align*}$$ Restricting from each $N_i$ to the trivial subgroup
induces a symmetric monoidal left adjoint
$\varphi: \textnormal{colim}_i \; \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_i}) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$.
For $X \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$, its
inflation $\textnormal{infl}^{N_i}_1(X)$ to
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_i})$ (for any
$i$) has restriction
$\textnormal{res}^{N_i}_1\textnormal{infl}^{N_i}_1(X) \simeq X$, hence
$\varphi$ is essentially surjective. Since the restrictions in the
colimit diagram preserve compact objects their colimit is compactly
generated, and by [@lur09 Proposition 5.3.5.11] it suffices to show that
$\varphi$ is fully faithful when restricted to compacts.\
The compact part of
$\textnormal{colim}_i \; \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_i})$
is $\mathcal{C}\mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=\textnormal{colim}_i \; \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_i}}^{\omega}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_i})$,
where the filtered colimit is computed in
$\textsf{\textup{Cat}}_{\infty}$. For two objects
$\bar{X}, \bar{Y} \in \mathcal{C}$ we can assume without restriction
that they are of the form $\varphi_k(X)$ and $\varphi_k(Y)$
respectively, where $X$ and $Y$ are compact objects of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_k}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_k})$ for the
same index $k$ and $\varphi_k$ is the structure map
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_k}}^{\omega}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_k}) \to \mathcal{C}$.
We can furthermore reduce to the case where the objects $X$ and $Y$ are
compact generators, i.e. of the form
$\Sigma^{\infty}_+ N_k/H \otimes R_{N_k}$ and
$\Sigma^{\infty}_+ N_k/H' \otimes R_{N_k}$ for two open subgroups
$H,H' \leq N_k$. We hence consider the map $$\begin{equation}
\label{eq:varphimappingspaces}
\textnormal{Map}_{\mathcal{C}}(\bar{X},\bar{Y}) \stackrel{\varphi}{\longrightarrow} \textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R}^{\omega}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})}(\varphi(\bar{X}),\varphi(\bar{Y}))
\end{equation}$$ induced by $\varphi$ on mapping spaces. Note that the
functor $(I^\textnormal{op})_{k/} \to I^\textnormal{op}$ is cofinal
[@lur09 Example 5.4.5.9, Lemma 5.4.5.12], hence by [@lur09 Proposition
4.1.1.8] the left-hand side of
[\[eq:varphimappingspaces\]](#eq:varphimappingspaces){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:varphimappingspaces"} identifies with
$$\textnormal{colim}_{i \geq k} \; \textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_i}}^{\omega}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_i})}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+ \textnormal{res}^{N_k}_{N_i}(N_j/H) \otimes R_{N_i},\Sigma^{\infty}_+ \textnormal{res}^{N_k}_{N_i}(N_j/H') \otimes R_{N_i}),$$
where we understand $i \geq k$ as $N_i \subseteq N_k$. Now there is a
$k' \geq k$ such that for all $i \geq k'$ the normal subgroup $N_i$ is
contained in both $H$ and $H'$ and hence acts trivially on $N_k/H$ and
$N_k/H'$. Using the same cofinality argument as before, we can thus
further reduce to showing that the map $$\begin{equation}
\label{eq:varphimappingspacesreduced}
\textnormal{colim}_{i} \; \textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_i}}^{\omega}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_i})}(R_{N_i},R_{N_i}) \stackrel{\varphi}{\longrightarrow} \textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R}^{\omega}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})}(R,R)
\end{equation}$$ is an equivalence. Since each
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_i}}^{\omega}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_i})$
is itself a colimit along inflation, the left-hand side of
[\[eq:varphimappingspacesreduced\]](#eq:varphimappingspacesreduced){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:varphimappingspacesreduced"}, where we write
$N_{ij} \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=N_iN_j/N_j$, identifies with $$\begin{align}
&\underset{i, \, \textnormal{res}}{\textnormal{colim}} \; \; \underset{j, \, \textnormal{infl}}{\textnormal{colim}} \; \; \textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_{ij}}}^{\omega}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_{ij}})}(R_{N_{ij}},R_{N_{ij}}) \notag \\
\simeq \; &\underset{j, \, \textnormal{infl}}{\textnormal{colim}} \; \; \underset{i, \, \textnormal{res}}{\textnormal{colim}} \; \; \textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{N_{ij}}}^{\omega}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{N_{ij}})}(R_{N_{ij}},R_{N_{ij}}). \label{eq:finalmappingspace}
\end{align}$$ But for each $j$, there is an $i'$ such that
$N_i \subseteq N_j$ for all $i \geq i'$, and in this case
$N_{ij}=N_iN_j/N_j$ is trivial. Using cofinality once more, this shows
that
[\[eq:finalmappingspace\]](#eq:finalmappingspace){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:finalmappingspace"} is given by
$\textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R}^{\omega}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})}(R,R)$,
and the map
[\[eq:varphimappingspacesreduced\]](#eq:varphimappingspacesreduced){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:varphimappingspacesreduced"} is an equivalence, as
desired. ◻
:::

::: {#cor:identifycolimitoforbits .cor}
**Corollary 1**. *There is an equivalence
$\textnormal{coind}^G_1(R) \simeq A_R$ in
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G))$.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* Apply
[1](#lem:filteredcolimitofmonadicadjunctions){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:filteredcolimitofmonadicadjunctions"} to the diagram of
[1](#lem:spectraascolimit){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:spectraascolimit"}, using
[1](#lem:resasbasechangeprofinite){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:resasbasechangeprofinite"}. ◻
:::

::: {#cor:endofunctors .cor}
**Corollary 1**. *There are equivalences of endofunctors of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$
$$(A_R \otimes_{R_G} -) \simeq \textnormal{coind}^G_1\textnormal{res}^G_1 \simeq \textnormal{colim}_i \, \textnormal{coind}^G_{N_i}\textnormal{res}^G_{N_i}.$$*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* This follows from
[1](#lem:resasbasechangeprofinite){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:resasbasechangeprofinite"},
[1](#cor:identifycolimitoforbits){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:identifycolimitoforbits"} and
[1](#rem:monads){reference-type="ref+Label" reference="rem:monads"}. ◻
:::

::: ex
**Example 1**. *Using adjunction and
[1](#cor:identifycolimitoforbits){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:identifycolimitoforbits"} we obtain an equivalence
$$\textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}}(\mathbb{S},\mathbb{S}) \simeq \textnormal{colim}_i \; \textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+ G/N_i,\mathbb{S}_G),$$
which on $\pi_0$ recovers the fact that the filtered colimit of the
group completions of the monoids of finite $N_i$-sets under disjoint
union $\pi_0^{N_i}(\mathbb{S}_G) \cong \mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Z}}(G/N_i)$
is $\mathbb{Z}$.*
:::

Let us fix some terminology for t-structures we will use throughout this
paper.

::: {#def:hypercompletetstructure .defi}
**Definition 1**. *Let $\mathcal{C}$ be a stable $\infty$-category with
a t-structure $(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0},\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0})$ and denote
by $\pi_n(-): \mathcal{C}\to \mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}$ its integer
indexed homotopy object functors. We say that an object
$X \in \mathcal{C}$ is*

1.  **acyclic* if $\pi_n(X) = 0$ for all $n \in \mathbb{Z}$,*

2.  **hypercomplete* if
    $\textnormal{Map}_{\mathcal{C}}(Y,X) \simeq \ast$ for all acyclic
    $Y \in \mathcal{C}$.*

*We call $\mathcal{C}$ *hypercomplete* if all of its objects are
hypercomplete.*
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *That $\mathcal{C}$ is hypercomplete is equivalent to all
acyclic objects being zero. If the t-structure on $\mathcal{C}$ is right
complete then hypercompleteness is equivalent to the t-structure being
left separated, i.e.
$\bigcap_{n \in \mathbb{Z}} \mathcal{C}_{\geq n} = 0$. Again assuming
right completeness, hypercompleteness is implied by Postnikov
completeness, which asserts that for every object $X$ the canonical map
$X \to \lim_{n\geq 0} \tau_{\leq n} X$ is an equivalence. The latter is
precisely the statement that the canonical map
$\mathcal{C}\to \textnormal{lim}_{n \geq 0} \; \mathcal{C}_{\leq n}$ is
fully faithful. This map being an equivalence is the condition that the
t-structure on $\mathcal{C}$ is left complete.*
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *It is clear from the definition that the full subcategory
of hypercomplete objects $\mathcal{C}^h \subseteq \mathcal{C}$ is closed
under limits. If $\mathcal{C}$ is additionally assumed to be presentable
and its t-structure is compatible with filtered colimits then the
inclusion $\mathcal{C}^h \hookrightarrow C$ has an accessible left
adjoint we denote by $L^h: \mathcal{C}\to \mathcal{C}^h$ [@cm21
Proposition 2.14], [@lur18 Proposition C.1.4.1]. Note that
$\mathcal{C}^h$ inherits a t-structure that makes the functor $L^h$
t-exact. If $\mathcal{C}$ is furthermore presentably symmetric monoidal
and this symmetric monoidal structure is compatible with the t-structure
(i.e., the connective part is a symmetric monoidal subcategory), then
[@cm21 Lemma 2.22] gives a unique symmetric monoidal structure on
$\mathcal{C}^h$ that makes the hypercompletion functor symmetric
monoidal.*
:::

Recall that for a finite group Borel complete spectra were defined as
the right orthogonal of nonequivariantly contractible spectra. We now
give characterisations for the latter notion in the profinite group
case. One of them uses the t-structure from
[1](#prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete"}, which in contrast
to the t-structure on $G$-spectra is only right but generally not left
complete. In particular, there can be nonzero acyclic objects.

::: {#prop:noneqcontractibleprofinite .prop}
**Proposition 1**. *For an $R_G$-module $X$, the following are
equivalent:*

1.  *$X$ is nonequivariantly contractible, i.e.
    $\textnormal{res}^G_1(X) \simeq 0$ in
    $\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$.*

2.  *$\textnormal{res}^G_1U_{R_G}(X) \simeq U_R\textnormal{res}^G_1(X)$
    is contractible in $\textsf{\textup{Sp}}$.*

3.  *$X \otimes_{R_G} A_R \simeq 0$ in
    $\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$.*

4.  *$U_R(X \otimes_{R_G} A_R) \simeq U_R(X) \otimes A_{\mathbb{S}}$ is
    contractible in $\textsf{\textup{Sp}}$.*

5.  *The levelwise completion $L_{\textnormal{lw}}(X)$ is acyclic, that
    is $\pi_n(L_{\textnormal{lw}}(X))=0$ for all $n \in \mathbb{Z}$,
    where the $\pi_n(-)$ are the t-structure homotopy modules of
    [1](#prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete){reference-type="ref"
    reference="prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete"}.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* That (1) and (2) are equivalent is clear from the
conservativity of the forgetful functor and
[1](#rec:modules){reference-type="ref+Label" reference="rec:modules"},
similarly that (3) and (4) are equivalent follows from the
conservativity of the forgetful functor and the projection formula for
the free-forgetful adjunction. The equivalence of assertions (1) and (3)
follows from the conservativity of $\textnormal{coind}^G_1$ (see
[1](#lem:resasbasechangeprofinite){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:resasbasechangeprofinite"}) and
[1](#cor:endofunctors){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:endofunctors"}.\
We now show that (3) and (5) are equivalent. Let $X$ be an $R_G$-module.
By [@lur09 Lemma 6.3.3.6] we have
$X \simeq \textnormal{colim}_i \, \textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}(X^{N_i})$,
so
$$L_{\textnormal{lw}}(X) \simeq \textnormal{colim}_i \, L_{\textnormal{lw}}\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}(X^{N_i}) \stackrel{\ref{diag:levelwisecompletion}}{\simeq} \textnormal{colim}_i \, h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}\theta_i^*(X^{N_i}) \simeq \textnormal{colim}_i \, h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}\sigma_i^*(X),$$
where $\theta_i: BG_i \to \textsf{\textup{Span}}(G_i)^{\textnormal{op}}$
picks out the orbit $G_i/1$ and
$\sigma_i: BG_i \to \textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}}$ picks
out the orbit $G/N_i$, using that each of the triangles $$\begin{tikzcd}
    & {\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)^{\textnormal{op}}} \\
    {BG_i} & {\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G_i)^{\textnormal{op}}}
    \arrow["{\sigma_i}", from=2-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["{\theta_i}", from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["{\overline{\textnormal{infl}}^G_{G_i}}"', from=2-2, to=1-2]
\end{tikzcd}$$ commutes. (Recall from
[1](#rem:changeofgroupspectralmackey){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:changeofgroupspectralmackey"} that categorical fixed
points precompose with the vertical functor
$\overline{\textnormal{infl}}^G_{G_i}$ of span categories.) Since the
t-structure from
[1](#prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete"} is compatible with
filtered colimits it follows for its homotopy modules $$\begin{align*}
\pi_n(L_{\textnormal{lw}}(X)) &\cong \textnormal{colim}_i \, \pi_n(h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}\sigma_i^*(X)) \cong \textnormal{colim}_i \, (h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i})^{\heartsuit} (\pi_n(\sigma_i^*(X))) \\
&\cong \textnormal{colim}_i \, (h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i})^{\heartsuit} \pi_n^{N_i}(X) \cong \textnormal{colim}_i \, \pi_n^{N_i}(X).
\end{align*}$$ Here $(h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i})^{\heartsuit}$ denotes
the restriction of $h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}$ to hearts, it is given
by the ordinary inflation from $(G_i;\pi_0(R))$-modules to discrete
$(G;\pi_0(R))$-modules. We use that $h\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}$ is
t-exact and hence commutes with homotopy objects. In the last step each
$\pi_n^{N_i}(X)$ is viewed as a discrete $(G;\pi_0(R))$-module.\
On the other hand
$X \otimes_{R_G} A_R \simeq \textnormal{colim}_i \, \underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+G/N_i \otimes R, X)$,
and this $R_G$-module is contractible if and only if its underlying
$G$-spectrum is, for which contractibility is equivalent to the
vanishing of all homotopy Mackey functors. For the $n^{\textnormal{th}}$
one, using that the forgetful functor
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$
commutes with colimits, the $N_j$-sections are given by $$\begin{align}
\pi^{N_j}_n(&\textnormal{colim}_i \, \underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+G/N_i, X)) \notag \\
\cong \;&\textnormal{colim}_i \, \textnormal{Hom}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(\Sigma^{\infty +n}_+G/N_j, \underline{\textnormal{map}}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(\Sigma^{\infty}_+G/N_i, X)) \notag \\
\cong \;&\textnormal{colim}_i \, \textnormal{Hom}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(\Sigma^{\infty +n}_+G/N_j \times G/N_i, X) \notag \\
\cong \;&\textnormal{colim}_i \bigoplus_{[N_j\backslash G /N_i]} \pi_n^{N_i\cap N_j}(X).
\label{eq:mackeyintersection}
\end{align}$$ Assume the vanishing of $\pi_n(L_{\textnormal{lw}}(X))$,
so for all $x\in \pi_n^{N_i}(X)$ there is an $N_l \subseteq N_i$ such
that $x$ vanishes in $\pi_n^{N_l}(X)$. Fix $j$. If $y$ is an element of
$\bigoplus_{k \in [N_j\backslash G /N_i]} \; \pi_n^{N_i\cap N_j}(X)$
with (finitely many) components $y_k \in \pi_n^{N_i \cap N_j}(X)$, then
there are $N_{k} \subseteq N_i\cap N_j$ such that $y_k$ vanishes in
$\pi_n^{N_{k}}(X)$, and hence $y$ vanishes when passing to the sections
for the group
$\bigcap_k N_{k} = (\bigcap_k N_{k}) \cap N_j \subseteq N_i \cap N_j$.\
Conversely, assume that
[\[eq:mackeyintersection\]](#eq:mackeyintersection){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:mackeyintersection"} vanishes for all $N_j$, then in
particular it vanishes for $N_j=G$. Then
[\[eq:mackeyintersection\]](#eq:mackeyintersection){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:mackeyintersection"} reads
$\textnormal{colim}_i \, \pi_n^{N_i}(X)$, which is precisely
$\pi_n(L_{\textnormal{lw}}(X))$. Using this argument for all $n$ shows
that (3) and (5) are equivalent, concluding the proof. ◻
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *If
$X \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ is compact,
then it is inflated from some $G_i$, i.e.
$X \simeq \textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}(X')$. Then
$X \otimes_{R_G} A_R \simeq 0$ is equivalent to
$\textnormal{res}^{G_i}_1(X') \simeq 0$. However, if we write a general
$X \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ as a
filtered colimit of compacts $X_j$, then $X \otimes_{R_G} A_R \simeq 0$
need not imply $X_j \otimes A_R \simeq 0$ for any $j$.*
:::

We can now extend [1](#defi:borelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="defi:borelcomplete"} to the profinite case.

::: defi
**Definition 1**. *We define the $\infty$-category of *Borel complete
$R_G$-modules*
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$$
as the full subcategory of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ on the
$A_R$-complete $R_G$-modules. That is, an $R_G$-module $X$ is Borel
complete if for all
$Y \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ with
$Y \otimes_{R_G} A_R \simeq 0$ the mapping space
$\textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(Y,X)$
is contractible.*
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *As in the finite group case, the general theory of
[@mnn17 §2.2] ensures that
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
is presentable and that its inclusion into
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ has a left adjoint
$L_{G;R}$ we call *Borel completion*. There is a unique symmetric
monoidal structure on
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
that makes $L_{G;R}$ symmetric monoidal.\
However, a large proportion of the theory does not carry over, since
there is no reason for the algebra object $A_R$ that we defined as an
infinite colimit to be dualisable.[^8] In particular,
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
is not necessarily the right orthogonal to $A_R^{-1}$-local objects as
defined in [@mnn17 Definition 3.10], and we do not have nice
descriptions of the completion functor or the $\infty$-category
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
as a functor category.*
:::

Nevertheless, we can show a generalisation of
[1](#cor:completionunderlying){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:completionunderlying"} via an argument that always holds
for complete objects in module categories in the rigidly-compactly
generated setting.

::: {#lem:completionunderlyingprofinite .lem}
**Lemma 1**. *An $R_G$-module $X$ is Borel complete if and only if its
underlying $G$-spectrum $U_{R_G}(X) \in \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$ is Borel
complete.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* For ease of notation, we write $U=U_{R_G}$ and $F=F_{R_G}$. One
implication is as in
[1](#rem:completeunderlyingeasydirection){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:completeunderlyingeasydirection"}: Assume that the
$R_G$-module $X$ is $A_R$-complete and let
$Y \in \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$ satisfy
$Y \otimes A_{\mathbb{S}} \simeq 0$. Then
$F(Y) \otimes_R A_R \simeq F(Y) \otimes_R F(A_{\mathbb{S}}) \simeq F(Y \otimes A_{\mathbb{S}}) \simeq 0$
and
$$\textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G}(Y,U(X)) \simeq \textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(F(Y),X) \simeq 0,$$
hence $U(X)$ is $A_{\mathbb{S}}$-complete. For the other direction, note
that by [@mnn17 §2.2] a map $f:U(X) \to M$ of $G$-spectra exhibits $M$
as the $A_{\mathbb{S}}$-completion of $U(X)$ if and only if the
following two conditions are satisfied:

1.  The map $f$ becomes an equivalence after tensoring with
    $A_{\mathbb{S}}$.

2.  The $G$-spectrum $M$ is $A_{\mathbb{S}}$-complete.

Let $c: X \to \iota L_{G;R_G}(X)$ be the $A_R$-completion map for $X$.
From the first part it follows that $U(\iota L_{G;R_G}(X))$ is
$A_{\mathbb{S}}$-complete. Using the projection formula for the
free-forgetful adjunction the map $U(c) \otimes A_{\mathbb{S}}$
identifies with
$$U(c \otimes_R A_R): U(X \otimes_R A_R) \to U(\iota L_{G;R_G}(X) \otimes_R A_R),$$
which is an equivalence since $c \otimes_R A_R$ is one. Hence
$U(c): U(X) \to U\iota L_{G;R_G}(X)$ exhibits $U\iota L_{G;R_G}(X)$ as
the $A_{\mathbb{S}}$-completion of $U(X)$. Now if $U(X)$ is
$A_{\mathbb{S}}$-complete then this map is an equivalence, and since $U$
is conservative $c$ is an equivalence, which means that $X$ is
$A_R$-complete, as desired. ◻
:::

We finally connect the $\infty$-category of Borel completes we just
defined to the previously defined $\infty$-category of levelwise Borel
complete modules.

::: {#thm:borelandhyperlewelwise .thm}
**Theorem 1**. *There is an inclusion
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}} \hookrightarrow \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$
which induces a symmetric monoidal equivalence
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}} \simeq \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)^h_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}.$$*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* We show that both categories agree as full subcategories of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$. Let
$X \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$,
we first show that it is levelwise Borel complete. This is as in
[1](#lem:categoricalfixedpointsborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:categoricalfixedpointsborelcomplete"} - $X$ defines a
compatible family $(X^{N_i})_{i \in I}$ for which we show that every
component is Borel complete. So for $i \in I$ let
$Y \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})$ such
that $\textnormal{res}^{G_i}_1(Y) \simeq 0$. Then
$\textnormal{res}^G_1\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}(Y) \simeq \textnormal{res}^{G_i}_1(Y) \simeq 0$
and hence $\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}(Y) \otimes_{R_G} A_R \simeq 0$ by
[1](#prop:noneqcontractibleprofinite){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:noneqcontractibleprofinite"}. Then
$$\textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})}(Y,X^{N_i}) \simeq \textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}(Y),X) \simeq 0,$$
so
$X^{N_i} \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})$
is Borel complete and $X$ is levelwise Borel. We hence have an inclusion
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}} \subseteq \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}.$$
Now, by definition
$X \in \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$
is hypercomplete if and only if the mapping space
$\textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}}(Z,X)$
is contractible for all acyclic $Z$, by
[1](#prop:noneqcontractibleprofinite){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:noneqcontractibleprofinite"} equivalently for all $Z$ of
the form $L_{\textnormal{lw}}(Y)$ with $Y \otimes_{R_G} A_R \simeq 0$
(using that the levelwise completion is essentially surjective). By
adjunction this holds for $X$ if and only if
$\textnormal{Map}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(Y,X)$
is contractible for all $R_G$-modules $Y$ for which
$Y \otimes_{R_G} A_R \simeq 0$ holds, which precisely means that $X$ is
Borel complete. This shows that $X$ is in
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
if and only if it is hypercomplete, and hence the two
$\infty$-categories in the statement agree as full subcategories of
$R_G$-modules. Since they are both symmetric monoidal localisations of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ it follows from
the universal property of symmetric monoidal localisations [@hin16
Proposition 3.2.2] that the induced equivalence functor is symmetric
monoidal. ◻
:::

We will see that under assumptions on $G$ and $R$ the $\infty$-category
of levelwise Borel complete $R_G$-modules is already hypercomplete, and
hence in this case the two notions of Borel completeness we considered
agree. We will discuss this in the next section.

## Sheaves and representations

In [@cm21 §4.1], Clausen and Mathew consider the classifying topos for a
profinite group and prove various results about hypercompleteness for
sheaves of spectra on the latter. The purpose of this section is to
connect the previous results on Borel completeness for profinite groups
to the results of Clausen and Mathew. We first recall some generalities.

::: rec
**Recollection 1**. *For a small $\infty$-category $\mathcal{C}$, we
denote by
$\mathcal{P}(C) = \textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Spc}})$
its *presheaf $\infty$-category* and by
$y: \mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow \mathcal{P}(C)$ the Yoneda embedding. If
$(\mathcal{C}, \tau)$ is an $\infty$-site and $W$ is the class of
$\tau$-covering sieves $T \to y(X)$ where $X \in \mathcal{C}$, then a
*$\tau$-sheaf on $\mathcal{C}$* is a $W$-local object in
$\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C})$. The full subcategory
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})$ of
$\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C})$ spanned by such sheaves is the
*$\infty$-category of $\tau$-sheaves on $\mathcal{C}$*. We denote the
left adjoint sheafification functor by
$\textsf{\textup{a}}_{\tau}: \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})$.\
If $W$ instead is the set of maps
$\textnormal{colim}_{[n] \in \Delta} \; y(\mathcal{U}_n) \to y(X)$ for
$\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}$ a hypercover of an object $X$, then an $W$-local
object of $\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C})$ is called a *$\tau$-hypersheaf on
$\mathcal{C}$*. The full subcategory of $\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C})$ they
span is denoted
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{\wedge}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})$. Each
hypersheaf is in particular a sheaf, and we denote the localisation
functor by
$(-)^{\wedge}: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{\wedge}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})$,
the composition with $\textsf{\textup{a}}_{\tau}$ by
$\textsf{\textup{a}}_{\tau}^{\wedge}: \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{\wedge}(\mathcal{C})$.*
:::

Replacing $\textsf{\textup{Spc}}$ by an $\infty$-category with small
limits, we get the following.

::: rec
**Recollection 1**. *Let $(\mathcal{C}, \tau)$ be an $\infty$-site and
let $\mathcal{D}$ an $\infty$-category with small limits. A
*$\tau$-(hyper)sheaf* on $\mathcal{C}$ with values in $\mathcal{D}$ is a
functor $\mathcal{F}: \mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{op}} \to \mathcal{D}$
such that its canonical limit-preserving extension
$\bar{\mathcal{F}}: \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C})^{\textnormal{op}} \to \mathcal{D}$
factors through the opposite of the localisation functor
$\textsf{\textup{a}}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}: \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C})$.
The *$\infty$-category of $\tau$-(hyper)sheaves on $\mathcal{C}$ with
values in $\mathcal{D}$* is the full subcategory
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\mathcal{C}; \mathcal{D})$
of
$\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{D})=\textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{op}},\mathcal{D})$
on $\tau$-(hyper)sheaves
$\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{op}} \to \mathcal{D}$.\
If $\mathcal{D}$ is furthermore presentable, then
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{\wedge}(\mathcal{C}; \mathcal{D})$ is
presentable and there is a left adjoint to the inclusion
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\mathcal{C}; \mathcal{D}) \subseteq \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{D})$
denoted
$\textsf{\textup{a}}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}: \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{D}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{D})$.
If the topology is clear from the context we omit the $\tau$ from the
notation.*
:::

::: {#rem:tstructureonsheaves .rem}
**Remark 1**. *When
$\mathcal{D}= \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$ for a
connective $\mathbb{E}_{\infty}$-ring $R$ we write
$\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C};R)$ and
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\mathcal{C}; R)$ and for
the respective presheaf and (hyper)sheaf $\infty$-categories with values
in $R$-modules. By [@lur18 Proposition 1.3.2.7, 2.1.1.1] there is a
t-structure on $\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; R)$ for
which a sheaf $\mathcal{F}$ is connective if the sheafification of the
presheaf of abelian groups $X \mapsto \pi_n\mathcal{F}(X)$ vanishes for
all $n<0$ and it is coconnective if all its sections are after applying
the forgetful functor
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}$.
This t-structure is right complete and compatible with filtered
colimits.*
:::

::: {#prop:hypersheafhypercomplete .prop}
**Proposition 1**. *A sheaf in
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; R)$ is a hypersheaf if
and only if it is hypercomplete (in the sense of
[1](#def:hypercompletetstructure){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="def:hypercompletetstructure"}).*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* For sheaves of spectra this follows from [@lur09 Lemma
6.5.3.11], [@dhi04; @tv03], [@lur09 Corollary 6.5.3.13] and [@lur18
Proposition 1.3.3.3], as discussed in [@cm21 Example 2.5]. We show that
the case with coefficients reduces to the one of spectral sheaves.\
Since the forgetful functor
$U_R: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}$
preserves and detects limits, [@agv22 Remark 2.3.3] implies that the
induced functor
$U_R: \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C};R) \to \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C};\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
preserves and detects sheaves and hypersheaves. Hence a sheaf in
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; R)$ is a hypersheaf if
and only if it is one after applying $U_R$.\
It remains to show the analogous statement for hypercomplete sheaves. It
follows from the description of the t-structure on
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; R)$ given in [@lur18
§2.1.1] that the functor
$U_R: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; R) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; \textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
is t-exact. Under the identification
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; R) \simeq \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}(\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; \textsf{\textup{Sp}}))$,
where $\underline{R}$ is the sheafification of the constant presheaf
with value $R$, the functor $U_R$ identifies with the forgetful functor
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}(\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; \textsf{\textup{Sp}})) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; \textsf{\textup{Sp}}).$$
It has a (automatically right t-exact) left adjoint we denote $F_R$, cf.
[@lur18 Proposition 2.1.0.3]. Now the argument is analogous to the one
given in
[1](#lem:completionunderlyingprofinite){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:completionunderlyingprofinite"} for complete objects in a
module category.\
Let $\mathcal{F}\in \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; R)$ be
hypercomplete. Then $U_R(\mathcal{F})$ is hypercomplete. Indeed, if
$\mathcal{G}\in \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; \textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
is acyclic then $F_R(\mathcal{G})$ is acyclic since $F_R$ is right
t-exact and the t-structures at play are right complete. We have
$$\textnormal{Map}_{\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; \textsf{\textup{Sp}})}(\mathcal{G},U_R(\mathcal{F})) \simeq \textnormal{Map}_{\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C};R)}(F_R(\mathcal{F}),\mathcal{G}) \simeq 0,$$
and hence $U_R(\mathcal{F})$ is hypercomplete. For the other direction,
a map $f:U_R(\mathcal{F}) \to \mathcal{M}$ of sheaves of spectra
exhibits $\mathcal{M}$ as the hypercompletion of $U_R(\mathcal{F})$ if
and only if the following two conditions are satisfied (cf. [@cm21
Proposition 2.32]):

1.  The map $f$ is a $\pi_{\ast}$-isomorphism.

2.  The sheaf $\mathcal{M}$ is hypercomplete.

Let $c: \mathcal{F}\to \iota(\mathcal{F}^h)$ be the hypercompletion map
for $\mathcal{F}$. From the first part it follows that
$U_R\iota(\mathcal{F}^h)$ is hypercomplete. Since $U_R$ commutes with
homotopy groups as a t-exact functor $U_R(c)$ is a
$\pi_{\ast}$-isomorphism. Hence
$U_R(c): U_R(\mathcal{F}) \to U_R(\iota(\mathcal{F}^h))$ exhibits
$U_R(\iota(\mathcal{F}^h))$ as the hypercompletion of
$U_R(\mathcal{F})$. Now if $U_R(\mathcal{F})$ is hypercomplete then this
map is an equivalence, and since $U_R$ is conservative $c$ is an
equivalence, which means that $\mathcal{F}$ is hypercomplete, which
concludes the proof. ◻
:::

::: nota
**Notation 1**. *For a profinite group $G$, we denote by
$G\textsf{\textup{-set}}$ the category of finite continuous $G$-sets
endowed with the discrete topology and $G$-equivariant maps. It becomes
a site by declaring covering sieves to be families of jointly surjective
maps.*
:::

When $G$ is finite, the sheaf category with ring spectrum coefficients
we obtain for this site has a familiar description.

::: {#lem:sheavesfunbg .lem}
**Lemma 1**. *Let $R \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$ be
connective and let $G$ be a finite group. Then there is a t-exact
equivalence
$$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R) \simeq \textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})).$$*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* This is [@cm21 Example 4.4], we just need to show that the
equivalence is t-exact. Let
$\alpha: BG \to G\textsf{\textup{-set}}^\textnormal{op}$ be the functor
that picks out the free $G$-set $G/1$. Then restriction along $\alpha$
and right Kan extension is an adjoint equivalence.
$$\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=30, row sep=30]
    & {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)} \\
    {\textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))} & {\mathcal{P}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)}
    \arrow["{\alpha^*}", shift right=3, shorten <=10pt, from=1-2, to=2-1]
    \arrow[""{name=0, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "\iota", shift left=2, hook', from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow["{\alpha_*}", shift left=5, shorten >=10pt, from=2-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["\sim"{marking, allow upside down}, shift left=4, draw=none, from=2-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[""{name=3, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\textsf{\textup{a}}}", shift left=2, from=2-2, to=1-2]
    \arrow[""{name=4, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\hat{\alpha}^*}"', from=2-2, to=2-1]
\end{tikzcd}$$ Here $\textsf{\textup{a}}$ is the sheafification functor
and $\hat{\alpha}^*$ is the restriction along $\alpha$ on presheaves. We
have $\alpha^* \simeq \hat{\alpha}^*\iota$, and hence
$\alpha_* \simeq \textsf{\textup{a}}\hat{\alpha}_!$ by passing to left
adjoints, where $\hat{\alpha}_!$ is the left adjoint of
$\hat{\alpha}^*$. Here we use that $\alpha_*$ is both left and right
adjoint to $\alpha^*$ since they are mutually inverse equivalences.
Since $\hat{\alpha}^*$ is t-exact, $\hat{\alpha}_!$ is right t-exact,
and since the sheafification $\textsf{\textup{a}}$ is t-exact it follows
that $\alpha_*$ is right t-exact.\
On the other hand, the right adjoint $\hat{\alpha}_*$ of
$\hat{\alpha}^*$ already lands in sheaves, and hence $\alpha_*$ factors
as $\textsf{\textup{a}}\hat{\alpha}_*$. Again since $\hat{\alpha}^*$ is
t-exact, $\hat{\alpha}_*$ is left t-exact, and hence $\alpha_*$ is left
t-exact as well, hence t-exact. ◻
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *It follows from [@cm21 Lemma 4.3] that the right Kan
extension $\alpha_*\mathcal{F}$ of a functor
$\mathcal{F}: BG \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$ to
$G\textsf{\textup{-set}}^\textnormal{op}$ sends the $G$-set $G/H$ to the
$H$-homotopy fixed points $\mathcal{F}(\ast_{BG})^{hH}$.*
:::

Together with the observation that for a profinite group $G$, the site
$G\textsf{\textup{-set}}$ is a filtered colimit of the sites associated
to the finite quotients of $G$, we get:

::: {#prop:sheavesborelcomplete .prop}
**Proposition 1**. *Let $R \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
be connective and let $G= \textnormal{lim}_i \, G_i$ be a profinite
group. Then the inclusion of hypercomplete objects gives a commutative
diagram $$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{\wedge}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}} \\
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}}
    \arrow["\sim", from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[hook, from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow[hook, from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow["\sim", from=2-1, to=2-2]
\end{tikzcd}$$ in which both horizontal functors are symmetric monoidal
equivalences.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* We first consider the lower horizontal functor. By [@cm21
Construction 4.5] the site $G\textsf{\textup{-set}}$ decomposes into a
filtered colimit of the $G_i\textsf{\textup{-set}}$ along the canonical
maps $G_j\textsf{\textup{-set}} \to G_i\textsf{\textup{-set}}$ that
inflate the $G_j$-action to a $G_i$-action along
$G_i \twoheadrightarrow G_j$ whenever $N_i \subseteq N_j$. Then the
sheaf category $\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)$
can be written as the cofiltered
$\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{R}}$-limit
$$\textnormal{lim}_i \; \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G_i\textsf{\textup{-set}};R) \stackrel{\ref{lem:sheavesfunbg}}{\simeq} \textnormal{lim}_i \; \textnormal{Fun}(BG_i,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))$$
with transition maps
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G_i\textsf{\textup{-set}};R) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G_j\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)$
given by restriction along the above functors of sites, cf. [@cm21
§3.1]. In terms of the right-hand side, these are the maps
$(-)^{h(N_j/N_i)}$. Passing to left adjoints, this is precisely the
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}})$-colimit that
defines
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$.
By [@lur18 Remark 1.3.2.8] the transition functors in the colimit
diagram for $\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)$ are
t-exact, hence the t-structure on the latter is the filtered colimit of
the t-structures on the
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G_i\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)$, as in
[1](#prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete"}. Since each of the
equivalences coming from
[1](#lem:sheavesfunbg){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:sheavesfunbg"} is t-exact it follows that the equivalence
$$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R) \simeq \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$$
is t-exact. Now the top horizontal equivalence follows from
[1](#thm:borelandhyperlewelwise){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:borelandhyperlewelwise"} and
[1](#prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete"} by passing to
hypercomplete objects. ◻
:::

In [@cm21 §4.1], it is studied when hypercompletion on
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
is smashing, and criteria for sheaves of spectra to be hypercomplete in
terms of weakly nilpotent group actions are given. Using this we can
give assumptions under which the vertical functors in
[1](#prop:sheavesborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:sheavesborelcomplete"} are actually equivalences. We
denote by $R^s$ the sheafification of the constant presheaf of spectra
with value $R$.

::: {#prop:smashinghypercompletion .prop}
**Proposition 1** (Clausen--Mathew). *Under each of the following
assumptions the two vertical functors in
[1](#prop:sheavesborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:sheavesborelcomplete"} are equivalences:*

1.  *The virtual cohomological dimension of $G$ is finite and $R^s$ is
    hypercomplete. (The latter in particular holds when the ring
    spectrum $R$ is truncated.)*

2.  *The group $G$ has finite cohomological dimension $d$ and $R^s$
    satisfies either of the two (equivalent) conditions:*

    1.  *For every normal containment $N \unlhd H$ of open subgroups the
        spectrum with $H/N$-action $R^s(G/N)$ is d-nilpotent.*

    2.  *There is a $d' \geq 0$ such that for every open normal subgroup
        $N \unlhd G$ the spectrum with $G/N$-action $R^s(G/N)$ is
        $d'$-nilpotent.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* Part (1) is [@cm21 Corollary 4.28], see also [@cm21 Corollary
4.29] for the statement about truncatedness. The second assertion
follows from the first and [@cm21 Theorem 4.26]. ◻
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *Loosely speaking, the condition of $d$-nilpotency for a
spectrum $X$ with an action of a finite group $G$ means that $X$ can be
built through retracts and extensions from an induced spectrum
$\oplus_G Y$ in $d+1$ many steps. For the precise definition we refer to
[@cm21 Definition 4.18], see also [@mnn17; @mnn19]. The statement of
[@cm21 Lemma 4.20] gives a characterisation in terms of the $d$-skeleton
of the universal space for free $G$-actions $EG$.*
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *When $G$ is finite, the colimit diagram computing the
levelwise Borel complete category has a terminal object and
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}} \simeq \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}.$$
Note also that the t-structure on
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$ is left complete, and
hence the functor category
$\textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))$ is
already hypercomplete. This is consistent with part (1) of
[1](#prop:smashinghypercompletion){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:smashinghypercompletion"}, as in this case $R^s$ is
hypercomplete since every object of
$\textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))$ is
Postnikov-complete (that is, equivalent to the limit of its Postnikov
tower).*
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *Under the assumption that the virtual cohomological
dimension of $G$ is finite, Clausen and Mathew also show that
hypercompletion on
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)$ (for general
connective $R \in \textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$) is smashing
and agrees with Postnikov completion [@cm21 Corollary 4.28, Proposition
2.10]. In view of
[1](#prop:sheavesborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:sheavesborelcomplete"} this means that in this case the
hypercompletion
$$L^h: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}} \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}$$
is computed as the Postnikov completion
$X \mapsto \lim_{n} \tau_{\leq n} X \simeq X \otimes \lim_n \tau_{\leq n} R^s$.*
:::

When $R$ is a discrete commutative ring, we can give another description
of the sheaf categories in
[1](#prop:sheavesborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:sheavesborelcomplete"}. As before, we write
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R)$ for the (Grothendieck abelian) category of
discrete $(G;R)$-modules, where $G$ is a profinite group, see e.g.
[@bg23 §2]. Its derived $\infty$-category
$\mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))$ carries a natural t-structure
[@lur17 Proposition 1.3.5.21], and although this t-structure is
generally not left complete, $\mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))$
is hypercomplete, since it has no nonzero acyclics.

::: {#prop:sheavesgrmodules .prop}
**Proposition 1**. *Let $R$ be a discrete commutative ring and let
$G= \textnormal{lim}_i \, G_i$ be a profinite group. Then the inclusion
of hypercomplete objects gives a commutative diagram $$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{\wedge}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)} & {\mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))} \\
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)} & {\textnormal{colim}_i \; \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R))}
    \arrow["\sim", from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[hook, from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow[hook, from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow["\sim", from=2-1, to=2-2]
\end{tikzcd}$$ in which both horizontal functors are symmetric monoidal
equivalences.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* Again consider the colimit decomposition of
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)$ from [@cm21
Construction 4.5]. For each $i \in I$, we have
$$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G_i\textsf{\textup{-set}};R) \simeq \textnormal{Fun}(BG_i,\mathcal{D}(R)) \simeq \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R)),$$
where the second equivalence follows from the identification of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R)$ with modules over the group algebra
$R[G_i]$ and the Schwede--Shipley theorem [@lur17 Theorem 7.1.2.1]. This
equivalence is t-exact.\
The functor
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G_j\textsf{\textup{-set}};R) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G_i\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)$
that is left adjoint to the restriction along the inflation map
$G_j\textsf{\textup{-set}} \to G_i\textsf{\textup{-set}}$ for
$N_i \subseteq N_j$ corresponds to the functor on derived categories
induced by
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_j;R) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R)$, the
exact functor that inflates a $G_j$-action to a $G_i$-action along
$G_i \twoheadrightarrow G_j$. In terms of group algebras, this is a
restriction of scalars along the canonical map $R[G_i] \to R[G_j]$. We
hence obtain an equivalence
$$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R) \simeq \textnormal{colim}_i \; \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R)),$$
where the colimit is taken in
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}_{\textnormal{st}})$
along the above maps. Using the same argument as in
[1](#prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:tstructureonlevelwiseborelcomplete"} we obtain a
t-structure on
$\textnormal{colim}_i \; \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R))$ that
is compatible with filtered colimits and has
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R)$ as a heart. It makes the above equivalence
t-exact. It remains to show that
$\mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))$ identifies with the full
subcategory of hypercomplete objects of the above colimit.\
The inflation functors
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R)$ are exact
and provide t-exact functors
$\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i}: \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R)) \to \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))$.
Then there is an induced symmetric monoidal left adjoint
$$\alpha: \textnormal{colim}_i \; \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R)) \to \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R)).$$
Write
$\alpha_i: \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R)) \to \textnormal{colim}_i \; \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R))$
for the canonical map and $\beta_i$ for its right adjoint. Let
$M \in \textnormal{colim}_i \; \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R))$.
Then [@lur09 Lemma 6.3.3.6] implies that
$M \simeq \textnormal{colim}_i \; \alpha_i \beta_i(M)$ and
$\alpha(M) \simeq \textnormal{colim}_i \; \textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i} \beta_i(M)$,
so
$$\pi_*(\alpha(M)) \cong \textnormal{colim}_i (\textnormal{infl}^G_{G_i})^\heartsuit \pi_*(\beta_i(M)) \cong \pi_*(M).$$
In particular, $\alpha$ is t-exact. Since the t-structure on
$\mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))$ is hypercomplete, we have that
$$\alpha(M) \simeq 0 \Leftrightarrow \pi_*(\alpha(M)) \cong 0 \Leftrightarrow \pi_*(M) \cong 0.$$
This means that the kernel of $\alpha$ precisely consists of the acyclic
objects, and we need to show that the right adjoint of $\alpha$ is fully
faithful. Denote by
$\widecheck{\mathcal{D}}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))$ the unseparated
derived $\infty$-category of [@lur18 §C.5.8]. There is a canonical
localisation functor that quotients by the acyclic complexes
$$\tilde{\alpha}: \widecheck{\mathcal{D}}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R)) \twoheadrightarrow  \widecheck{\mathcal{D}}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))/\widecheck{\mathcal{D}}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))_{\textnormal{ac}} \simeq \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R)).$$
Its right adjoint is fully faithful and identifies the target with the
hypercomplete objects of the source. Combining the universal property of
the prestable part of the unseparated derived $\infty$-category [@lur18
Corollary C.5.8.9] with [@lur18 Proposition C.3.1.1, C.3.2.1] we see
that $\tilde{\alpha}$ factors as $\alpha \circ \check{\alpha}$. Here
$$\check{\alpha}: \widecheck{\mathcal{D}}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R)) \to \textnormal{colim}_i \; \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R))$$
is the unique t-exact left adjoint that is given by the identity on
hearts. Note that $\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R)$ is itself the filtered
colimit of the categories $\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R)$ along the left
exact left adjoint inflation functors
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_j;R) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R)$ induced
by the quotient maps $G_i \twoheadrightarrow G_j$. So by [@lur18
Proposition C.5.5.20, C.3.3.5] we can also write
$\widecheck{\mathcal{D}}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))$ as a
$\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}$-colimit of the
$\widecheck{\mathcal{D}}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R))$, and the right
adjoint of $\check{\alpha}$ is a
$\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{R}}$-limit of fully faithful functors
$$\mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R)) \hookrightarrow \widecheck{\mathcal{D}}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R)).$$
So the right adjoints of $\check{\alpha}$ and $\tilde{\alpha}$ are fully
faithful, and hence the right adjoint of $\alpha$ must be fully faithful
as well. This finishes the proof. ◻
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *Recall the classical equivalence
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R)\simeq \textnormal{Sh}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)$,
where the latter is the 1-category of sheaves of (discrete) $R$-modules
on $G$-sets, and $G$ again is a profinite group. Then there is a fully
faithful embedding
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R)\simeq \textnormal{Sh}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R) \hookrightarrow \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R),$$
and by [@lur18 Corollary 2.1.2.3] it extends to a fully faithful functor
$$\mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R)) \hookrightarrow \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}(G\textsf{\textup{-set}};R)$$
which has essential image given by hypersheaves. This provides an
alternative approach to the previous proposition.*
:::

In this setting,
[1](#prop:smashinghypercompletion){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:smashinghypercompletion"} implies the following.

::: {#cor:smashinghypercompletionreps .cor}
**Corollary 1**. *If the virtual cohomological dimension of the
profinite group $G$ is finite, the vertical functors in
[1](#prop:sheavesgrmodules){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:sheavesgrmodules"} are equivalences.*
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *By definition the unseparated derived $\infty$-category
$\widecheck{\mathcal{D}}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))$ of [@lur18
Corollary C.5.8.9] agrees with Krause's homotopy category of injectives
$$\mathcal{K}(\textnormal{Inj} \; \textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))$$ of
[@kra05; @kra15]. It generally differs from the filtered colimit of
derived categories we considered above, this is already the case for
finite groups.\
When $G$ is finite, and when the coefficient ring $R$ is assumed regular
and Noetherian, then a theorem of Mathew shows that
$\mathcal{K}(\textnormal{Inj} \; \textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))$ coincides
with the category of modules over the Borel completion of $R_G$ in
$G$-spectra, which in this case also agrees with
$\textnormal{Ind}\,\textnormal{Fun}(BG,\textsf{\textup{Perf}}(R))$, see
[@bar21 Theorem 3.7], [@tre15 Theorem A.4]. We refer to [@bchnl25 Remark
3.35] for further discussion.*
:::

## Smooth Artin motives

We now start with the geometric part of the paper, in which we will
consider $\infty$-categories of smooth Artin motives for the Nisnevich
and the étale topology. They are constructed using the theory of finite
correspondences [@cd19 §9]. For versions of (smooth) Artin motives for
the étale topology without correspondences we refer the reader to
[@az12], [@rui25a; @rui25b].\
We will work over a base scheme $S$ that we assume to be connected,
noetherian, and of finite Krull dimension.[^9] All morphisms of schemes
are assumed to be separated and of finite type. In particular, all
schemes considered are noetherian. Throughout, $R$ is a discrete
commutative ring.

::: defi
**Definition 1**. *Let $\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S$ be the category of
smooth $S$-schemes with morphisms given by morphisms of $S$-schemes, and
let $\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S$ be the full subcategory on finite étale
$S$-schemes. The fibre product over $S$ endows both
$\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S$ and $\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S$ with a symmetric
monoidal structure which commutes with coproducts in both variables.*
:::

::: {#rec:cycles .rec}
**Recollection 1** ([@cd19 §8.1.a]). *For a scheme $X$, a *cycle with
domain $X$* is a $\mathbb{Z}$-linear combination of points of $X$. We
write
$$\langle X \rangle \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=\sum_{x \in X^{(0)}} \textnormal{lg}(\mathcal{O}_{X,x}).x$$ for the
*cycle associated to $X$*.[^10] The sum runs over the generic
points[^11] of $X$. When $Z$ is a closed subscheme of a scheme $X$, the
cycle $\langle Z \rangle$ is understood as a cycle with domain $X$. If
$f: X \to Y$ is a morphism of schemes, the *pushforward* of a cycle
$\alpha=\sum_{i \in I} n_ix_i$ with domain $X$ by $f$ is the cycle with
domain $Y$ given by $$f_*(\alpha) = \sum_{i \in I} n_id_i.f(x_i),$$
where $d_i$ is the degree of the extension $\kappa(x_i)/\kappa(f(x_i))$
if it is finite and $0$ otherwise.*
:::

::: {#defi:correspondence .defi}
**Definition 1**. *Let $X$ and $Y$ be smooth $S$-schemes. Then
$\textsf{\textup{Cor}}_S(X,Y)$, the group of *finite $S$-correspondences
from $X$ to $Y$*, is the abelian group consisting of the finite and
universally integral cycles over $S$ with domain $X \times_S Y$.\
Using each of the tensor products
$\textsf{\textup{Cor}}_S(X,Y) \otimes R$ as hom-sets defines an additive
category $\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R)$ which has objects the smooth
$S$-schemes and morphisms the finite $S$-correspondences with
coefficients in $R$. Composition is defined using pushforward and
intersection of cycles, and the identity of a smooth $S$-scheme $X$ is
given by the cycle $\langle \Delta_X \rangle$, where
$\Delta_X \subseteq X\times_S X$ is the diagonal [@cd19 §9.1].\
By $\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$ we denote the full subcategory of
$\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R)$ spanned by the finite étale $S$-schemes.
Finite direct sums are given by the disjoint union of schemes, and the
fibre product over $S$ endows both categories with an additively
symmetric monoidal structure [@cd19 §9.2].*
:::

::: {#rem:nicecorrespondences .rem}
**Remark 1**. *The definition given above is the most general one taken
from [@cd19 §9.1], which goes back to [@sv00 §3], [@mvw06 Appendix 1A],
and we will not need it in its full detail. We want to highlight three
special cases of which the third one will be of importance later:*

1.  *If the base scheme $S$ is regular, then
    $\textsf{\textup{Cor}}_S(X,Y)$ is the free abelian group on integral
    closed subschemes $Z \subseteq X \times_S Y$ which are finite and
    equidimensional over $X$ (that is, they are finite over $X$ and
    dominate an irreducible component of $X$), see [@cd19 Remark 8.3.29,
    9.1.3].*

2.  *Assume that $X$ is smooth and $Y$ étale over $S$, write
    $p_1: X \times_S Y \to X$ for the canonical (étale) morphism. Then
    by [@cd19 Lemma 10.2.6] the group $\textsf{\textup{Cor}}_S(X,Y)$ is
    free abelian on the set of connected components $U$ of
    $X \times_S Y$ such that $p_1(U)$ is equal to a connected component
    of $X$.*

3.  *From (2) it follows that if $X$ is smooth and $Y$ finite étale over
    $S$, then $\textsf{\textup{Cor}}_S(X,Y)$ is the free abelian group
    on the connected components $X \times_S Y$, cf. [@cd19 Proposition
    10.2.5].*

*The apparent discrepancy between (1) and (2) can be explained as
follows: every connected scheme $X$ that is smooth over a regular and
hence normal scheme $S$ is already itself normal, and since it is
assumed noetherian it is integral.*
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *In [@cd19 §9] finite correspondences with coefficients in
a ring $R$ are defined by means of $\Lambda$-universal cycles, where
$\Lambda \subseteq \mathbb{Q}$ is the localisation of $\mathbb{Z}$ at
the integers that are invertible in $R$. By [@cd19 Remark 9.1.3]
tensoring the resulting correspondence groups over $\Lambda$ with $R$
results in the hom-modules of $\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R)$ defined
above.*
:::

::: {#rem:isoetalecorrespondences .rem}
**Remark 1**. *The identification made in (2) of
[1](#rem:nicecorrespondences){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:nicecorrespondences"} poses a subtlety: it identifies
connected components with the sum of their generic points. For example,
the identity of $S$ in $\textsf{\textup{Cor}}_S(S,S)$ is given by
$\langle \Delta_S \rangle$, but if $S$ is only connected but not
irreducible $\Delta_S \cong S$ can have multiple irreducible components.
Under the identification made in (2) the cycle
$\langle \Delta_S \rangle$ corresponds to the unique connected component
of $S \times_S S$. In fact, the proof of [@cd19 Lemma 10.2.6] shows that
for $X$ a smooth and $Y$ an étale $S$-scheme the map
$$\mathbb{Z}[\pi_0^{\textnormal{fin}}(X \times_S Y/X)] \to \textsf{\textup{Cor}}_S(X,Y), \; U \mapsto \langle U \rangle = \sum_{u \in U^{(0)}} \textnormal{lg}(\mathcal{O}_{U,u}).u$$
is an isomorphism, where $\pi_0^{\textnormal{fin}}(X \times_S Y/X)$ is
the set of connected components $U$ of $X \times_S Y$ such that the
projection $p_1(U)$ is equal to a connected component of $X$. When $Y$
is finite étale over $S$ the condition on the projection can be
dropped.*
:::

::: {#rec:graphfunctors .rec}
**Recollection 1**. *Let $f: X \to Y$ be a morphism of smooth
$S$-schemes. Then its graph $\Gamma_f$ is a closed subscheme of
$X \times_S Y$, and by [@cd19 Example 9.1.4] the cycle
$\langle \Gamma_f \rangle$ with domain $X \times_S Y$ defines an element
of $\textsf{\textup{Cor}}_S(X,Y)$. This construction gives faithful
functors
$\gamma: \textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S\to \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R)$ and
$\gamma: \textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\to \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$ which
are the identity on objects and take a morphism of $S$-schemes
$f: X \to Y$ to the cycle $\langle \Gamma_f \rangle$. They are symmetric
monoidal [@cd19 9.2.4].*
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *Using the description of correspondence groups given in
[1](#rem:nicecorrespondences){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:nicecorrespondences"} it follows that all objects of
$\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$ are self-dual with respect to the
symmetric monoidal structure of
[1](#defi:correspondence){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="defi:correspondence"}.*
:::

::: nota
**Notation 1**. *In what follows, $\tau$ will denote either the étale or
the Nisnevich topology on $\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S$ or a full subcategory
thereof. For the former, covering families are finite families
$(X_i \to X)_{i \in I}$ of étale morphisms that are jointly surjective,
and for the latter we furthermore demand that for every $x \in X$ there
exists $i \in I$ and $x_i \in X_i$ mapping to $x$ such that the induced
map of residue fields $\kappa(x) \to \kappa(x_i)$ is an isomorphism.\
For $\mathcal{C}$ an additive $\infty$-category (which in our case will
be a correspondence category) we will write
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C};\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$ for the
$\infty$-category of additive presheaves on $\mathcal{C}$ with values in
spectra. We refer to Appendix
[6](#sec:sheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref"
reference="sec:sheaveswithtransfers"} for some recollections.*
:::

::: {#defi:sheaveswithtransfers .defi}
**Definition 1**. *The objects of
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
are called *presheaves with transfers*. We define the $\infty$-category
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
of *$\tau$-(hyper)sheaves with transfers* as the pullback
$$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} \\
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}}),}
    \arrow[from=1-1, to=1-2, "{\textsf{\textup{o}}^{(\wedge),\textnormal{tr}}_{\tau}}", hook]
    \arrow[from=1-1, to=2-1, "\gamma^*"]
    \arrow[from=1-2, to=2-2, "\hat{\gamma}^*"]
    \arrow[from=2-1, to=2-2, "{\textsf{\textup{o}}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}}", hook]
    \arrow["\usebox\pullback"{anchor=center, pos=0.125}, draw=none, from=1-1, to=2-2]
\end{tikzcd}$$ where the horizontal functors are the canonical
inclusions and vertical functors precompose with the graph functor
$\gamma: \textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S\to \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R)$. When
$\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S$ is replaced by $\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S$ we
denote the resulting sheaf category by
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$.*
:::

Note that in the above pullback we use that sheaves for both topologies
already preserve finite products, see e.g. [@lur18 Proposition A.3.3.1].

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *Under our assumptions on the base scheme $S$ the
Nisnevich topos
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S)$
is hypercomplete, which follows from [@lur18 Theorem 3.7.7.1] or [@cm21
Theorem 3.18] using the argument given in [@mat24 Lemma 5.1].
Furthermore, by [@lur18 Proposition 1.3.3.3] a sheaf of spectra is a
hypersheaf if and only if the underlying sheaf of spaces is one, so we
do not need to distinguish between sheaves and hypersheaves for the
Nisnevich topology.*
:::

::: {#rem:tstructuresheaveswithtransfers .rem}
**Remark 1**. *In the étale case, we can express hypersheaves with
transfers as the iterated pullback $$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{\wedge}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} \\
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{\wedge}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).}
    \arrow["{\textsf{\textup{o}}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\wedge,\textnormal{tr}}}", hook, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["{\gamma^*}", from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow["{\textsf{\textup{o}}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\textnormal{tr}}}", hook, from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow["{\gamma^*}", from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow["{\hat{\gamma}^*}", from=1-3, to=2-3]
    \arrow["{\textsf{\textup{o}}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\wedge}}", hook, from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["{\textsf{\textup{o}}_{\textnormal{ét}}}", hook, from=2-2, to=2-3]
    \arrow["\usebox\pullback"{anchor=center, pos=0.125}, draw=none, from=1-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["\usebox\pullback"{anchor=center, pos=0.125}, draw=none, from=1-2, to=2-3]
\end{tikzcd}$$ Recall from
[1](#prop:hypersheafhypercomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:hypersheafhypercomplete"} that hypercomplete sheaves are
precisely the hypercomplete objects in sheaves. There is a right
complete t-structure on sheaves with transfers which makes the functor
$\gamma^*$ to sheaves t-exact (see
[1](#rem:tstrucuresonsheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:tstrucuresonsheaveswithtransfers"}), and hypersheaves
with transfers are precisely the hypercomplete objects with respect to
this t-structure, by essentially the same argument as in
[1](#prop:hypersheafhypercomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:hypersheafhypercomplete"}.\
In view of [1](#rem:pushoutinsteadofpullback){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:pushoutinsteadofpullback"} all the functors in the
diagram above have left adjoints, and in particular hypercompletion on
étale sheaves induces a left adjoint on étale sheaves with transfers
$$(-)^\wedge_\textnormal{ét}: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^\wedge(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).$$*
:::

::: {#rem:representables .rem}
**Remark 1**. *For a smooth (resp. finite étale) $S$-scheme $X$ the
presheaf with transfers
$$R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S(X) \mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=\bar{y}(X) \simeq \textsf{\textup{Cor}}_S(-,X) \otimes R$$ is an étale
hypersheaf with transfers, where
$\bar{y}: \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R) \hookrightarrow \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
(resp.
$\bar{y}: \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R) \hookrightarrow \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$)
is the stable Yoneda embedding of
[1](#rec:psigma){reference-type="ref+Label" reference="rec:psigma"},
which follows from [@cd16 Proposition 2.1.4], see also [@bh21 Remark
14.6]. This gives an induced symmetric monoidal functor
$$R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S: \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{\wedge}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}),$$
and replacing $\textsf{\textup{Sm}}$ by $\textsf{\textup{Fét}}$ gives
its analogue for finite étale schemes.*
:::

::: {#defi:motives .defi}
**Definition 1**. *The $\infty$-category
$\mathcal{DM}^{\textnormal{eff},(\wedge)}_{\tau}(S;R)$ of *effective
$\tau$-motives* is the full subcategory of
