$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
on the $\mathcal{S}$-local objects, where $\mathcal{S}$ is the
collection of morphisms
$R^\textnormal{tr}_S(\mathbb{A}^1_X) \to R^\textnormal{tr}_S(X)$ for
$X \in \textsf{\textup{Sm}}_{S}$. We denote the compact part
$\mathcal{DM}^{\textnormal{eff},(\wedge)}_{\tau}(S;R)^{\omega}$ by
$\mathcal{DM}^{\textnormal{eff,gm}, (\wedge)}_{\tau}(S;R)$, its objects
are called *geometric* motives.*
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *By [@lur09 Proposition 5.5.4.15] the $\infty$-category
$\mathcal{DM}^{\textnormal{eff},(\wedge)}_{\tau}(S;R)$ is presentable
and a localisation of
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$.
We could also define it as a further pullback along the inclusion of
$\mathbb{A}^1$-invariant sheaves with transfers into the latter. We
write
$$L_{\mathbb{A}^1}: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{DM}^{\textnormal{eff},(\wedge)}_{\tau}(S;R)$$
for the left adjoint localisation functor. By
[1](#rem:pushoutinsteadofpullback){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:pushoutinsteadofpullback"},
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
has a symmetric monoidal structure, and using [@lur17 Proposition
2.2.1.9] one can check that the functor $L_{\mathbb{A}^1}$ above is a
symmetric monoidal localisation. We obtain a symmetric monoidal
composition
$$\mathcal{M}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}: \textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S\stackrel{\gamma}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R) \stackrel{R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S}{\longrightarrow} \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \stackrel{L_{\mathbb{A}^1}}{\longrightarrow} \mathcal{DM}^{\textnormal{eff},(\wedge)}_{\tau}(S;R),$$
and for $X \in \textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S$ we call
$\mathcal{M}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(X)$ the *effective motive associated to
$X$*. For the Nisnevich topology, it follows from the finite
cohomological dimension of the base scheme [@ks86 1.2.5] that
$\mathcal{DM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}^{\textnormal{eff}}(S;R)$ is compactly
generated by the motives $\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(X)$ for $X$
smooth over $S$.*
:::

::: {#defi:smoothartinmotives .defi}
**Definition 1**. *The $\infty$-category
$\mathcal{DAM}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(S;R)$ of *smooth Artin $\tau$-motives*
is defined as the localising subcategory of
$\mathcal{DM}^{\textnormal{eff},(\wedge)}_{\tau}(S;R)$ generated by the
motives associated to finite étale $S$-schemes. We denote the compact
part $\mathcal{DAM}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(S;R)^{\omega}$ by
$\mathcal{DAM}^{\textnormal{gm}, (\wedge)}_{\tau}(S;R)$, its objects are
called *geometric smooth Artin motives*.*
:::

As in the Nisnevich case the generators of
$\mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S;R)$ are compact we can also describe
the latter as the thick subcategory of
$\mathcal{DM}^{\textnormal{eff,gm}}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S;R)$ generated
by the motives associated to finite étale $S$-schemes, using
Neeman--Thomason localisation. Since the motives associated to finite
étale schemes are self-dual, in particular dualisable,
$\mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S;R)$ is rigidly compactly generated.

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *Recall that one passes from effective motives
$\mathcal{DM}^{\textnormal{eff},(\wedge)}_{\tau}(S;R)$ to non-effective
motives $\mathcal{DM}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}(S;R)$ by tensor inverting the
effective motive associated to the motivic sphere
$\mathbb{S}^{\mathbb{A}^1} = \textnormal{cofib}(\mathcal{M}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(S) \to \mathcal{M}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\mathbb{G}_{m,S}))$.
Here the map $S \to \mathbb{G}_{m,S}$ is the constant section with value
$1$ of the canonical map $\mathbb{G}_{m,S} \to S$. In general the
question when the functor from effective to non-effective motives is
fully faithful is subtle, see e.g. [@voe00 Theorem 4.3.1], [@voe10],
[@cd19 Corollary 11.2.15]. In this paper we will only deal with
effective motives.*
:::

::: {#cons:fettosm .cons}
**Construction 1**. *The inclusion functor
$\iota: \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R) \hookrightarrow \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R)$
induces an adjunction
$$(\hat{\iota}_!,\hat{\iota}^*): \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \rightleftarrows \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
of categories of presheaves with transfers. Here $\hat{\iota}^*$ is
restriction along $\iota$, and $\hat{\iota}_!$ is given by left Kan
extension along $\iota$, which restricts to $\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(-;R)$
by [@chll24 Proposition 3.3.2]. The pointwise formula for the left Kan
extension
$$\hat{\iota}_!F(Y) \simeq \textnormal{colim}_{(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)^\textnormal{op})_{/Y}} \; F$$
ensures that
$\hat{\iota}_!(R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S(X)) \simeq R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S(X)$
holds for a finite étale scheme $X$.*
:::

Fix a geometric point $s$ of the base scheme $S$, i.e. a morphism
$s: \textnormal{Spec}(k) \to S$ where $k$ is an algebraically closed
field. From now on, write $G$ for $\pi^{\textnormal{ét}}_1(S,s)$, the
étale fundamental group of the base scheme [@sga1]. We write
$\underline{R}$ for the Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum of the constant
Mackey functor associated to the ring $R$ and
$\textsf{\textup{perm}}(G;R)$ for the additive category of
$(G;R)$-permutation modules [@bg23 §2]. Our aim now is to extend
Voevodsky's theorem [@voe00 §3.4] that expresses Artin motives over a
field in terms of permutation modules.

::: {#thm:damnisasmodunderlr .thm}
**Theorem 1**. *There is a symmetric monoidal equivalence
$$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \simeq \mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S;R).$$*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* We first produce a left adjoint
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \to \mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S;R)$
and then apply the Barr-Beck theorem. Using [@bcn25 Example 2.15] or
[@fuh25 Corollary 3.18] we can work with
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{perm}}(G;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
instead of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$
throughout. Recall Grothendieck's equivalence of finite discrete
$G$-sets and finite étale $S$-schemes from [@sga1], and consider the
symmetric monoidal functor defined on 1-categories of spans thereof:
$$\begin{align*}
\Psi: \textsf{\textup{span}}(G) \simeq \textsf{\textup{span}}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S) \to \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R).
\end{align*}$$ It is the identity on objects and sends a span of finite
étale $S$-schemes, represented by a map $f: U \to X \times_S Y$, to the
pushforward correspondence
$$f_*\langle U \rangle = \sum_{u \in U^{(0)}} \textnormal{lg}(\mathcal{O}_{U,u}) \cdot d_u. f(u).$$
By [@cd19 9.1.1, Lemma 10.2.6] the cycle $f_*\langle U \rangle$ is an
element of $\textsf{\textup{Cor}}_S(X,Y)$. The identity span
$(X \to X \times_S X)$ is sent to
$\langle \Delta_X \rangle = \textnormal{id}_X \in \textsf{\textup{Cor}}_S(X,X)$,
and composition of finite correspondences as described in [@cd19
Definition 9.1.5] makes $\Psi$ functorial. It preserves finite
coproducts and hence extends to a functor
$$\Psi: \Omega(G) \to \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R),$$ where $\Omega(G)$
is the Burnside category of finite $G$-sets obtained by group completing
the hom-sets in $\textsf{\textup{span}}(G)$ that are abelian monoids
under disjoint union. There is a 'cohomological' ideal $\mathcal{I}(G)$
of homomorphisms in $\Omega(G)$ that is generated by elements
$$\begin{equation}
\label{eq:cohidealgeneator}
(G/H \stackrel{\pi^H_K}{\longleftarrow} G/K \stackrel{\pi^H_K}{\longrightarrow} G/H) - ([H:K] \cdot \textnormal{id}_{G/H})
\end{equation}$$ for a pair of open subgroups $K \leq H \leq G$, see
[@bg23 §4]. The left-hand span in
[\[eq:cohidealgeneator\]](#eq:cohidealgeneator){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:cohidealgeneator"} corresponds to a span of finite étale
$S$-schemes $$\begin{equation}
\label{eq:spangenerator}
(X/\overline{H} \stackrel{\pi^{\overline{H}}_{\overline{K}}}{\longleftarrow} X/\overline{K} \stackrel{\pi^{\overline{H}}_{\overline{K}}}{\longrightarrow} X/\overline{H})
\end{equation}$$ for some finite étale cover $X \to S$ which can be
chosen so that the kernel of the canonical homomorphism
$\varphi: G \to \textnormal{Aut}_{\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S}(X)$ is
contained in $K$, hence in $H$, and $\overline{H}$, $\overline{K}$ are
the respective images of $H$ and $K$ under $\varphi$. The map
$\pi^{\overline{H}}_{\overline{K}}$ is the canonical projection, induced
by the universal property of the quotient by a group action [@gw23
Proposition 20.87]. We now show that the generators in
[\[eq:cohidealgeneator\]](#eq:cohidealgeneator){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:cohidealgeneator"} and hence the ideal $\mathcal{I}(G)$
are killed by the functor $\Psi$.\
Let $f: X/\overline{K} \to X/\overline{H} \times_S X/\overline{H}$
correspond to the span
[\[eq:spangenerator\]](#eq:spangenerator){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:spangenerator"}. It factors through the diagonal
$\Delta_{X/\overline{H}} \subseteq X/\overline{H} \times_S X/\overline{H}$
which is isomorphic to $X/\overline{H}$. We thus have a commutative
diagram $$\begin{tikzcd}
    {X/\overline{K}} & {\Delta_{X/\overline{H}}} & {X/\overline{H} \times_S X/\overline{H}.} \\
    & {X/\overline{H}}
    \arrow[from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["f", bend left=20, from=1-1, to=1-3]
    \arrow[from=1-1, to=2-2, "\pi^{\overline{H}}_{\overline{K}}"']
    \arrow[hook, from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[from=1-2, to=2-2, "\hspace*{-0.01cm}\rotatebox{90}{$\sim$}"]
    \arrow[from=1-3, to=2-2, "p_1"]
\end{tikzcd}$$ It hence suffices to show that
$(\pi^{\overline{H}}_{\overline{K}})_*\langle X/\overline{K} \rangle = [H:K] . \langle X/\overline{H} \rangle$
holds. The canonical projection
$\pi^{\overline{H}}_{\overline{K}}: X/\overline{K} \to X/\overline{H}$
is surjective and thus maps generic points to generic points. Let $y$ be
a generic point of $X/\overline{H}$. Then the coefficient of $y$
appearing in the cycle
$(\pi^{\overline{H}}_{\overline{K}})_*\langle X/\overline{K} \rangle$ is
$$\begin{align*}
\sum_{x} \; [\kappa(x) : \kappa(y)] \; \cdot \textnormal{lg}(\mathcal{O}_{X/\overline{K},x}) &= \textnormal{lg}(\mathcal{O}_{X/\overline{H},y}) \cdot \sum_{x} \; [\kappa(x) : \kappa(y)] \tag*{\cite[{}10.52.13]{stacks}} \\
&= \textnormal{lg}(\mathcal{O}_{X/\overline{H},y}) \cdot \textnormal{deg}(\pi^{\overline{H}}_{\overline{K}}) \tag*{\cite[Prop. 12.21]{gw10}}\\
&= \textnormal{lg}(\mathcal{O}_{X/\overline{H},y}) \cdot [H:K] \tag*{\cite[Prop. 20.89]{gw23}}
\end{align*}$$ where the sum runs over all
$x \in (\pi^{\overline{H}}_{\overline{K}})^{-1}(y)$. Since
$\textnormal{lg}(\mathcal{O}_{X/\overline{H},y})$ is precisely the
coefficient of $y$ in $\langle \Delta_{X/\overline{H}} \rangle$ it
follows that
$$\Psi(f) = f_* \langle X/\overline{K} \rangle = [H:K] . \langle \Delta_{X/\overline{H}} \rangle = [H:K] . \textnormal{id}_{X/\overline{H}}.$$
Hence $\Psi$ descends to $\Omega(G)/\mathcal{I}(G)$. Since the target
$\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$ is $R$-linear, we can extend the source
to $\Omega_R(G)/\mathcal{I}_R(G)$, as in [@bg23 Corollary 4.21]. Here
$\Omega_R(G)$ is the $R$-linearisation of $\Omega(G)$, and
$\mathcal{I}_R(G)$ is the analogous ideal of $\Omega_R(G)$ on the same
generators as for $\mathcal{I}(G)$. By [@bg23 Proposition 4.17] the free
$R$-module functor induces an equivalence
$\textsf{\textup{perm}}(G;R)\simeq \Omega_R(G)/\mathcal{I}_R(G)$, and we
have an additive symmetric monoidal functor
$$\psi: \textsf{\textup{perm}}(G;R)\stackrel{\Psi}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R) \stackrel{\iota}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R),$$
where the second functor is the canonical inclusion. Using [@chll24
Proposition 3.3.2] restriction and left Kan extension provide an
adjunction
$$(\psi_!, \psi^*): \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{perm}}(G;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \rightleftarrows \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).$$
If we write
$\iota_!: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
for the functor given by the composition of $\hat{\iota}_!$ (left Kan
extension on $\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}$, see
[1](#cons:fettosm){reference-type="ref+Label" reference="cons:fettosm"})
and sheafification it makes the following diagram commute:
$$\begin{tikzcd}
    \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R) & \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R) \\
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).}
    \arrow["\iota", hook, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S", from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow["R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S", from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow["{\iota_!}", shift left, from=2-1, to=2-2]
\end{tikzcd}$$ Hence, the composition
$L_{\mathbb{A}^1}\textsf{\textup{a}}_{\textnormal{Nis}}^{\textnormal{tr}}\psi_!$
lands in the full subcategory $\mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S;R)$ of
$\mathcal{DM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}^{\textnormal{eff}}(S;R)$. Composing
$(\psi_!, \psi^*)$ with the adjunctions
$$\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=1.4cm]
    {\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{DM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}^{\textnormal{eff}}(S;R)}
    \arrow["{(\textsf{\textup{a}}_{\textnormal{Nis}}^{\textnormal{tr}},\textsf{\textup{o}}_{\textnormal{Nis}}^{\textnormal{tr}})}", shift left, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[shift left=2, from=1-2, to=1-1]
    \arrow["{(L_{\mathbb{A}^1},\textnormal{incl})}", shift left, from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[shift left, from=1-3, to=1-2]
\end{tikzcd}$$ we thus obtain an adjunction
$$(\alpha, \beta): \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G);R) \rightleftarrows \mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S;R).$$
Now, $\alpha$ sends the presheaf associated to a finite $G$-set (i.e.,
its free $\underline{R}$-module) to the motive associated to its
corresponding finite étale $S$-scheme. By construction $\alpha$ is
symmetric monoidal, so it preserves dualisable and hence compact
objects, and it sends a set of generators to a set of generators. It
follows that $\beta$ preserves colimits and is conservative. Since the
projection formula holds automatically in this case, [@mnn17 Proposition
5.29] gives a symmetric monoidal equivalence
$$\mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S;R) \simeq \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\beta(\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}(S)})}\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \simeq \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\beta(\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}(S)})}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G),$$
where the second equivalence is [@lur17 Corollary 3.4.1.9]. It remains
to identify the algebra object
$\beta(\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S))$. For some open subgroup
$H \leq G$, the $n$-th homotopy is given by $$\begin{align}
\pi_n^{H}(\beta(\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S))) &= \textnormal{Hom}_{\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)}(\Sigma^{\infty +n}_+G/H \otimes \underline{R},\beta(\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S))) \notag \\
&\cong \textnormal{Hom}_{\mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S;R)}(\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(X/\overline{H}),\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S)), \label{eq:homotopy}
\end{align}$$ where $X/\overline{H}$ is the finite étale $S$-scheme
corresponding to the $G$-set $G/H$ - here $X$ is a finite étale
$S$-scheme chosen so that $H$ contains the kernel of the canonical
homomorphism $G \to \textnormal{Aut}_{\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S}(X)$, and
$\overline{H}$ is the image of $H$ under this homomorphism. By [@cd19
Theorem 11.2.14, Proposition 10.2.5] the group
[\[eq:homotopy\]](#eq:homotopy){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:homotopy"} is isomorphic to $R$ when $n=0$, and zero
otherwise. Furthermore, for an inclusion of open subgroups $H \leq K$ of
$G$ the restriction map
$$\pi_n^{K}(\beta(\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S))) \to \pi_n^{H}(\beta(\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S)))$$
is given by precomposition with the map of motives that comes from the
canonical degree $[K:H]$-map $X/\overline{H} \to X/\overline{K}$, where
$X$ is chosen such that the kernel of
$G \to \textnormal{Aut}_{\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S}(X)$ contains $K$ and
hence $H$. Again by [@cd19 Theorem 11.2.14, Proposition 10.2.5] in
degree $0$ this identifies with the map
$$R(\pi_0(X/\overline{H})) \to R(\pi_0(X/\overline{K})),$$ which is the
identity since both schemes are connected. It follows that there is an
abstract equivalence
$\beta(\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S)) \cong \underline{R}$ (note
that [@cd19 Proposition 11.2.5] is precisely the statement that the
resulting Mackey functor is cohomological [@tw95 §16]). We need to show
that it is induced by a map of algebra objects.\
Consider the unit map
$\varphi: \underline{R} \to \beta(\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S))$.
Since both algebra objects live in the heart
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)^{\heartsuit} \simeq \textsf{\textup{Mack}}_R^{\textsf{\textup{coh}}}(G)$,
the map $\varphi$ is induced by an algebra map in the heart
$\varphi^{\heartsuit}: \underline{R}^\heartsuit \to \beta(\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S))^\heartsuit$.
Since both sides are isomorphic to the Mackey functor $\underline{R}$
and the latter is the initial commutative cohomological Green functor,
$\varphi^\heartsuit$ is an isomorphism, and $\varphi$ is an equivalence
of commutative algebra objects. Hence, we have
$\beta(\mathcal{M}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S)) \simeq \underline{R}$ in
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$. This concludes the proof. ◻
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *Using the spectral Mackey functor description of
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$, we could also produce a left adjoint from the
latter to $\mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S;R)$, in fact with less
work on finite correspondences. Then the Barr-Beck theorem applies, but
the difficulty then lies in identifying the algebra object we obtain by
applying the right adjoint to the unit. This identification is simpler
when we already deal with an $\underline{R}$-module.*
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *Another way to obtain a functor
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G\to \mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S;R)$ is
constructing a functor
$c_S: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G\to \mathcal{SH}^\textnormal{eff}(S)$ to
effective motivic spectra over $S$, and then passing to effective
motives. Over a field, such a functor has been constructed by
Heller--Ormsby [@ho16], for a much more general construction see [@bh21
§10.2].\
In the case of the real and the complex numbers, it is known
[@lev14; @ho16; @ho18] that these functors (now with values in all of
$\mathcal{SH}$) restrict to equivalences
$$c_{\mathbb{C}}: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}\stackrel{\sim}{\longrightarrow} \mathcal{SH}_{\textnormal{A}}(\mathbb{C}) \hspace{1cm} \textnormal{and} \hspace{1cm} c_{\mathbb{R}}: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{C_2} \stackrel{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{SH}_{\textnormal{A}}(\mathbb{R})$$
onto the localising subcategories generated by motivic spectra
associated to finite étale algebras for the respective fields. The
inverses are given by complex and real Betti realisation, which are
induced by the assignment that sends a complex (resp. real) variety to
its complex (resp. real) points. Combining this with the fact that
$C_2$-equivariant Betti realisation sends the motivic cohomology
spectrum associated to $\mathbb{Z}$ to the constant Mackey functor
$\underline{\mathbb{Z}}$ [@ho16 Theorem 4.17], the compatibility of this
with restriction [@bhs22 Recollection 1.19] and the fact that motives
over a field of characteristic zero are modules over motivic cohomology
[@ro08] we obtain induced equivalences
$$c_{\mathbb{C}}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \stackrel{\sim}{\longrightarrow} \mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\mathbb{C};\mathbb{Z}) \hspace{0.5cm} \textnormal{and} \hspace{0.5cm} c_{\mathbb{R}}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{C_2}) \stackrel{\sim}{\longrightarrow} \mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{Z}).$$
Note that over a field, we do not need to distinguish between effective
and non-effective Artin motives [@bg23 Remark 7.9].*
:::

::: {#rem:alternativeproof .rem}
**Remark 1**. *One can also show directly that
$\Psi: \textsf{\textup{span}}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S) \to \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$
which we used above induces an equivalence
$\textsf{\textup{perm}}(G;R)\simeq \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$ upon
factoring out the cohomological ideal, see [@bg23 Proposition 4.17] and
the ideas indicated in [@bg23 Remark 6.5]. From there one can pursue a
similar proof strategy as in op. cit. by showing that since Nisnevich
covers of finite étale schemes split, a presheaf with transfers on
finite étale schemes is automatically a Nisnevich sheaf. Then one can
argue that these sheaves embed fully faithfully into Nisnevich sheaves
with transfers on smooth schemes and are not affected by
$\mathbb{A}^1$-localisation.*
:::

Having dealt with the Nisnevich case, we now consider the étale
topology. The following considerations also hold for the Nisnevich
topology, but we will stick to the former in terms of notation, since
that is the case in which they will be applied.

::: {#lem:restrictionsheaves .lem}
**Lemma 1**. *The restriction functor $\hat{\iota}^*$ from
[1](#cons:fettosm){reference-type="ref+Label" reference="cons:fettosm"}
preserves (hyper)sheaves with transfers and hence induces a functor
$$\iota^*: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).$$*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* First, note that the restriction
$\hat{i}^*:\mathcal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
along the inclusion
$i: \textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\hookrightarrow \textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S$
preserves (hyper)sheaves, since
$i: \textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\hookrightarrow \textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S$
preserves covers. We thus have an induced functor
$i^*: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$.
Since there is a commutative square $$\begin{tikzcd}
    \textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S& \textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S\\
    \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R) & \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R),
    \arrow["i", hook, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["\gamma", from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow["\gamma", from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow["\iota", hook, from=2-1, to=2-2]
\end{tikzcd}$$ the universal property of the pullback squares defining
sheaves with transfers using the functor $i^*$ together with the
restriction functors on presheaves and presheaves with transfers induces
a functor
$$\iota^*: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}),$$
which is given by restricting the functor $\hat{\iota}^*$ on presheaves
with transfers to sheaves with transfers. ◻
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *The functor
$\iota^*: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
has a left adjoint $\iota_!$ given by
$\textsf{\textup{a}}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{(\wedge), \textnormal{tr}} \hat{\iota}_!$,
as in the proof of
[1](#thm:damnisasmodunderlr){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:damnisasmodunderlr"}. In particular, the property
$\iota_!(R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S(X)) \simeq R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S(X)$ for
$X$ finite étale over $S$ also holds on (hyper)sheaves with transfers.*
:::

::: {#lem:iotastar .lem}
**Lemma 1**. *The functor $\iota^*$ from
[1](#lem:restrictionsheaves){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:restrictionsheaves"} preserves colimits.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* As a right adjoint between stable $\infty$-categories $\iota^*$
is exact. We hence need to show that $\iota^*$ preserves filtered
colimits. Filtered colimits in
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
and
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
are computed pointwise and $\hat{\iota}^*$ preserves them. Colimits in
(hyper)sheaves with transfers are computed as the (hyper)sheafification
of the colimit taken in presheaves. By
[1](#lem:restrictionsheaves){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:restrictionsheaves"} the functor $\iota^*$ is the
restriction of $\hat{\iota}^*$ to (hyper)sheaves with transfers, which
implies the claim. ◻
:::

::: {#lem:inclusionderived .lem}
**Lemma 1**. *The functor
$\iota_!: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
is fully faithful and symmetric monoidal.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* To show that $\iota_!$ is fully faithful we consider the unit
$\textnormal{id} \to \iota^*\iota_!$ of the adjunction
$\iota_! \dashv \iota^*$. By
[1](#lem:iotastar){reference-type="ref+Label" reference="lem:iotastar"}
the restriction $\iota^*$ preserves colimits, $\iota_!$ preserves
colimits as a left adjoint, and
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
is generated by 'representables' $R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S(X)$ under
colimits and shifts. This means that in order to show that
$\textnormal{id} \to \iota^*\iota_!$ is an equivalence it suffices to
evaluate on objects $R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S(X)$ for $X$ a finite étale
$S$-scheme. In this case we have
$\iota^*\iota_!(R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S(X)) \simeq \iota^*(R_S^{\textnormal{tr}}(X)) \simeq R_S^{\textnormal{tr}}(X)$,
and we conclude that $\iota_!$ is fully faithful.\
The symmetric monoidal structure on presheaves with transfers is
obtained by localising the Day convolution on presheaves, so as
$\iota: \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R) \hookrightarrow \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R)$
is symmetric monoidal with respect to the monoidal structure of [@cd19
§9.2], the left adjoint on presheaves with transfers $\hat{\iota}_!$ is
as well [@bs24 Proposition 3.6]. Since the symmetric monoidal structure
on (hyper)sheaves with transfers is obtained by localising the symmetric
monoidal structure on presheaves with transfers, the
(hyper)sheafification functor
$\textsf{\textup{a}}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{(\wedge)}$ is symmetric
monoidal, and so is
$\iota_! = \textsf{\textup{a}}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{(\wedge), \textnormal{tr}}\hat{\iota}_!$. ◻
:::

In the hypercomplete case, we can give assumptions under which the
composition of the functor $\iota_!$ with $\mathbb{A}^1$-localisation to
effective motives stays fully faithful, here computations of étale
cohomology are at hand (see the comparison to the classical setup in
Appendix [6](#sec:sheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref"
reference="sec:sheaveswithtransfers"}).

::: {#lem:aonelocalisationetale .lem}
**Lemma 1**. *If the ring $R$ has positive characteristic $n$ and the
residue characteristics of $S$ are prime to $n$, then the functor
$$\iota_!: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \hookrightarrow \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
remains fully faithful after composing it with the
$\mathbb{A}^1$-localisation functor
$$L_{\mathbb{A}^1}: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \twoheadrightarrow \mathcal{DM}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\textnormal{eff}}(S;R).$$*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* As in the proof of [@bg23 Proposition 7.4], using [@cd16
Proposition 2.2.3], it suffices to show that for any finite étale
$S$-scheme $Y$ and any smooth $S$-scheme $X$ the canonical map
$$\textnormal{H}^i_{\textnormal{ét}}(X \times_S Y,R) \to \textnormal{H}^i_{\textnormal{ét}}(\mathbb{A}^1_{X \times_S Y},R)$$
is an isomorphism in all degrees. The map $X \times_S Y \to X$ and hence
its pullback to $\mathbb{A}^1_X$ are étale, and the residue
characteristics of $X$ can only be those of $S$. We can then invoke
[@cd16 Theorem 1.3.2], as in [@cd16 Proposition 3.1.7]. ◻
:::

The following can be interpreted as a weak form of the rigidity theorem
of Suslin--Voevodsky, see e.g. [@cd16 Theorem 4.5.2].

::: {#cor:dametassheaves .cor}
**Corollary 1**. *Under the assumptions of
[1](#lem:aonelocalisationetale){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:aonelocalisationetale"}, the functor
$L_{\mathbb{A}^1}\iota_!$ induces a symmetric monoidal equivalence
$$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \simeq \mathcal{DAM}^{\wedge}_{\textnormal{ét}}(S;R).$$*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* By [1](#lem:aonelocalisationetale){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:aonelocalisationetale"} $L_{\mathbb{A}^1}\iota_!$ is
fully faithful. Since
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
is generated by the objects $R_S^{\textnormal{tr}}(X)$ for $X$ finite
étale over $S$ under colimits and finite limits,
$L_{\mathbb{A}^1}\iota_!$ lands in
$\mathcal{DAM}^{\wedge}_{\textnormal{ét}}(S;R)$. On the other hand,
$\mathcal{DAM}^{\wedge}_{\textnormal{ét}}(S;R)$ is generated as a
localising subcategory of effective motives by objects in the essential
image of $L_{\mathbb{A}^1}\iota_!$ and the latter is closed under
colimits and finite limits since $L_{\mathbb{A}^1}\iota_!$ is a fully
faithful exact left adjoint. Hence the claim. ◻
:::

In the non-hypercomplete case, recall from
[1](#lem:inclusionderived){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:inclusionderived"} that the functor
$$\iota_!: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \hookrightarrow \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
is fully faithful. Furthermore, its composition with $L_{\mathbb{A}^1}$
lands in $\mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{ét}}(S;R)$, but we will not be able
to say that the composition is fully faithful since in this case we have
no cohomology computations available.\
In the Nisnevich case,
[1](#rem:alternativeproof){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:alternativeproof"} asserts that a presheaf with transfers
on finite étale schemes is automatically a sheaf. In the étale case the
situation is dual: an étale sheaf on finite étale schemes automatically
has transfers. In order to show this, we need a preliminary construction
that refines the pullback diagram of
[1](#defi:sheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="defi:sheaveswithtransfers"}.

::: {#cons:refinedpullback .cons}
**Construction 1**. *The functor
$\gamma: \textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\to \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$
factors as
$$\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\stackrel{\rho}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\times \textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R \stackrel{\gamma \times \sigma}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R) \times \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R) \stackrel{\times_S}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R).$$
Here $\textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R$ is the category of finite rank free
$R$-modules, and by [@lur18 Remark D.1.1.5] we have
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \simeq \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$.
The functor $\rho$ is the identity on the fist factor and constant with
value $R$ on the second, $\sigma$ is the canonical additive functor
$\textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R \to \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$ that sends
$R$ to $S$, and the bifunctor $(-)\times_S(-)$ comes from the monoidal
structure on $\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$. We denote the composition
functor
$\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\times \textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R \to \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$
by $\delta$. Using
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S;R) \simeq \textnormal{Fun}^{\textnormal{bi-}\times}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S^\textnormal{op}\times \textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R^\textnormal{op},\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$,
the pullback square of
[1](#defi:sheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="defi:sheaveswithtransfers"} refines to the iterated pullback
$$\begin{equation}
\label{diag:refinedpullback}
\begin{tikzcd}
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} \\
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S;R)} & {\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S;R)} \\
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).}
    \arrow["{\textsf{\textup{o}}^{(\wedge),\textnormal{tr}}_{\textnormal{ét}}}", hook, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["{\delta^*}", from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow["{\hat{\delta}^*}", from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow["{\textsf{\textup{o}}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{(\wedge)}}", hook, from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["{\rho^*}", from=2-1, to=3-1]
    \arrow["{\hat{\rho}^*}", from=2-2, to=3-2]
    \arrow["{\textsf{\textup{o}}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{(\wedge)}}", hook, from=3-1, to=3-2]
    \arrow["\usebox\pullback"{anchor=center, pos=0.125}, draw=none, from=1-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["\usebox\pullback"{anchor=center, pos=0.125}, draw=none, from=2-1, to=3-2]
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}$$ As before for $\gamma$, the presheaf versions of the
restriction along $\rho$ and $\delta$ are denoted $\hat{\rho}^*$ and
$\hat{\delta}^*$, and the sheaf versions $\rho^*$ and $\delta^*$.*
:::

::: {#thm:etaletransfers .thm}
**Theorem 1**. *The functor
$\delta^*: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S;R)$
is an equivalence.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* We first consider the case of sheaves. Let $S_i$ be the finite
étale $S$-scheme corresponding to the orbit $G/N_i$, so that the
canonical map $G \to \textnormal{Aut}_{\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S}(S_i)$
has kernel given by $N_i$. Write $\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_{{S_i}/S}$ for
the slice $(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S)_{S_i/}$. Then as for $G$-sets
[@cm21 Construction 4.5] the site $\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S$ decomposes
into a filtered colimit of sites
$\textnormal{colim}_i \, \textsf{\textup{Fét}}_{{S_i}/S}$ (each of which
has covering sieves given by jointly surjective families). The
transition maps are the natural maps
$\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_{{S_i}/S} \to \textsf{\textup{Fét}}_{{S_j}/S}$
that precompose with the map $S_i \to S_j$ whenever there is an
inclusion $N_i \subseteq N_j$.\
Note that each of the canonical maps
$\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_{{S_i}/S} \to \textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S$ is fully
faithful. We write $\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}({S_i}/S;R)$ for the full
subcategory of $\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$ on the schemes in
$\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_{{S_i}/S}$. Since each of the maps of sites
descends to these correspondence categories (see
[1](#rem:nicecorrespondences){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:nicecorrespondences"}), we obtain a similar colimit
decomposition
$$\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R) \simeq \textnormal{colim}_i \, \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}({S_i}/S;R),$$
compatible with the functors
$\delta_i: \textsf{\textup{Fét}}_{{S_i}/S} \times \textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R \to \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}({S_i}/S;R)$
that are restricted from the functor $\delta$ of
[1](#rec:graphfunctors){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rec:graphfunctors"}. Using the upper pullback of
[\[diag:refinedpullback\]](#diag:refinedpullback){reference-type="ref"
reference="diag:refinedpullback"}, the decomposition of the (pre)sheaf
categories involved induces one for sheaves of transfers as a limit
along restriction:
$$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \simeq \textnormal{lim}_i \; \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}({S_i}/S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).$$
So it suffices to show that each
$\delta_i^*: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}({S_i}/S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_{{S_i}/S};R)$
which precomposes with the map $\delta_i$ from above is an equivalence.
For the rest of the proof we will assume that $G$ is finite. The
argument we give then holds verbatim for each of the $\delta_i$.\
Note that $\textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R$ is the coproduct completion of its
full subcategory $\{R \} \subseteq \textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R$ on the
object $R$, i.e. it is the smallest full subcategory of
$\mathcal{P}(\{R \})$ that contains the object $R$ and is closed under
finite coproducts. Hence [@lur09 Proposition 5.3.6.2] implies that
restriction and right Kan extension provide an equivalence
$$\begin{equation}
\label{eq:latr}
\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \simeq \textnormal{Fun}^{\times}(\textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R^\textnormal{op},\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \simeq \textnormal{Fun}(\{R\}^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).
\end{equation}$$ For the moment, we will work with the description on
the right-hand side of [\[eq:latr\]](#eq:latr){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:latr"}. Let
$\beta: BG \times \{R \} \to \textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\times \{R \}$ be
the functor that on the first component picks out the scheme $\tilde{S}$
corresponding to the orbit $G/1$ and is given by the identity on the
other component. Write
$\alpha: BG \times \{R \} \to \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$ for its
composition with the functor
$\delta: \textsf{\textup{Fét}}_{S} \times \{R\} \to \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$.
Restriction and right Kan extension along $\alpha$ provide an adjunction
$$(\hat{\alpha}^*,\hat{\alpha}_*): \mathcal{P}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \rightleftarrows \mathcal{P}(BG \times \{R \};\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
which factors as the composition of adjunctions $$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\mathcal{P}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\times \{R \};\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{P}(BG \times \{R \};\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).}
    \arrow["{(\hat{\delta}^*,\hat{\delta}_*)}", shift left, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[shift left, from=1-2, to=1-1]
    \arrow["{(\hat{\beta}^*,\hat{\beta}_*)}", shift left, from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[shift left, from=1-3, to=1-2]
\end{tikzcd}$$ By [@cm21 Example 4.4] (which we already used in
[1](#lem:sheavesfunbg){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="lem:sheavesfunbg"}, up to self-duality of $BG$), the functor
$\hat{\beta}_*$ is fully faithful and has essential image those additive
presheaves that are étale sheaves on the first component (i.e.,
presheaves corresponding to elements in
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S;R) \subseteq \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S;R)$
under the equivalence [\[eq:latr\]](#eq:latr){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:latr"}). We denote the resulting equivalence functor by
$$\beta_*: \mathcal{P}(BG \times \{R \};\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \stackrel{\sim}{\longrightarrow} \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\times \{R \};\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).$$
The functor $\alpha$ sends the unique object $(\ast_{BG},R)$ of its
domain to $\tilde{S}$. By
[1](#rem:nicecorrespondences){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:nicecorrespondences"} the endomorphisms of these two
objects are isomorphic to the free module $R(G)$, and $\alpha$ sends a
morphism $(g,r)$ to
$r \cdot \langle \Gamma_{{\textnormal{id}}^g}\rangle$, where
${\textnormal{id}}^g: \tilde{S} \to \tilde{S}$ corresponds to
conjugation by $g$. Under the description given in
[1](#rem:nicecorrespondences){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:nicecorrespondences"} this corresponds to the element
$r\cdot g$ of $R(G)$ and it follows that $\alpha$ is fully faithful.
Hence the right Kan extension functor $\hat{\alpha}_*$ is fully faithful
as well.\
We now show that $\hat{\alpha}_*$ lands in sheaves with transfers. Let
$\mathcal{F}: BG^\textnormal{op}\times \{R \}^\textnormal{op}\to \textsf{\textup{Sp}}$
be a functor. For a subgroup $H \leq G$, the evaluation of the right Kan
extension $(\hat{\alpha}_*\mathcal{F})(\tilde{S}/H)$ is computed as the
limit of the restriction of $\mathcal{F}$ to
$$\mathcal{Q}\mathrel{\vcenter{\baselineskip 0.4ex \lineskiplimit 0pt
\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}%
=(BG^\textnormal{op}\times \{R \}^\textnormal{op}) \times_{\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)^\textnormal{op}} (\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)^\textnormal{op})_{(\tilde{S}/H)\textnormal{\big /}}.$$
Now, the functor $\delta$ induces an isomorphism of $R$-modules
$$\textnormal{Hom}_{\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\times \textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R}((\tilde{S},R),(\tilde{S}/H,R)) \to \textsf{\textup{Cor}}_S(\tilde{S},\tilde{S}/H)\otimes R,$$
here both sides are isomorphic to $R(G/H)$. It follows that $\delta$
induces an equivalence of $\mathcal{Q}$ to the diagram
$$(BG^\textnormal{op}\times \{R \}^\textnormal{op}) \times_{\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S^\textnormal{op}\times \{R \}^\textnormal{op}} (\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S^\textnormal{op}\times \{R \}^\textnormal{op})_{(\tilde{S}/H,R)\textnormal{\big /}},$$
which computes
$\beta_*\mathcal{F}(\tilde{S}/H) \simeq \mathcal{F}(\ast_{BG},R)^{hH}$
(see also the proof of [@kerodon Proposition 7.3.1.16]). So we have
$\hat{\delta}^*\hat{\alpha}_*(\mathcal{F}) \simeq \hat{\beta}_*(\mathcal{F})$
and $\hat{\delta}^*\hat{\alpha}_*(\mathcal{F})$ is right Kan extended
from $BG^\textnormal{op}\times \{R\}^\textnormal{op}$, and thus a sheaf
and in particular product preserving. Hence
$\hat{\alpha}_*(\mathcal{F})$ is a sheaf with transfers. We denote the
resulting adjunction by
$$(\alpha^*,\alpha_*): \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \rightleftarrows \textnormal{Fun}(BG \times \{R \}^\textnormal{op},\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).$$
Now, for
$\mathcal{G}\in \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\times \{R \};\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
there exists
$\mathcal{F}\in \mathcal{P}(BG \times \{R \};\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$ such
that $\beta_*(\mathcal{F}) \simeq \mathcal{G}$. Since right Kan
extensions compose we have
$\hat{\delta}_*(\mathcal{G}) \simeq \hat{\delta}_*\beta_*(\mathcal{F}) \simeq \alpha_*(\mathcal{F})$
which is a sheaf with transfers. Hence $\hat{\delta}_*$ sends sheaves to
sheaves with transfers and we denote the resulting functor by
$$\delta_*: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\times \{R\};\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}),$$
it is right adjoint to restriction along $\delta$. Now, since $\alpha_*$
and $\beta_*$ are fully faithful, $\delta_*$ is fully faithful as well.
Since $\delta$ is essentially surjective $\hat{\delta}^*$ is
conservative, so since
$\textsf{\textup{o}}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\textnormal{tr}}$ is fully
faithful $\delta^*$ is conservative as well. We now have an adjunction
$\delta^* \dashv \delta_*$ for which the right adjoint is fully faithful
and the left adjoint is conservative, so this adjunction is an
equivalence. Since
$$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S\times \{R\};\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \simeq \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S;R),$$
this finishes the argument for sheaves. The statement for hypersheaves
follows from the one for sheaves since the hypersheaf version
$$\delta^*: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S;R)$$
is the pullback of the functor on sheaves, as in
[1](#rem:tstructuresheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:tstructuresheaveswithtransfers"}. Hence it is an
equivalence as well, which concludes the proof. ◻
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *In the hypercomplete case, the statement of the previous
proposition is also implied by [@cd16 Corollary 2.1.9], which relies on
a concrete identification of the effect of the graph functor on
'representable' sheaves and sheaves with transfers. We also refer to
[@cm21 Corollary 4.39] for some form of converse for sheaves on the
small étale site, and to [@bh21 Corollary C.13] for a similar statement
using $\infty$-categories of spans.\
Morally speaking, étale sheaves on finite $G$-sets already have
transfers because they are right Kan extended from $BG$ (at least when
$G$ is finite) and thus have an overlying Borel complete $G$-spectrum.\
One can make this line of thoughts precise by explicitly identifying the
correspondence category $\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$ with
$\textsf{\textup{perm}}(G;R)$, using that by [@fuh25 Corollary 3.17]
spectral presheaves on the latter are the same as modules over the
constant $R$-linear Mackey functor $\underline{R}$ in
$\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G$, and then using that the Borel complete
subcategory only sees the restriction of $\underline{R}$ to the trivial
group.*
:::

In the following theorem, we will write
$\mathcal{D}\textsf{\textup{Perm}}(G;R)$ for the derived
$\infty$-category of $(G;R)$-permutation modules [@bg22a], [@bg23 §3],
[@fuh25 §4]. It combines the results of the previous sections.

::: {#thm:bigdiagram .thm}
**Theorem 1**. *There is a commutative diagram in which horizontal
functors are equivalences and vertical functors are localisations.
$$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\mathcal{D}\textsf{\textup{Perm}}(G;R)} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)} & {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})}  \\
    {\textnormal{colim}_i \, \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R))} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}} & {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})}  \\
    {\mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G;R))} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{Borel}}} & {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).} 
    \arrow["\sim", from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["\alpha", from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow["\sim", from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow["\beta", from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow["\textsf{\textup{a}}_{\textnormal{ét}}", from=1-3, to=2-3]
    \arrow["\sim", from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["L^h", from=2-1, to=3-1]
    \arrow["\sim", from=2-2, to=2-3]
    \arrow["L^h", from=2-2, to=3-2]
    \arrow["(-)^{\wedge}_\textnormal{ét}", from=2-3, to=3-3]
    \arrow["\sim", from=3-1, to=3-2]
    \arrow["\sim", from=3-2, to=3-3]
\end{tikzcd}$$ If the virtual cohomological dimension of the group $G$
is finite, all vertical functors from the second to the third row are
equivalences.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* The content of the statement for the lower two rows was
discussed already: The commutativity of the two bottom squares was shown
in [1](#prop:sheavesborelcomplete){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:sheavesborelcomplete"},
[1](#prop:sheavesgrmodules){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:sheavesgrmodules"} and
[1](#thm:etaletransfers){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:etaletransfers"}. The assertion about the virtual
cohomological dimension of $G$ is
[1](#prop:smashinghypercompletion){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:smashinghypercompletion"} and
[1](#cor:smashinghypercompletionreps){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:smashinghypercompletionreps"}. Let us consider the upper
part of the diagram.\
The equivalences in the top row hold by [@fuh25 Corollary 4.8, Corollary
3.18], [@bcn25 Example 2.15] and
[1](#thm:damnisasmodunderlr){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:damnisasmodunderlr"} - using that Nisnevich covers in
$\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S$ split the left adjoint in the latter theorem
factors through
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$,
hence is fully faithful, and it clearly hits generators. These
equivalences are compatible with the filtered colimit decompositions of
the three $\infty$-categories involved we will use below.\
By passing to Ind-objects, [@bg25b Proposition 2.4] implies that the
$\infty$-category $\mathcal{D}\textsf{\textup{Perm}}(G;R)$ decomposes as
a filtered colimit in $\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}$ along
inflation. Similarly,
$\textnormal{colim}_i \, \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R))$ by
definition is a $\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}$-colimit along
inflation. The functor $\alpha$ for a finite quotient $G_i$ is given as
the Ind-extension of the functor
$$\mathcal{K}_{\textnormal{b}}(\textsf{\textup{perm}}(G_i;R)^\natural) \to \mathcal{D}_{\textnormal{b}}(\textsf{\textup{mod}}(G_i;R)) \to \mathcal{D}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}(G_i;R))$$
that is induced by the inclusion
$\textsf{\textup{perm}}(G_i;R)^\natural \hookrightarrow \textsf{\textup{mod}}(G_i;R)$,
where the latter category denotes finitely generated $(G_i;R)$-modules.\
Using (the profinite extension of) [@gs14 §5.2], there is a ring map
$R_G \to \underline{R}$ which is determined by being the unit map
$\mathbb{A}_R \to \underline{R}$ in $R$-linear Mackey functors on
$\pi_0$. The levelwise completion functor of
[3](#sec:borelprofinite){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="sec:borelprofinite"} induces a functor $$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\beta: \, \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G) \simeq \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)} & {\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{L_{\textnormal{lw}(\underline{R})}}(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}),}
    \arrow["{L_{\textnormal{lw}}}", from=1-1, to=1-2]
\end{tikzcd}$$ on module categories, where the first equivalence is
[@lur17 Corollary 3.4.1.9]. By [@fuh25 Construction 3.8, p.39], we can
write $\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)$ as
a $\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}$-colimit along the functors
induced by inflation on module categories, followed by an extension of
scalars along a ring map
$\textnormal{infl}_{G_j}^{G_i}(\underline{R})\to \underline{R}$ that is
determined by being the identity on the sections for the trivial group
on $\pi_0$. Under this description, the functor $\beta$ identifies with
the one induced on colimits by the functors
$$\beta_i: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{\underline{R}}\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i}) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{L_{G_i;R_{G_i}}(\underline{R})}\left(\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})_{\textnormal{Borel}}\right).$$
Each $\beta_i$ is the functor induced by
$L_{G_i;R_{G_i}}: \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i}) \to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})_{\textnormal{Borel}}$
on $\underline{R}$-modules. But since the map
$R_{G_i} \to \underline{R}$ becomes an equivalence when restricting to
the trivial group, $L_{G_i;R_{G_i}}(\underline{R})$ identifies with the
unit of
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_{G_i}}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^{G_i})_{\textnormal{Borel}}$,
and $\beta$ lands in
$\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_{R_G}(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}^G)_{\textnormal{lwBorel}}$.\
The functor
$\textsf{\textup{a}}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\textnormal{tr}}: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
is the restriction of the étale sheafification functor to Nisnevich
sheaves with transfers (which by
[1](#rem:alternativeproof){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:alternativeproof"} are just presheaves with transfers),
see [1](#rem:pushoutinsteadofpullback){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:pushoutinsteadofpullback"}.\
We now show that the two upper squares commute. Using the compatibility
of the above functors with colimit decompositions, it suffices to check
commutativity for each finite quotient $G/N_i=G_i$. By the universal
property of the $\infty$-category in the upper row [@fuh25 Corollary
3.17] it suffices to establish commutativity of the restrictions to
$\textsf{\textup{perm}}(G_i;R)$. The latter is the coproduct completion
of its full subcategory on the permutation modules $R(G_i/H)$ for
$H\leq G$, and we can furthermore restrict to this subcategory. In this
case, there is a commutative diagram $$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\{R(G_i/H) \mid H \leq G_i\}} & {\{\underline{R} \otimes \Sigma^{\infty}_+G_i/H \mid H \leq G_i\}} & {\{R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S(\tilde{S}_i/H) \mid H \leq G_i\}} \\
    {\{R(G_i/H) \mid H \leq G_i\}} & {\{R[G/H_i] \mid H \leq G_i\}} & {\{R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S(\tilde{S}_i/H) \mid H \leq G_i\}}
    \arrow["\sim", from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["\alpha", from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow["\sim", from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow["\beta", from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow["{\textsf{\textup{a}}^{\textnormal{tr}}_{\textnormal{ét}}}", from=1-3, to=2-3]
    \arrow["\sim", from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["\sim", from=2-2, to=2-3]
\end{tikzcd}$$ in which every node indicates the full subcategory of the
respective node in the upper two rows of the diagram in the statement.
The centre of the bottom row we view as a full subcategory of
$\textnormal{Fun}(BG_i,\textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}}))$,
and under this description the functor $\beta$ evaluates a spectral
presheaf on $\textsf{\textup{perm}}(G_i;R)$ on $R(G_i)$. For the two
categories on the right, $\tilde{S}_i$ is an $S$-scheme corresponding to
the $G$-orbit $G/N_i$, i.e. the kernel of the canonical homomorphism
$\varphi: G \to \textnormal{Aut}_{\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S}(\tilde{S}_i)$
is $N_i$. Note that by
[1](#rem:representables){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:representables"} the elements
$R^{\textnormal{tr}}_S(\tilde{S}_i/H)$ are already étale sheaves with
transfers. For the behaviour of the bottom right horizontal functor we
refer to the proof of
[1](#thm:etaletransfers){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:etaletransfers"} - its inverse evaluates on
$\tilde{S}_i$. This concludes the proof. ◻
:::

We can now amend the diagram of the previous theorem to the right.

::: {#thm:bigdiagramright .thm}
**Theorem 1**. *There is a commutative diagram $$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S;R)} & {\mathcal{DM}^{\textnormal{eff}}_{\textnormal{Nis}}(S;R)} \\
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{DAM}_{\textnormal{ét}}(S;R)} & {\mathcal{DM}^{\textnormal{eff}}_{\textnormal{ét}}(S;R)}\\
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\textnormal{ét}}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{DAM}^{\wedge}_{\textnormal{ét}}(S;R)} & {\mathcal{DM}^{\textnormal{eff},\wedge}_{\textnormal{ét}}(S;R).}
    \arrow["{L_{\mathbb{A}^1}\iota_!}", from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["\sim"', draw=none, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["{\textsf{\textup{a}}^{\textnormal{tr}}_{\textnormal{ét}}}", from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow["{\textsf{\textup{a}}^{\textnormal{tr}}_{\textnormal{ét}}}", from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow["{\textsf{\textup{a}}^{\textnormal{tr}}_{\textnormal{ét}}}", from=1-3, to=2-3]
    \arrow["{L_{\mathbb{A}^1}\iota_!}", from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow["(\ast)"', draw=none, from=3-1, to=3-2]
    \arrow["{(-)^\wedge_\textnormal{ét}}", from=2-1, to=3-1]
    \arrow["(\ast\ast)"', draw=none, from=2-1, to=3-1]
    \arrow["{(-)^\wedge_\textnormal{ét}}", from=2-2, to=3-2]
    \arrow["(\ast\ast)"', draw=none, from=2-2, to=3-2]
    \arrow["{(-)^\wedge_\textnormal{ét}}", from=2-3, to=3-3]
    \arrow["{L_{\mathbb{A}^1}\iota_!}", from=3-1, to=3-2]
    \arrow[hook, from=1-2, to=1-3]
    \arrow[hook, from=2-2, to=2-3]
    \arrow[hook, from=3-2, to=3-3]
\end{tikzcd}$$ in which the top left horizontal arrow is an equivalence.
We furthermore have the following implications:*

1.  *If the ring $R$ has positive characteristic $n$ and the residue
    characteristics of $S$ are prime to $n$, the functor labelled
    $(\ast)$ is an equivalence.*

2.  *If the virtual cohomological dimension of $G$ is finite, then the
    two functors labelled $(\ast\ast)$ are equivalences.*

3.  *If $R$ is a $\mathbb{Q}$-algebra, all vertical functors and hence
    the horizontal functors on the left side of the diagram are
    equivalences.*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* The functors on Artin motives and effective motives are induced
by étale sheafification and hypercompletion on smooth $S$-schemes, we
denote them by the same symbols as on sheaves with transfers.
Commutativity of the diagram is clear by construction. The fact that the
top horizontal map is an equivalence follows from
[1](#thm:damnisasmodunderlr){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:damnisasmodunderlr"}, see also the proof of
[1](#thm:bigdiagram){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:bigdiagram"}. Assertion (1) was shown in
[1](#cor:dametassheaves){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:dametassheaves"}, (2) was recorded in
[1](#thm:bigdiagram){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="thm:bigdiagram"} using
[1](#prop:smashinghypercompletion){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="prop:smashinghypercompletion"} and
[1](#cor:smashinghypercompletionreps){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cor:smashinghypercompletionreps"}, and (3) is implied by
[@cd16 Proposition 2.2.10]. ◻
:::

## Sheaves with transfers

Using the language of triangulated categories, (effective) Voevodsky
motives (for the Nisnevich topology or more generally for other
topologies on smooth schemes) are classically defined by deriving the
$1$-category of Nisnevich sheaves with transfers and then localising
with respect to $\mathbb{A}^1$ [@cd19 §11]. On the other hand,
definitions using $\infty$-(pre)sheaves are present in the literature
[@ek20; @bh21]. In this appendix we show that our definition of sheaves
with transfers (that is, before $\mathbb{A}^1$-localisation) agrees with
the more classical one that uses derived categories, thereby also
justifying that we use classical computations of mapping spaces in
effective motives. For this, we will focus on hypersheaves, which on the
categorical level correspond to derived $\infty$-categories of
$1$-categorical sheaves. We keep all standing assumptions from the
previous section.\
We first recall a few facts about the $\infty$-category of additive
presheaves with values in an $\infty$-category which for our purposes
will always be spectra or an $\infty$-category of modules in spectra.

::: {#rec:psigma .rec}
**Recollection 1**. *Let $\mathcal{C}$ be an $\infty$-category which
admits finite coproducts. Let
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C}) \subseteq \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C}) = \textnormal{Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\textnormal{op}},\textsf{\textup{Spc}})$
be the full subcategory on presheaves that preserve finite products.
Then $\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C})$ is compactly generated [@lur09
Proposition 5.5.8.10(6)] and an accessible localisation of
$\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C})$ [@lur09 Proposition 5.5.8.10(1)], we write
$$L_{\Sigma}: \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C}) \to \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C})$$
for the left adjoint localisation functor. The Yoneda embedding
$y: \mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C})$ factors through
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C})$ [@lur09 Proposition 5.5.8.10(2)].\
Let $\mathcal{C}$ be additively symmetric monoidal[^12] and let
$\mathcal{D}$ be a presentably symmetric monoidal stable
$\infty$-category. Then the presheaf $\infty$-categories
$\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C})$ and $\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{D})$
become symmetric monoidal under Day convolution [@lur17 §2.2.6, 4.8.1].
These structures make the map
$\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C}) \to \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{D})$
induced by the unit $\textsf{\textup{Spc}}\to \mathcal{D}$ in
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}})$ symmetric
monoidal, see [@nik16 Corollary 3.7] or [@bs24 Proposition 3.3].\
As in [@bgs20 Lemma 3.7] the tensor product on
$\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C})$ preserves $L_{\Sigma}$-equivalences in both
variables since it preserves colimits in each variables, and
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C})$ by [@lur17 Proposition 2.2.1.9]
inherits a symmetric monoidal structure from $\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C})$
which informally is given by performing a Day convolution in
$\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C})$ and then applying the localisation functor
$L_{\Sigma}$. This makes
$L_{\Sigma}: \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C}) \to \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C})$
and $y: \mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C})$
symmetric monoidal.\
If $\mathcal{C}$ carries a Grothendieck topology $\tau$ such that the
tensor product on $\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{D})$ preserves
$a^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}$-equivalences, then tensoring in
$\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{D})$ followed by (hyper)sheafification
endows
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}(\mathcal{C}; \mathcal{D})$
with a symmetric monoidal structure which makes it presentably symmetric
monoidal.\
Analogously, the full subcategory
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{D}) \subseteq \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{D})$
on $\mathcal{D}$-valued presheaves which preserve finite products is a
symmetric monoidal localisation of
$\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{D})$, cf. [@aok20 Remark 2.10]. When
$\mathcal{D}= \textsf{\textup{Mod}}_R(\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$ we write
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C};R)$. By [@ggn15 Corollary 2.10(iii),
Example 5.3(ii)] or [@lur18 Proposition C.1.5.7] there is a symmetric
monoidal equivalence
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C}) \simeq \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C};\textsf{\textup{Sp}}_{\geq 0})$
which is induced by the functor
$\Omega^{\infty}: \textsf{\textup{Sp}}_{\geq 0} \to \textsf{\textup{Spc}}$.
Together with the inclusion of connective spectra into spectra this
induces a symmetric monoidal and fully faithful 'stable Yoneda
embedding'
$$\bar{y}: \mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C}) \simeq \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C};\textsf{\textup{Sp}}_{\geq 0}) \hookrightarrow \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C};\textsf{\textup{Sp}}),$$
as in [@aok20 Corollary 2.9]. For $\mathcal{D}$ presentably symmetric
monoidal and stable the essentially unique symmetric monoidal left
adjoint $\textsf{\textup{Sp}}\to \mathcal{D}$ again induces a symmetric
monoidal functor
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C};\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{D})$.*
:::

Recall that [1](#defi:sheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="defi:sheaveswithtransfers"} defined (hyper)sheaves with
transfers as the pullback $$\begin{equation}
\label{diag:pullbacksheaveswithtransfers}
\begin{tikzcd}
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} \\
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).}
    \arrow[from=1-1, to=1-2, "{\textsf{\textup{o}}^{(\wedge),\textnormal{tr}}_{\tau}}", hook]
    \arrow[from=1-1, to=2-1, "\gamma^*"]
    \arrow[from=1-2, to=2-2, "\hat{\gamma}^*"]
    \arrow[from=2-1, to=2-2, "{\textsf{\textup{o}}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}}", hook]
    \arrow["\usebox\pullback"{anchor=center, pos=0.125}, draw=none, from=1-1, to=2-2]
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}$$

The following two remarks are formulated for smooth $S$-schemes, but
they hold verbatim after replacing smooth by finite étale schemes. As in
[5](#sec:motives){reference-type="ref+Label" reference="sec:motives"},
$\tau$ stands for either the Nisnevich or the étale topology.

::: {#rem:pushoutinsteadofpullback .rem}
**Remark 1**. *By [1](#rec:psigma){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rec:psigma"} the functor
$\textsf{\textup{o}}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}: \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \hookrightarrow \mathcal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
has a left adjoint (hyper)sheafification functor
$$\textsf{\textup{a}}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}: \mathcal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).$$
The forgetful functor
$\hat{\gamma}^*: \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
has a left adjoint given by left Kan extension
$$\hat{\gamma}_!: \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
along $\gamma: \textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S\to \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R)$,
which restricts to $\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}$ by [@chll24 Proposition
3.3.2]. This means that
[\[diag:pullbacksheaveswithtransfers\]](#diag:pullbacksheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref"
reference="diag:pullbacksheaveswithtransfers"} is a pullback in
$\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{R}}_{\textnormal{st}}$, and we denote
the left adjoints to $\gamma^*$ and
$\textsf{\textup{o}}^{(\wedge),\textnormal{tr}}_{\tau}$ by $\gamma_!$
and $\textsf{\textup{a}}_{\tau}^{(\wedge), \textnormal{tr}}$
respectively. It follows formally that
$\gamma_! \simeq \textsf{\textup{a}}_{\tau}^{(\wedge), \textnormal{tr}} \circ \hat{\gamma}_!$.
We can hence express
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
as the $\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}_{\textnormal{st}}$-pushout
$$\begin{tikzcd}
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} \\
    {\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})} & {\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).}
    \arrow[from=1-2, to=1-1, "{\textsf{\textup{a}}_{\tau}^{(\wedge), \textnormal{tr}}}"']
    \arrow[from=2-1, to=1-1, "\gamma_!"']
    \arrow[from=2-2, to=1-2, "\hat{\gamma}_!"']
    \arrow[from=2-2, to=2-1, "{\textsf{\textup{a}}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}}"']
    \arrow["\usebox\pullback"{anchor=center, pos=0.125}, draw=none, from=1-1, to=2-2]
\end{tikzcd}$$ Furthermore, both functors $\hat{\gamma}_!$ and
$\textsf{\textup{a}}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}$ admit symmetric monoidal
enhancements (using [1](#rec:psigma){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rec:psigma"} and [@bs24 Proposition 3.6]), and since
$\textsf{\textup{a}}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}$ is a localisation this pushout
can also be computed in
$\textnormal{CAlg}(\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{L}}_{\textnormal{st}})$.
The resulting symmetric monoidal structure on
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
agrees with the one inherited from
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
as a symmetric monoidal localisation, in the sense of [@lur17
Proposition 2.2.1.9].*
:::

::: {#rem:tstrucuresonsheaveswithtransfers .rem}
**Remark 1**. *The stable $\infty$-categories defining the pullback
square in [1](#defi:sheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="defi:sheaveswithtransfers"} carry t-structures. For
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$ and
$\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
both connectivity and coconnectivity are detected levelwise, see e.g.
[@bcn25 Definition 2.5]. This makes the restriction
$$\hat{\gamma}^*: \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
into a t-exact functor. For
$\mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$
we already discussed the t-structure in
[1](#rem:tstructureonsheaves){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="rem:tstructureonsheaves"}, it makes the (hyper)sheafification
$$\textsf{\textup{a}}^{(\wedge)}_{\tau}: \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{(\wedge)}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
into a t-exact functor. There is an induced t-structure on
(hyper)sheaves with transfers for which coconnectivity is detected on
the level of presheaves with transfers, i.e. levelwise, and the
connective objects are determined by orthogonality. The hearts of these
t-structures are the abelian categories of (pre)sheaves (with transfers)
with values in abelian groups.*
:::

We will now compare the $\infty$-category of hypersheaves with transfers
of [1](#defi:sheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="defi:sheaveswithtransfers"} with the derived
$\infty$-category of the Grothendieck abelian category of $\tau$-sheaves
with transfers
$$\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R) \subseteq \textnormal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R).$$
Note that in [@cd19 §10] and [@cd16], Cisinski--Déglise use a
coefficient ring $\Lambda$ for the finite correspondences, which is a
localisation of $\mathbb{Z}$ at the invertible primes of $R$, or
equivalently the maximal subring $\Lambda \subseteq \mathbb{Q}$ such
that $R$ is a $\Lambda$-algebra. They then work with $\Lambda$-linear
(pre)sheaves
$\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\Lambda)^\textnormal{op}\to \textsf{\textup{Mod}}(R)$.
But by [@cd19 Remark 9.1.3] we have
$\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\Lambda) \cong \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z}) \otimes_{\mathbb{Z}} \Lambda$,
and there is an equivalence
$$\textnormal{P}_{\Lambda\textnormal{-lin}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\Lambda);R) \simeq \textnormal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R)$$
which descends to sheaves with transfers. We will work with the latter
category.

::: {#thm:derivedsheaveswithtransfers .thm}
**Theorem 1**. *There is an equivalence of symmetric monoidal
$\infty$-categories
$$\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R)) \simeq \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).$$*
:::

::: proof
*Proof.* By [@cd19 §10] we have a diagram of adjunctions between
Grothendieck abelian categories in which we use uppercase letters to
distinguish the functors from the ones on $\infty$-categorical
(pre)sheaves. $$\begin{equation}
\label{diag:sheaveswithtransfers}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=0.9cm,row sep=0.9cm]
    {\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R)} & {\textnormal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R)} \\
    {\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R)} & {\textnormal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R),}
    \arrow[""{name=0, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau}^{\textnormal{tr}}}"', shift right=2, hook, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[""{name=1, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\Gamma^*}", shift left=2, from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow[""{name=2, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\textsf{\textup{A}}_{\tau}^{\textnormal{tr}}}"', two heads, shift right=2, from=1-2, to=1-1]
    \arrow[""{name=3, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\hat{\Gamma}^*}", shift left=2, from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow[""{name=4, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\Gamma_!}", shift left=2, from=2-1, to=1-1]
    \arrow[""{name=5, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau}}"', shift right=2, hook, from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow[""{name=6, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\hat{\Gamma}_!}", shift left=2, from=2-2, to=1-2]
    \arrow[""{name=7, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\textsf{\textup{A}}_{\tau}}"', two heads, shift right=2, from=2-2, to=2-1]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center, rotate=-90}, draw=none, from=2, to=0]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center}, draw=none, from=4, to=1]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center, rotate=-90}, draw=none, from=7, to=5]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center}, draw=none, from=6, to=3]
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}$$ By definition of
$\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R)$ the
diagram of right adjoints is a pullback. According to [@cd19 Proposition
10.3.3, 10.3.9] the functors $\hat{\Gamma}^*$ and $\Gamma^*$ admit a
further right adjoint, in particular they are exact, and the two
sheafification functors $\textsf{\textup{A}}_{\tau}$ and
$\textsf{\textup{A}}^{\textnormal{tr}}_{\tau}$ are exact as well. We
hence obtain a diagram of adjunctions of derived $\infty$-categories
$$\begin{equation}
\label{diag:derivedsheaveswithtransfers}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=0.9cm,row sep=0.9cm]
    {\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R))} & {\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R))} \\
    {\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R))} & {\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R)).}
    \arrow[""{name=0, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\textbf{\textup{R}}\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau}^{\textnormal{tr}}}"', hook, shift right=2, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow[""{name=1, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\Gamma^*}", shift left=2, from=1-1, to=2-1]
    \arrow[""{name=2, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\textsf{\textup{A}}_{\tau}^{\textnormal{tr}}}"', two heads, shift right=2, from=1-2, to=1-1]
    \arrow[""{name=3, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\hat{\Gamma}^*}", shift left=2, from=1-2, to=2-2]
    \arrow[""{name=4, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\textbf{\textup{L}}\Gamma_!}", shift left=2, from=2-1, to=1-1]
    \arrow[""{name=5, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\textbf{\textup{R}}\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau}}"', hook, shift right=2, from=2-1, to=2-2]
    \arrow[""{name=6, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\textbf{\textup{L}}\hat{\Gamma}_!}", shift left=2, from=2-2, to=1-2]
    \arrow[""{name=7, anchor=center, inner sep=0}, "{\textsf{\textup{A}}_{\tau}}"', two heads, shift right=2, from=2-2, to=2-1]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center, rotate=-90}, draw=none, from=2, to=0]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center}, draw=none, from=4, to=1]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center, rotate=-90}, draw=none, from=7, to=5]
    \arrow["\dashv"{anchor=center}, draw=none, from=6, to=3]
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}$$ The right derived functors
$\textbf{\textup{R}}\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau}^{(\textnormal{tr})}$ are
fully faithful by the argument used in the proof of [@ser03 Corollary
4.1], which boils down to the fact that the derived right adjoints can
be computed using a K-injective resolution (which is a fibrant
replacement with respect to the injective model structure of [@lur17
Proposition 1.3.5.3]). According to [@lur09 Proposition 5.5.7.6] the
$\textsf{\textup{Pr}}^{\textnormal{R}}$-pullback of the cospan
$$\begin{equation}
\label{diag:derivedcospan}
\begin{tikzcd}
    {\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R))} & {\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R))} & {\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R))}
    \arrow["{\hspace*{-0.16cm}\textbf{\textup{R}}\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau}}", hook, from=1-1, to=1-2]
    \arrow["{\hat{\Gamma}^*}"', from=1-3, to=1-2]
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}$$ is computed in
$\widehat{\textsf{\textup{Cat}}}_{\infty}$, and hence it is equivalent
to the full subcategory
$$\mathcal{C}\subseteq \mathcal{D}(\textnormal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R))$$
on complexes of presheaves with transfers $F_\bullet$ such that
$\hat{\Gamma}^*(F_\bullet)$ lies in the essential image of the fully
faithful functor
$\textbf{\textup{R}}\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau}: \mathcal{D}(\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R)) \hookrightarrow \mathcal{D}(\textnormal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R))$.
The latter is equivalent to the unit map $$\begin{align}
\label{eq:unitpullback}
\hat{\Gamma}^*(F_\bullet) \to \textbf{\textup{R}}\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau}\textsf{\textup{A}}_{\tau}\hat{\Gamma}^*(F_\bullet)
\end{align}$$ being an equivalence. Since the sheafification functors
and the functors that forget transfers are exact before derivation,
[@cd19 Proposition 10.3.9] implies that the target of this map
identifies with
$\textbf{\textup{R}}\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau}\Gamma^*\textsf{\textup{A}}_{\tau}^{\textnormal{tr}}(F_\bullet) \simeq \hat{\Gamma}^*\textbf{\textup{R}}\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau}^{\textnormal{tr}}\textsf{\textup{A}}_{\tau}^{\textnormal{tr}}(F_\bullet)$.
The resulting map
$\hat{\Gamma}^*(F_\bullet) \to \hat{\Gamma}^*\textbf{\textup{R}}\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau}^{\textnormal{tr}}\textsf{\textup{A}}_{\tau}^{\textnormal{tr}}(F_\bullet)$
identifies with $\hat{\Gamma}^*$ applied to the unit map $$\begin{align}
\label{eq:unitpullbacki}
F_\bullet \to \textbf{\textup{R}}\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau}^{\textnormal{tr}}\textsf{\textup{A}}_{\tau}^{\textnormal{tr}}(F_\bullet).
\end{align}$$ Since $\hat{\Gamma}^*$ is faithful and exact on abelian
categories, it is conservative on derived categories. Hence
[\[eq:unitpullback\]](#eq:unitpullback){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:unitpullback"} is an equivalence if and only if
[\[eq:unitpullbacki\]](#eq:unitpullbacki){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:unitpullbacki"} is one. It follows that $\mathcal{C}$ is
equivalent to the essential image of
$$\textbf{\textup{R}}\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau}^{\textnormal{tr}}: \mathcal{D}(\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R)) \hookrightarrow \mathcal{D}(\textnormal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R)),$$
and the diagram of right adjoints in
[\[diag:derivedsheaveswithtransfers\]](#diag:derivedsheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref"
reference="diag:derivedsheaveswithtransfers"} is a pullback. So in order
to show that there is an equivalence
$$\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R)) \simeq \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}})$$
it suffices to identify the cospans of
[\[diag:pullbacksheaveswithtransfers\]](#diag:pullbacksheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref"
reference="diag:pullbacksheaveswithtransfers"} and
[\[diag:derivedcospan\]](#diag:derivedcospan){reference-type="ref"
reference="diag:derivedcospan"}, up to an refinement of the former as in
[1](#cons:refinedpullback){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cons:refinedpullback"}. Since the left adjoints will identify
as symmetric monoidal functors, this equivalence will be symmetric
monoidal. We first consider the nodes. By [@lur18 Corollary 2.1.2.3] the
inclusion of sheaves into $\infty$-hypersheaves
$\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R) \hookrightarrow \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R)$
induces a t-exact equivalence $$\begin{equation}
\label{eq:derivedsheaves}
\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R)) \simeq \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R).
\end{equation}$$ The same argument implies that for presheaves there is
a t-exact equivalence $$\begin{equation}
