\label{eq:derivedpresheaves}
\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R)) \simeq \mathcal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R).
\end{equation}$$ Formally extending scalars to $R$ in the additive
category $\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z})$ induces an equivalence
to $R$-linear presheaves
$$\textnormal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R) \simeq \textnormal{P}_{R\textnormal{-lin}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);R).$$
We can thus view $\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R)$ as a full subcategory of
$\textnormal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R)$ via
the $R$-linear Yoneda embedding
$$\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R) \hookrightarrow \textnormal{P}_{R\textnormal{-lin}}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);R),$$
and its elements form a system of compact projective generators in the
sense of [@pst23 Definition 2.51]. Then [@pst23 Lemma 2.61] provides a
t-exact equivalence $$\begin{equation}
\label{eq:derivedsheavespresheavestransfers}
\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R)) \simeq \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}).
\end{equation}$$ Let us consider the functors between the nodes. We have
equivalences $$\begin{align*}
&\textnormal{Fun}^{\textnormal{L,t-ex}}(\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R)),\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R))) \\
\simeq \; &\textnormal{Fun}^{\textnormal{L,lex}}(\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R))_{\geq 0},\mathcal{D}(\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R))_{\geq 0}) \\
\simeq \; &\textnormal{Fun}^{\textnormal{L,lex}}(\textnormal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R),\textnormal{Sh}_{\tau}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;\mathbb{Z});R)),
\end{align*}$$ where the first equivalence holds by [@lur18 Proposition
C.3.1.1, C.3.2.1] and the second one by [@lur18 Theorem C.5.4.9]. The
hypersheafification functor
$$\textsf{\textup{a}}^{\wedge}_{\tau}: \mathcal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R) \to \mathcal{S}\textnormal{h}_{\tau}^{\wedge}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R)$$
is t-exact, and its restriction to hearts is given by the functor
$\textsf{\textup{A}}_{\tau}$ of
[\[diag:sheaveswithtransfers\]](#diag:sheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref"
reference="diag:sheaveswithtransfers"}. From the above equivalence of
functor categories it follows that the adjunctions
$(\textsf{\textup{A}}_{\tau},\textbf{\textup{R}}\textsf{\textup{O}}_{\tau})$
and
$(\textsf{\textup{a}}^{\wedge}_{\tau},\textsf{\textup{o}}_{\tau}^{\wedge})$
identify under the equivalences
[\[eq:derivedsheaves\]](#eq:derivedsheaves){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:derivedsheaves"} and
[\[eq:derivedpresheaves\]](#eq:derivedpresheaves){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:derivedpresheaves"}. As in
[1](#cons:refinedpullback){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cons:refinedpullback"}, we now consider the restriction
functor
$$\hat{\delta}^*: \mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R);\textsf{\textup{Sp}}) \to \mathcal{P}(\textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S;R)$$
that precomposes with the functor
$$\delta: \textsf{\textup{Sm}}_S\times \textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R \stackrel{\gamma \times \sigma}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R) \times \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R) \stackrel{\times_S}{\longrightarrow} \textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R).$$
Here $\textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R$ is the category of finite rank free
$R$-modules, $\sigma$ is the canonical additive functor
$\textsf{\textup{Lat}}_R \to \textsf{\textup{FétCor}}(S;R)$ that sends
$R$ to $S$, and the bifunctor $(-)\times_S(-)$ comes from the monoidal
structure on $\textsf{\textup{SmCor}}(S;R)$. Left Kan extension followed
by localisation to $\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}$ provides a left adjoint to
$\hat{\delta}^*$, in particular the latter preserves finite colimits. It
also preserves filtered colimits since they are computed pointwise.
Hence $\hat{\delta}^*$ is a t-exact left adjoint, and the same argument
that we just used for $\textsf{\textup{a}}^{\wedge}_{\tau}$ now applies:
on hearts $\hat{\delta}^*$ is given by the functor $\hat{\Gamma}^*$ of
[\[diag:sheaveswithtransfers\]](#diag:sheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref"
reference="diag:sheaveswithtransfers"}. We can hence identify the
functors $\hat{\Gamma}^*$ and $\hat{\delta}^*$ under the equivalences
[\[eq:derivedpresheaves\]](#eq:derivedpresheaves){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:derivedpresheaves"} and
[\[eq:derivedsheavespresheavestransfers\]](#eq:derivedsheavespresheavestransfers){reference-type="ref"
reference="eq:derivedsheavespresheavestransfers"}. The observation that
the diagram
[\[diag:pullbacksheaveswithtransfers\]](#diag:pullbacksheaveswithtransfers){reference-type="ref"
reference="diag:pullbacksheaveswithtransfers"} refines to an iterated
pullback involving $\hat{\delta}^*$ as in
[1](#cons:refinedpullback){reference-type="ref+Label"
reference="cons:refinedpullback"} now finishes the proof. ◻
:::

::: rem
**Remark 1**. *The same proof goes through for
$\textsf{\textup{Fét}}_S$, in fact we could replace
$\textsf{\textup{Sm}}$ by any class $\mathscr{P}$ of separated finite
type morphisms between noetherian schemes of finite dimension which
contains étale separated morphisms of finite type [@cd19 10.0.1], and we
could replace the topology $\tau$ with any that is mildly compatible
with transfers, in the sense of [@cd19 Definition 10.3.5].*
:::

[^1]: Under the assumptions on $S$ we work with we could also use
    hypersheaves instead of sheaves.

[^2]: *That is, $\mathcal{D}$ is presentable and admits a symmetric
    monoidal structure for which the tensor product preserves colimits
    in both variables.*

[^3]: Since $\textsf{\textup{Span}}(G)$ is self-dual, we could omit the
    $(-)^\textnormal{op}$ in the functor category, which is the more
    commonly used convention for spectral Mackey functors. Later on we
    will consider many (pre)sheaf categories for sites that are not
    self-dual, and the above choice works better with the flow of these.

[^4]: *When $H$ is furthermore of finite index, then
    $\textnormal{res}^G_H$ also has a left adjoint, the *induction*
    $\textnormal{ind}^G_H$. This goes in line with the next remark,
    where $H$ being of finite index ensures that $(G \times X)/H$ is a
    finite $G$-set for a finite $H$-set $X$.*

[^5]: *In [@mnn17 Example 6.20] this is denoted $\underline{M}$, we do
    not use this notation to avoid confusion with constant Mackey
    functors. When $R_G = \mathbb{S}_G$ this $G$-spectrum represents
    Borel equivariant $M$-cohomology on $G$-spaces.*

[^6]: *The notation $\textsf{\textup{Glo}}$ is also used in the
    literature for the global category of compact Lie groups.*

[^7]: *In the sense of [@lur17 Example 2.2.1.3].*

[^8]: *Or more generally, that tensoring with $A_R$ preserves limits.*

[^9]: Connectedness is assumed for convenience, it is not of technical
    relevance and one can restrict to individual connected components
    when working with étale fundamental groups. The assumption on the
    Krull dimension is a technical one and will only be used for
    hypercompleteness of the Nisnevich topos. Assuming $S$ to be
    noetherian is also used for the latter (for this it would be enough
    that $S$ is qcqs), but it is also important for the theory of finite
    correspondences.

[^10]: *Here $\textnormal{lg}(\mathcal{O}_{X,x})$ is the geometric
    multiplicity of $x \in X$, i.e. the length of the local ring
    $\mathcal{O}_{X,x}$ (considered as a module over itself).*

[^11]: *Throughout, we understand a generic point as one of an
    irreducible component.*

[^12]: *That is, $\mathcal{C}$ is additive and admits a symmetric
    monoidal structure for which the tensor product is additive in both
    variables.*
